<!-- theorem-1: body_md -->
\label{thm:gaifman}
  Every \(\ensuremath{\@empty{FO}}\) formula \(\varphi\) is equivalent to a formula \(\varphi'\) in Gaifman normal form.
  Moreover, \(\operatorname*{free}(\varphi') = \operatorname*{free}(\varphi)\),
  and $\varphi'$ can be computed from $\varphi$.
<!-- end theorem-1 -->
<!-- example-1: body_md -->
\label{ex:fo-gaifman}
  Let \(\sigma \coloneqq \ensuremath{\{E, \@empty{orange}, \@empty{blue}\}}\)
  and consider the \(\ensuremath{\@empty{FO}}\) formula
  \[
    \varphi(x) \ \coloneqq \ \exists y\, \Big(\@empty{orange}(y)
    \land \neg\exists z\, \big(E(x,z) \land E(z,y)\big)
    \land \neg\exists z\, \big(\@empty{blue}(z) \land E(y,z)\big)\Big).
  \]
  This formula states that there exists an orange node \(y\)
  that is not reachable from \(x\) by a walk of length \(2\)
  and that does not have a blue neighbour.

  \begin{figure}
    \centering
    \begin{tikzpicture}[scale=0.9]
      \def4ex{4ex}
\def1.4*4ex{1.4*4ex}
\def0.6*4ex{0.6*4ex}
\node[vertex, fill=ibm-orange] (3) {\(3\)};
\foreach \x in {1,2} {
  \node[vertex] at ($(3) + {3-\x}*(-1.4*4ex, -0.6*4ex)$) (\x) {\(\x\)};
}
\foreach \x in {4,5} {
  \node[vertex] at ($(3) + {\x-3}*(-1.4*4ex, 0.6*4ex)$) (\x) {\(\x\)};
}
\foreach \x in {6} {
  \node[vertex] at ($(5) + {(\x-5)}*(1.4*4ex, 0.6*4ex)$) (\x) {\(\x\)};
}
\node[vertex, fill=ibm-orange] at ($(6) + (1.4*4ex, 0ex)$) (7) {\(7\)};
\foreach \x in {8} {
  \node[vertex] at ($(7) + (1.4*4ex, 0ex)$) (\x) {\(\x\)};
}
\foreach \x in {10,11} {
  \node[vertex] at ($(5) + {\x-9}*(-1.4*4ex, -0.6*4ex)$) (\x) {\(\x\)};
}
\node[vertex] at ($(1) + (-1.4*4ex, 0.6*4ex)$) (9) {\(9\)};
\foreach \x in {12,...,15} {
   \node[vertex] at ($(11) + {1.5*(\x-11)}*(-4ex, 0ex)$) (\x) {\(\x\)};
}
\node[vertex, fill=ibm-orange] at ($(15) + 1.5*(-4ex, 0ex)$) (16) {\(16\)};
\node[vertex, fill=ibm-ultramarine] at ($(16) + 1.5*(-4ex, 0ex)$) (17) {\(17\)};

\begin{scope}
  \clip ($(17) + (0, -2*4ex)$) rectangle ($(14) + (0, 2.5*4ex)$);
  \node[draw, circle, line width=.4ex, ibm-magenta!30, minimum width={14.65*4ex}] at (11) {};
  \node[draw, circle, line width=.4ex, ibm-magenta, minimum width={14.8*4ex}] at (11) {};
\end{scope}
\node[ibm-magenta] at ($(14) + (-.7*4ex,2*4ex)$) {\(\ensuremath{N_{7}^{\ensuremath{\mathcal{G}}}(v)} = \ensuremath{N_{7}^{\ensuremath{\mathcal{G}}}(v')}\)};

\draw[edge] (1) -- (2) -- (3) -- (4) -- (5) -- (6) -- (7) -- (8)
  (1) -- (9) -- (11) -- (12) -- (13) -- (14) -- (15) -- (16) -- (17)
  (5) -- (10) -- (11);

\node at ($(1) + (-2.25ex, -2.5ex)$) (v) {\(v\)};
\node at ($(5) + (-2.ex, 3ex)$) (vs) {\(v'\)};
     \end{tikzpicture}
    \caption{Structure \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{G}}=\ensuremath{\mathcal{G}}_7\) of \cref{ex:fo-gaifman}.
      Undirected edges represent directed edges in both directions,
      \(\@empty{orange}^{\ensuremath{\mathcal{G}}} = \ensuremath{\{3,7,16\}}\),
      and \(\@empty{blue}^{\ensuremath{\mathcal{G}}}=\ensuremath{\{17\}}\).
    }
    \label{fig:fo-gaifman}
  \end{figure}

  Let \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{G}}\) be the \(\sigma\)-structure depicted in \cref{fig:fo-gaifman},
  and let \(v \coloneqq 1\) and \(v' \coloneqq 5\)
  (as indicated in the figure).
  Clearly, \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{G}} \models \varphi(v)\) and \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{G}} \not \models \varphi(v')\).
  Observe that \(\ensuremath{N_{7}^{\ensuremath{\mathcal{G}}}(v)} = \ensuremath{N_{7}^{\ensuremath{\mathcal{G}}}(v')}\),
  and let \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{N}}_7 \coloneqq \ensuremath{\mathcal{N}_{7}^{\ensuremath{\mathcal{G}}}(v)} = \ensuremath{\mathcal{N}_{7}^{\ensuremath{\mathcal{G}}}(v')}\).
  In \cref{fig:fo-gaifman}, \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{N}}_7\) is the substructure of \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{G}}\)
  induced on the pink part.

  Now consider an arbitrary \(r \geqslant 7\),
  and let \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{G}}_r\) be the \(\sigma\)-structure that looks exactly like \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{G}}\),
  with the only difference that the path from vertex \(11\) to the leftmost orange vertex
  (vertex \(16\) for \(r=7\))
  has length \(r{-}2\)
  (hence, the distance between the leftmost orange vertex and \(v\) as well as \(v'\) is \(r\)).
  The structure \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{G}}\) depicted in \cref{fig:fo-gaifman} is \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{G}}_7\).
  Clearly,
  \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{G}}_r \models \varphi(v)\) and \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{G}}_r \not \models \varphi(v')\).
  Furthermore, \(\ensuremath{N_{r}^{\ensuremath{\mathcal{G}}_r}(v)} = \ensuremath{N_{r}^{\ensuremath{\mathcal{G}}_r}(v')}\).
  Let \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{N}}_r \coloneqq \ensuremath{\mathcal{N}_{r}^{\ensuremath{\mathcal{G}}_r}(v)} = \ensuremath{\mathcal{N}_{r}^{\ensuremath{\mathcal{G}}_r}(v')}\).
  We use Ehrenfeucht--Fra\"{\i}ss{\'e}\ games (cf., \cite{Libkin-FMT,EF-FMT}).
  An exhaustive exploration proves the following claim,
  which, combined with the Ehrenfeucht--Fra\"{\i}ss{\'e}\ Theorem
  (cf., \cite{Libkin-FMT,EF-FMT}),
  yields that \(\varphi(x)\) is not equivalent to a Boolean combination \(\varphi'(x)\)
  of local formulas of quantifier rank \(\leqslant 2\)
  and of basic local sentences (see \cref{appendix:fo-gaifman} for details).
\begin{restatable}{claim}{GaifmanCounterExample}
    \label{claim:GaifmanCounterExample}
    For every \(r \in \mathbb{N}\) with \(r \geqslant 7\),
    Duplicator has a winning strategy for the 2-round
    Ehrenfeucht--Fra\"{\i}ss{\'e}\ game on \((\ensuremath{\mathcal{N}}_r, v)\) and \((\ensuremath{\mathcal{N}}_r, v')\).
  \end{restatable}
<!-- end example-1 -->
<!-- claim-1: body_md -->
\label{claim:GaifmanCounterExample}
    For every \(r \in \mathbb{N}\) with \(r \geqslant 7\),
    Duplicator has a winning strategy for the 2-round
    Ehrenfeucht--Fra\"{\i}ss{\'e}\ game on \((\ensuremath{\mathcal{N}}_r, v)\) and \((\ensuremath{\mathcal{N}}_r, v')\).
<!-- end claim-1 -->
<!-- example-2: body_md -->
\label{example:noGNFforFOW}
Let $\sigma=\emptyset$, let $\mathbb{S}=\ensuremath{\{S\}}$ for an \emph{infinite}
abelian group $S$, and let $\ensuremath{\mathbf{W}}=\ensuremath{\{\ensuremath{\mathtt{w}}\}}$
for a unary weight symbol \(\ensuremath{\mathtt{w}}\) with range \(S\).
Let \(\ensuremath{\mathbb{P}}\)
contain the \emph{equality predicate for $S$}, denoted
by $\ensuremath{\@empty{P}}_{=}$ with semantics $\left\llbracket \ensuremath{\@empty{P}}_= \right\rrbracket=\ensuremath{\ensuremath{\{(s,s) : s\in S\}}}$.
Then,
\(
  \varphi(x)  \coloneqq
  \exists y\,\bigl(\neg\, x{=}y \ \land \
  \ensuremath{\@empty{P}}_{=}\bigl(\ensuremath{\mathtt{w}}(x), \ensuremath{\mathtt{w}}(y)\bigr)\bigr)
\)
is an $\ensuremath{\ensuremath{\@empty{FOW}}}$ formula that expresses
in
a $(\sigma,\ensuremath{\mathbf{W}})$-structure
$\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}$ that $x$ is assigned with an
$a\in A$ for which there exists
a $b\in A$ that is
different from $a$ but has the same weight as $a$.
It is not too difficult to prove that $\varphi(x)$ is not
equivalent to any Boolean combination of
sentences and local formulas of $\ensuremath{\ensuremath{\ensuremath{\@empty{FOW}}}^+}$ (see \cref{appendix:fow}).
<!-- end example-2 -->
<!-- example-3: body_md -->
Let \(\mathbb{S} \) contain the abelian group $(\mathbb{Z},+)$,
  let $\ensuremath{\mathbf{W}}$ contain a unary weight symbol $\ensuremath{\mathtt{w}}$
  with range $\mathbb{Z}$,
  and let \(\ensuremath{\mathbb{P}}\) contain a predicate $\ensuremath{\@empty{P}}_<$ with
  \(\left\llbracket \ensuremath{\@empty{P}}_< \right\rrbracket \coloneqq \ensuremath{\ensuremath{\{(i,j) \in \mathbb{Z}^2 : i < j\}}}\).
Let \(\sigma \coloneqq \ensuremath{\{E\}}\) be the signature of directed graphs.
  The \(\ensuremath{\ensuremath{\ensuremath{\@empty{ngFOW}}}^+}\) sentence
  \(
  \varphi
  \coloneqq
  \forall x \forall y\,
  \Bigl(
    \neg E(x,y) \lor
    \big(\,
      \ensuremath{\@empty{P}}_<(\ensuremath{\mathtt{w}}(x), \ensuremath{\mathtt{w}}(y))
      \und
      \operatorname{dist}(x,y)\,{\leqslant}\, 1
    \,\big)
    \Bigr)\)
  expresses in a vertex-weighted digraph that all edges are directed towards the
  vertex with the larger weight.
<!-- end example-3 -->
<!-- example-4: body_md -->
\label{ex:FOplus}
The logic $\ensuremath{\@empty{FO}}^+$ of
\cite[Section~7.2]{GroheKreutzerSiebertz_2017_NowhereDense} extends
$\ensuremath{\@empty{FO}}$ by the use of distance atoms.
This coincides with $\ensuremath{\ensuremath{\ensuremath{\@empty{ngFOW}}}^+}$
(and with $\ensuremath{\ensuremath{\ensuremath{\@empty{FOW}}}^+}$)
for the special case where
\(\mathbb{S} = \ensuremath{\mathbf{W}} = \ensuremath{\mathbb{P}} = \emptyset\), because then,
no weight symbols or predicates can be used.
<!-- end example-4 -->
<!-- example-5: body_md -->
\label{ex:FOMODplus}
For a finite set $M$ of integers $m\geqslant 2$, let $\ensuremath{\@empty{FO{+}MOD}}_M$ denote the
extension of
$\ensuremath{\@empty{FO}}$
with modulo-counting quantifiers
$\exists^{i \text{\,mod\,}m}x$ for $m\in M$ and $i\in[0,m{-}1]$.
A formula  $\exists^{i \text{\,mod\,}m}x\,\varphi$ expresses
in a $\sigma$-structure $\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}$ that the number of elements $a\in A$ that
can be assigned to $x$ such that $\varphi$ is satisfied is congruent to $i$
modulo $m$.  Extending this logic by the use of distance atoms, one
obtains the logic $\ensuremath{\ensuremath{\@empty{FO{+}MOD}}^{+}_{M}}$. This logic is embedded in
$\ensuremath{\ensuremath{\ensuremath{\@empty{ngFOW}}}^+}$ for the special case where $\ensuremath{\mathbb{P}}=\emptyset$, $\mathbb{S}$
consists of the abelian groups $\mathbb{Z}/m\mathbb{Z}$ with addition modulo
$m$, for all $m\in M$, and $\ensuremath{\mathbf{W}}=\ensuremath{\ensuremath{\{\ensuremath{\mathtt{w}}_m : m\in M\}}}$ with
\(\ensuremath{\mathtt{w}}_m\) being a unary weight symbol with range \(\mathbb{Z}/m\mathbb{Z}\).
For this, we can turn any $\sigma$-structure $\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}$ into the
$(\sigma,\ensuremath{\mathbf{W}})$-structure $\hat{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}$ by letting $\ensuremath{\mathtt{w}}_m(a)=1\in
\mathbb{Z}/m\mathbb{Z}$ for all $m\in M$ and $a\in A$. Then, the formula $\exists^{i
  \text{\,mod\,}m}x\,\varphi$ corresponds to the formula
$\big(i=\sum\ensuremath{\mathtt{w}}_m(x).\varphi\big)$. In fact, since $\ensuremath{\mathbb{P}}=\emptyset$,
no predicates can be used, and there is a
1-to-1 correspondence that maps formulas $\psi(\bar x)\in\ensuremath{\ensuremath{\@empty{FO{+}MOD}}^{+}_{M}}$ to
formulas $\psi'(\bar x)\in\ensuremath{\ensuremath{\ensuremath{\@empty{ngFOW}}}^+}$ (for this choice of $\mathbb{S},\ensuremath{\mathbf{W}},\ensuremath{\mathbb{P}}$)
such that, for every $\sigma$-structure $\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}$
and every assignment $\bar a\in A^{|\bar x|}$,
we have $\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}\models\psi(\bar a)\iff\hat{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}\models\psi'(\bar a)$.
<!-- end example-5 -->
<!-- theorem-2: body_md -->
\label{thm:GNF_ngFOWplus}
Let $q,k,d\in\mathbb{N}$. Every $\varphi\in\ensuremath{\ensuremath{\@empty{F}}(q,k,d)}$ is equivalent to a
formula $\varphi'$ in Gaifman normal form for $\ensuremath{\ensuremath{\@empty{F}}(q,k,d)}$.
Furthermore, $\operatorname*{free}(\varphi')=\operatorname*{free}(\varphi)$,
the
outer (inner) quantifier rank of $\varphi'$
is at most $\operatorname*{qr}(\varphi)$ (resp.\ $\operatorname*{qr}(\varphi){-}1$),
the outer (inner) radius of $\varphi'$
is at most $\operatorname{r} ({q}, {k}, {d})$
(resp.\ $\operatorname{r} ({q{-}1}, {k{+}\wmaxar}, {d})$),
and the width (inner distance)
is at most $k{+}1{+}(q{-}1)\wmaxar$
(resp.\ $\operatorname{r} ({q}, {k}, {d})+2\operatorname{r} ({q{-}1}, {k{+}\wmaxar}, {d})$).
Moreover,
such a $\varphi'$ can be computed from $\varphi,q,k,d$.
<!-- end theorem-2 -->
<!-- lemma-1: body_md -->
\label{lemma:LocalityLemma}
  For all \(q, k, d \in \mathbb{N}\),
  it holds that \(\operatorname{L} ({q}, {k}, {d}) \subseteq \ensuremath{\ensuremath{\@empty{F}}(q,k,d)}\),
  and every formula \(\varphi \in \operatorname{L} ({q}, {k}, {d})\) is \(\operatorname{r} ({q}, {k}, {d})\)-local.
<!-- end lemma-1 -->
<!-- theorem-3: body_md -->
\label{thm:GNF_ngFOWplus_refined}
Let $q,k,d\in\mathbb{N}$. Every $\varphi\in \ensuremath{\ensuremath{\@empty{F}}(q,k,d)}$
is equivalent to a
formula $\varphi'$
that is a Boolean combination of formulas in
$\operatorname{S} ({q}, {k}, {d})\cup\operatorname{L} ({q}, {k}, {d})$. Furthermore, $\operatorname*{free}(\varphi')=\operatorname*{free}(\varphi)$,
the
outer (inner) quantifier rank of $\varphi'$ is at most $\operatorname*{qr}(\varphi)$
(resp.\ $\operatorname*{qr}(\varphi){-}1$),
and there is an algorithm that computes such a $\varphi'$ from $\varphi,q,k,d$.
<!-- end theorem-3 -->
<!-- lemma-2: body_md -->
\label{lemma:fv}\label{lem:fv_decomp}
  Let \(q,\myk,d \in \mathbb{N}\) with \(\myk \geqslant 2\).
Let \(\vec{x} \coloneqq (x_1,\ldots,x_{\myk})\),
\(r' \coloneqq \operatorname{r} ({q}, {\myk}, {d})\),
\(K \subseteq \brac{\myk}\),
and
  \(I \subseteq K\) with \(\emptyset \neq I \neq K\).
For every \(\lambda \paren{{\myvec{x}}_{K}} \in \operatorname{L} ({q}, {\myk}, {d})\),
  there exists a finite and non-empty set \(\Delta\) of pairs of formulas
  \(\big(\alpha\paren{{\myvec{x}}_{I}},\beta\paren{{\myvec{x}}_{K \setminus I}}\big)\)
  such that \(\alpha\paren{{\myvec{x}}_{I}}\) and
  \(\beta\paren{{\myvec{x}}_{K \setminus I}}\) belong to \(\operatorname{L} ({q}, {\myk}, {d})\),
  their
  quantifier rank is at most $\operatorname*{qr}(\lambda)$,
  and
  \begin{equation}\label{eq:lem:fv_decomp}
    \lambda(\bar{x}_K)
    \; \und\;
    \operatorname{dist}(\bar{x}_I;\bar{x}_{K \setminus I}) \,{>}\, r'
    \ \ \equiv \ \Oder_{(\alpha,\beta) \in \Delta}
   \paren[\Big]{
      \alpha \paren{{\myvec{x}}_{I}}
      \und
      \beta \paren{{\myvec{x}}_{K \setminus I}}
    }
     \mathbin{\mathchoice{\ }{}{}{} \land \mathchoice{\ }{}{}{}}
    \operatorname{dist}({\myvec{x}}_{I};{\myvec{x}}_{K \setminus I}) \,{>}\, r'
    .
  \end{equation}
  Moreover, we can ensure that the first entries in $\Delta$ are \emph{mutually exclusive},
  i.e., for every two distinct $(\alpha,\beta)$ and $(\alpha',\beta')$
  in $\Delta$, the formula $(\alpha\und\alpha')$ is unsatisfiable.
  Furthermore, there is an algorithm that computes \(\Delta\)
  on input \(q, \myk, d, K, I, \lambda\).
<!-- end lemma-2 -->
<!-- proof-1: body_md -->
We proceed by induction on \(q\).
Here, we focus on the main issues;
a detailed proof is provided in \cref{appendix:DetailedGNFproof}.
The induction base with \(q = 0\) is trivial.
For the induction step from \(q\) to \(q{+}1\),
consider \(q, k, d \in \mathbb{N}\)
and a formula \(\varphi \in \ensuremath{\ensuremath{\@empty{F}}(q{+}1,k,d)}\);
in particular, \(\operatorname*{free}(\varphi) \subseteq \ensuremath{\{x_1, \dots, x_k\}}\).
The two crucial cases are those where
\(\varphi\) is of the form \(\exists x_{k+1}\,\varphi_1\)
and where
\(\varphi\) is of the form \(\bigl(s = \sum\ensuremath{\mathtt{w}}(\bar{y}).\varphi_1\bigr)\),
for some \(\varphi_1 \in \ensuremath{\ensuremath{\@empty{F}}(q,k',d)}\) with \(k' \coloneqq k{+}\wmaxar\).
The latter case can be handled similarly as the analogous case in the
proof of~\mbox{\cite[Theorem~4.6]{vanBergeremSchweikardt_2021_FOWA}},
but we rely on \cref{lemma:fv} instead of the Feferman--Vaught decompositions
of~\cite{vanBergeremSchweikardt_2021_FOWA}.
The case \(\varphi = \exists x_{k+1}\,\varphi_1\) is the most intricate case;
in order to avoid an increase of the (outer/inner) quantifier rank,
we cannot proceed similarly as previous proofs of
Gaifman normal form theorems in
\cite{GaifmanPaper,EF-FMT,KuskeSchweikardt_ICALP18,vanBergeremSchweikardt_2021_FOWA},
but we have to come up with a different construction.
For the remainder of this proof, we focus on this case.

By applying the induction hypothesis to \(\varphi_1\)
and  performing standard transformations,
we can reduce to the following task:
for a \(\lambda \in \operatorname{L} ({q}, {k'}, {d})\) with
\(\operatorname*{free}(\lambda) \subseteq \operatorname*{free}(\varphi) \cup \ensuremath{\{x_{k+1}\}}\)
and \(\operatorname*{qr}(\lambda) \leqslant \operatorname*{qr}(\varphi){-}1\),
transfer \(\exists x_{k+1}\,\lambda\) into an equivalent
Boolean combination of formulas in
\(\operatorname{S} ({q{+}1}, {k}, {d}) \cup \operatorname{L} ({q{+}1}, {k}, {d})\)
with outer (inner) quantifier rank
\(\leqslant \operatorname*{qr}(\varphi)\) (resp.~\(\operatorname*{qr}(\varphi)-1\)).

If \(\operatorname*{free}(\varphi) = \emptyset\),
then \(\exists x_{k+1}\,\lambda\) is a sentence in \(\operatorname{S} ({q{+}1}, {k}, {d})\),
and we are done.
Thus, consider the case that \(\operatorname*{free}(\varphi) \neq \emptyset\),
and hence, \(k \geqslant 1\).
Let \(\bar{x} \coloneqq (x_1, \dots, x_{k'})\),
\(I \coloneqq \ensuremath{\ensuremath{\{i \in [k] : x_i \in \operatorname*{free}(\varphi)\}}}\), and
\(r' \coloneqq \operatorname{r} ({q}, {k'}, {d})\).
Clearly, \(\exists x_{k+1}\,\lambda\) is equivalent to
\(\psi \lor \ensuremath{\bigvee}_{i \in I} \vartheta_i\)
with
\(\vartheta_i \coloneqq \exists x_{k+1}\bigl(\operatorname{dist}(x_i,x_{k+1}) \,{\leqslant}\, r'
\mathbin{\mathchoice{\ }{}{}{} \land \mathchoice{\ }{}{}{}} \lambda\bigr)\) for all \(i\in I\),
and
\(\psi \coloneqq \exists x_{k+1}\bigl(\operatorname{dist}(\bar{x}_I; x_{k+1}) \,{>}\, r'
\mathbin{\mathchoice{\ }{}{}{} \land \mathchoice{\ }{}{}{}} \lambda\bigr)\).
Since the \(\vartheta_i\) belong to \(\operatorname{L} ({q{+}1}, {k}, {d})\),
all that remains to be done is to transfer \(\psi\) into an
equivalent Boolean combination of formulas in
\(\operatorname{S} ({q{+}1}, {k}, {d}) \cup \operatorname{L} ({q{+}1}, {k}, {d})\)
with outer (inner) quantifier rank
\(\leqslant \operatorname*{qr}(\varphi)\) (resp.~\(\operatorname*{qr}(\varphi)-1\)).
Applying \cref{lemma:fv} with \(K \coloneqq I \cup \ensuremath{\{k{+}1\}}\) to \(\lambda\)
yields a finite, non-empty set \(\Delta\),
which we can utilize to reduce to the following task:
for a formula
\(\beta \in \operatorname{L} ({q}, {k'}, {d})\) with \(\operatorname*{free}(\beta) = \ensuremath{\{x_{k+1}\}}\)
and \(\operatorname*{qr}(\beta) \leqslant \operatorname*{qr}(\varphi){-}1\),
transform the formula
\[
  \mu(\bar{x}_I)
  ~~ \coloneqq ~~
  \exists x_{k+1} \bigl(
    \operatorname{dist}(\bar{x}_I; x_{k+1}) \,{>}\, r' \mathbin{\mathchoice{\ }{}{}{} \land \mathchoice{\ }{}{}{}} \beta(x_{k+1})
  \big)
\]
into an equivalent formula \(\mu'(\bar{x}_I)\)
that is a Boolean combination of formulas in
\(\operatorname{S} ({q{+}1}, {k}, {d}) \cup \operatorname{L} ({q{+}1}, {k}, {d})\)
with outer (inner) quantifier rank at most
\(\operatorname*{qr}(\varphi)\) (resp.~\(\operatorname*{qr}(\varphi)-1\)).
Let \(\tilde{r} \coloneqq \operatorname{r} ({q{+}1}, {k}, {d})\)
and \(\hat{r} \coloneqq \tilde{r} - r'\).
For constructing \(\mu'(\bar{x}_I)\),
we use the following formulas:
\begin{itemize}
  \item
    \(\displaystyle
    \delta_i(\bar{x}_I) \coloneqq \exists x_{k+1} \big(
      \operatorname{dist}(x_i,x_{k+1}) \,{\leqslant}\, \hat{r}
      \mathbin{\mathchoice{\ }{}{}{} \land \mathchoice{\ }{}{}{}}
      \operatorname{dist}(\bar{x}_I; x_{k+1}) \,{>}\, r'
      \mathbin{\mathchoice{\ }{}{}{} \land \mathchoice{\ }{}{}{}}
      \beta(x_{k+1})
    \big)\)
    for all \(i \in I\),
  \item
    \(\displaystyle
    \psi_\ell^{(c)}(\bar{x}_I) \coloneqq
    \ensuremath{\bigvee}_{\substack{J \subseteq I,\\ \left\lvertJ\right\rvert = \ell}}
    \paren[\Big]{
      \ensuremath{\bigwedge}_{\substack{j,j' \in J,\\ j \neq j'}}
      \!\!\!\operatorname{dist}(x_j,x_{j'}) \,{>}\, 4cr'
      \mathbin{\mathchoice{\ }{}{}{} \land \mathchoice{\ }{}{}{}}
      \ensuremath{\bigwedge}_{j \in J} \exists x_{k+1} \,
      \paren[\big]{
        \operatorname{dist}(x_j,x_{k+1}) \,{\leqslant}\, r' \mathbin{\mathchoice{\ }{}{}{} \land \mathchoice{\ }{}{}{}} \beta(x_{k+1})
      }
    }\)
    for all \(\ell, c \in [\left\lvertI\right\rvert]\),
    and
  \item
    \(\displaystyle
    \xi_\ell^{(c)} \coloneqq \exists y_1 \cdots \exists y_\ell \Big(\!
      \ensuremath{\bigwedge}_{1 \leqslant j < j' \leqslant \ell}\!\!\!
      \operatorname{dist}(y_j, y_{j'}) \,{>}\, 2(2c{+}1)r'
      \land
      \ensuremath{\bigwedge}_{j \in [\ell]} \!\beta(y_j)
    \Big)\)
    for all \(\ell \in [\left\lvertI\right\rvert{+}1]\) and \(c \in [0,\left\lvertI\right\rvert]\).
\end{itemize}

\noindent
Note that
\(\delta_i(\bar{x}_I) \in \operatorname{L} ({q{+}1}, {k}, {d})\) for all \(i \in I\),
that \(\psi_\ell^{(c)}(\bar{x}_I) \in \operatorname{L} ({q{+}1}, {k}, {d})\)
for all \(\ell, c \in [\left\lvertI\right\rvert]\),
and that \(\xi_\ell^{(c)} \in \operatorname{S} ({q{+}1}, {k}, {d})\)
for all \(\ell \in [\left\lvertI\right\rvert{+}1]\) and \(c \in [0,\left\lvertI\right\rvert]\).
We let
\[
  \mu'(\bar{x}_I)
  \quad \coloneqq \quad
  \xi^{(0)}_{\left\lvertI\right\rvert+1} \ \lor \ \; \ensuremath{\bigvee}_{c,\ell \in [\left\lvertI\right\rvert]} \ \Big(
    \xi^{(c)}_\ell \mathbin{\mathchoice{\ }{}{}{} \land \mathchoice{\ }{}{}{}} \neg\,\xi^{(c{-}1)}_{\ell+1}
    \mathbin{\mathchoice{\ }{}{}{} \land \mathchoice{\ }{}{}{}}
    \big(\ensuremath{\bigvee}_{i \in I} \delta_i(\bar{x}_I)
    \mathbin{\mathchoice{\ }{}{}{} \lor \mathchoice{\ }{}{}{}} \neg\,\psi^{(c)}_\ell(\bar{x}_I)\big)
  \Big).
\]
Clearly, \(\mu'\) is a Boolean combination of formulas in
\(\operatorname{S} ({q{+}1}, {k}, {d}) \cup \operatorname{L} ({q{+}1}, {k}, {d})\)
with
\(\operatorname*{free}(\mu') = \ensuremath{\ensuremath{\{x_i : i \in I\}}} = \operatorname*{free}(\varphi)\),
and \(\mu'\) has outer (inner) quantifier rank
\(\leqslant \operatorname*{qr}(\varphi)\) (resp.~\(\operatorname*{qr}(\varphi)-1\)).
Next, we prove that \(\mu'(\bar{x}_I)\) is equivalent to \(\mu(\bar{x}_I)\).
Consider a \((\sigma,\ensuremath{\mathbf{W}})\)-structure \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}\) and a tuple \(\bar{a} \in A^k\).
We have to show that
\(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \models \mu' (\bar{a}_I) \iff \ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \models \mu (\bar{a}_I)\).
We will use the following observation,
which is an immediate consequence of a double use of the triangle inequality.
\begin{restatable}{claim}{distanceByDoubleTriangle}
  \label{claim:distance_by_double_triangle}
Let \(s, t \in \mathbb{N}\),
  and let \(c_1, c_2, d_1, d_2 \in A\)
  such that
  \(\distS{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}{d_1, d_2} \,{>}\, s{+}2t\)
  and
  \(\distS{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}{c_j, d_j} \,{\leqslant}\, t\) for all \(j \in \ensuremath{\{1,2\}}\).
  Then we have \(\distS{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}{c_1, c_2} \,{>}\, s\).
\end{restatable}

We now focus on proving that
\(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \models \mu' (\bar{a}_I) \iff \ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \models \mu (\bar{a}_I)\).
Throughout the remainder of this proof,
we let \(B \coloneqq \ensuremath{\ensuremath{\{b \in A : \ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \models \beta (b)\}}}\).

   \abovedisplayskip 10\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus5\p@
   \abovedisplayshortskip \z@ \@plus3\p@

            \parsep\z@ \@plus\p@
            \topsep 8\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus4\p@
            \itemsep \parsep\leftmargin\leftmargini
            \parsep\z@ \@plus\p@
            \topsep 8\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus4\p@
            \itemsep \parsep\@xpt{13} 
   \abovedisplayskip 10\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus5\p@
   \abovedisplayshortskip \z@ \@plus3\p@
   \belowdisplayshortskip 6\p@ \@plus3\p@ \@minus3\p@
   \belowdisplayskip \abovedisplayskip
   \let\leftmargin\leftmargini
            \parsep\z@ \@plus\p@
            \topsep 8\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus4\p@
            \itemsep \parsep\leftmargin\leftmargini
            \parsep\z@ \@plus\p@
            \topsep 8\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus4\p@
            \itemsep \parsep\@xpt{13} 
   \abovedisplayskip 10\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus5\p@
   \abovedisplayshortskip \z@ \@plus3\p@
   \belowdisplayshortskip 6\p@ \@plus3\p@ \@minus3\p@
   \belowdisplayskip \abovedisplayskip
   \let\leftmargin\leftmargini
            \parsep\z@ \@plus\p@
            \topsep 8\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus4\p@
            \itemsep \parsep\leftmargin\leftmargini
            \parsep\z@ \@plus\p@
            \topsep 8\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus4\p@
            \itemsep \parsep\@xpt{13} 
   \abovedisplayskip 10\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus5\p@
   \abovedisplayshortskip \z@ \@plus3\p@
   \belowdisplayshortskip 6\p@ \@plus3\p@ \@minus3\p@
   \belowdisplayskip \abovedisplayskip
   \let\leftmargin\leftmargini
            \parsep\z@ \@plus\p@
            \topsep 8\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus4\p@
            \itemsep \parsep\leftmargin\leftmargini
            \parsep\z@ \@plus\p@
            \topsep 8\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus4\p@
            \itemsep \parsep\bfseries}{Case 1:}
\(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \models \xi^{(0)}_{\left\lvertI\right\rvert+1}\).
Then, \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \models \mu' (\bar{a}_I)\).
We have to show that \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \models \mu (\bar{a}_I)\).

Since \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \models \xi^{(0)}_{\left\lvertI\right\rvert+1}\),
there exist \( b_1, \dots, b_{\left\lvertI\right\rvert+1}  \in B\) of pairwise distance \(> 2r'\).
Let \(\pi\) be a mapping that maps every
\(b \in \ensuremath{\{ b_1, \dots, b_{\left\lvertI\right\rvert+1} \}}\)
to an element \(a \in \ensuremath{\ensuremath{\{a_i : i \in I\}}}\) that is closest to \(b\),
that is, an element that minimises \(\distS{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}{b,a}\),
where ties are broken arbitrarily.
Then, by the pigeonhole principle,
there are two distinct \(j, j' \in \brac{\left\lvertI\right\rvert{+}1}\)
with \(\pi(b_j) = \pi(b_{j'}) = a_i\) for some \(i \in I\).
Since \(\distS{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}{b_j, b_{j'}} > 2r'\),
by the triangle inequality,
we know that \(\distS{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}{a_i, b_j} > r'\)
or \(\distS{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}{a_i, b_{j'}} > r'\).
Say, w.l.o.g., \(\distS{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}{a_i, b_j} > r'\).
By the choice of \(\pi\),
we obtain that \(\distS{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}{\bar{a}_I; b_j} > r'\).
This \(b_j \in B\) witnesses that \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \models \mu (\bar{a})\).

   \belowdisplayskip \abovedisplayskip
   \let\leftmargin\leftmargini
            \parsep\z@ \@plus\p@

   \abovedisplayskip 10\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus5\p@
   \abovedisplayshortskip \z@ \@plus3\p@
   \belowdisplayshortskip 6\p@ \@plus3\p@ \@minus3\p@
   \belowdisplayskip \abovedisplayskip
   \let\leftmargin\leftmargini
            \parsep\z@ \@plus\p@
            \topsep 8\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus4\p@
            \itemsep \parsep\leftmargin\leftmargini
            \parsep\z@ \@plus\p@
            \topsep 8\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus4\p@
            \itemsep \parsep\bfseries}{Case 2:}
\(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \not\models \xi^{(0)}_1\).
Then, there does not exist any \(b \in A\) such that
\(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \models \beta (b)\), so \(B = \emptyset\).
Hence, \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \not\models \mu (\bar{a}_I)\).
Furthermore, \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \not\models \xi^{(0)}_{\left\lvertI\right\rvert+1}\) and, moreover,
for all \(c, \ell \in \brac{\left\lvertI\right\rvert}\),
we have that \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \not\models \xi^{(c)}_\ell\).
Thus, \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \not\models \mu' (\bar{a}_I)\),
so we have that \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \not\models \mu' (\bar{a}_I)\)
and \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \not\models \mu (\bar{a}_I)\).

   \abovedisplayskip 10\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus5\p@

   \belowdisplayskip \abovedisplayskip
   \let\leftmargin\leftmargini
            \parsep\z@ \@plus\p@
            \topsep 8\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus4\p@

            \topsep 8\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus4\p@

   \belowdisplayshortskip 6\p@ \@plus3\p@ \@minus3\p@
   \belowdisplayskip \abovedisplayskip
   \let\leftmargin\leftmargini
            \parsep\z@ \@plus\p@
            \topsep 8\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus4\p@

            \parsep\z@ \@plus\p@
            \topsep 8\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus4\p@
            \itemsep \parsep\@xpt{13} 
   \abovedisplayskip 10\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus5\p@
   \abovedisplayshortskip \z@ \@plus3\p@
   \belowdisplayshortskip 6\p@ \@plus3\p@ \@minus3\p@
   \belowdisplayskip \abovedisplayskip
   \let\leftmargin\leftmargini
            \parsep\z@ \@plus\p@
            \topsep 8\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus4\p@
            \itemsep \parsep\leftmargin\leftmargini
            \parsep\z@ \@plus\p@
            \topsep 8\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus4\p@
            \itemsep \parsep\bfseries}{Case 3:}
We are neither in Case~1 nor in Case~2.
Then, we have that
\(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \models \xi^{(0)}_1 \land \neg\,\xi^{(0)}_{\left\lvertI\right\rvert+1}\),
so \(B \neq \emptyset\),
but \(B\) does not contain \(\left\lvertI\right\rvert{+}1\) elements
of pairwise distance \(> 2r'\).

\begin{restatable}{claim}{rpGaifmanScatteredThreshold}
  \label{claim:proof:rp_gaifman:scattered_threshold}
  There exist \(c, \ell \in \brac{\left\lvertI\right\rvert}\)
  such that \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \models \xi^{(c)}_\ell \land \neg\,\xi^{(c-1)}_{\ell+1}\).\end{restatable}
\begin{claimproof}
  For every \(c \in [0,\left\lvertI\right\rvert]\),
  let \(\ell^{(c)} \in \mathbb{N} \cup \ensuremath{\{\infty\}}\) be maximal
  such that there exists a set \(X \subseteq B\) of \(\ell^{\paren{c}}\) elements
  of pairwise distance \(> 2(2c{+}1)r'\).
From \(B \neq \emptyset\),
  we obtain that \(\ell^{\paren{c}} \geqslant 1\) for each \(c \in \brac{0,\left\lvertI\right\rvert}\).
Since $B$ does not contain $\left\lvertI\right\rvert{+}1$ elements of pairwise distance
  $>2r'$, we know that
  \(\ell^{\paren{c}} \leqslant \left\lvertI\right\rvert\) for each \(c \in \brac{0,\left\lvertI\right\rvert}\).
  Furthermore, for all \(c \geqslant 1\), we have \(\ell^{\paren{c}} \leqslant \ell^{(c-1)}\),
  because \(2(2c{+}1)r' \geqslant 2(2(c{-}1){+}1)r'\).
Hence, we have
  $\left\lvertI\right\rvert \geqslant \ell^{\paren{0}} \geqslant \ell^{\paren{1}}  \geqslant
    \cdots \geqslant  \ell^{\paren{\left\lvertI\right\rvert}} \geqslant 1$.
  By the pigeonhole principle,
  there exists a \(c \in \brac{\left\lvertI\right\rvert}\)
  such that \(\ell^{(c-1)} = \ell^{\paren{c}}\).
We choose such a \(c\) and let \(\ell \coloneqq \ell^{\paren{c}}\).
  Since \(\ell = \ell^{\paren{c}}\), we have that \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \models \xi^{(c)}_\ell\).
  Since \(\ell = \ell^{(c-1)}\) and \(\ell^{(c-1)}\) is maximal,
  we have that  \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \models \neg\,\xi^{(c-1)}_{\ell+1}\).
\end{claimproof}
 
Consider arbitrary \(c, \ell \in \brac{\left\lvertI\right\rvert}\)
such that \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \models \xi^{(c)}_\ell \land \neg\,\xi^{(c-1)}_{\ell+1}\),
which exist due to \cref{claim:proof:rp_gaifman:scattered_threshold}.

   \let\leftmargin\leftmargini
            \parsep\z@ \@plus\p@
            \topsep 8\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus4\p@

            \parsep\z@ \@plus\p@
            \topsep 8\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus4\p@
            \itemsep \parsep\bfseries}{Case 3.1:}
\(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \models \delta_i (\bar{a}_I)\) for some \(i \in I\).
This implies that \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \models \mu' (\bar{a}_I)\)
and \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \models \mu (\bar{a}_I)\).

   \belowdisplayskip \abovedisplayskip
   \let\@listi\@listI\@xpt{13} 
   \abovedisplayskip 10\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus5\p@

   \belowdisplayskip \abovedisplayskip
   \let\leftmargin\leftmargini
            \parsep\z@ \@plus\p@
            \topsep 8\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus4\p@

            \parsep\z@ \@plus\p@
            \topsep 8\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus4\p@
            \itemsep \parsep\@xpt{13}

   \abovedisplayshortskip \z@ \@plus3\p@
   \belowdisplayshortskip 6\p@ \@plus3\p@ \@minus3\p@
   \belowdisplayskip \abovedisplayskip
   \let\leftmargin\leftmargini
            \parsep\z@ \@plus\p@
            \topsep 8\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus4\p@
            \itemsep \parsep\leftmargin\leftmargini
            \parsep\z@ \@plus\p@
            \topsep 8\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus4\p@
            \itemsep \parsep\@xpt{13} 
   \abovedisplayskip 10\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus5\p@
   \abovedisplayshortskip \z@ \@plus3\p@
   \belowdisplayshortskip 6\p@ \@plus3\p@ \@minus3\p@
   \belowdisplayskip \abovedisplayskip
   \let\leftmargin\leftmargini
            \parsep\z@ \@plus\p@
            \topsep 8\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus4\p@
            \itemsep \parsep\leftmargin\leftmargini
            \parsep\z@ \@plus\p@
            \topsep 8\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus4\p@
            \itemsep \parsep\bfseries}{Case 3.2:}
\(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \models \neg\,\psi^{(c)}_\ell (\bar{a}_I)\).
Then \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \models \mu' (\bar{a}_I)\).
We have to show that \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \models \mu (\bar{a}_I)\).

From \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \models \xi^{(c)}_\ell\),
we know that there exist \( b_1, \dots, b_\ell  \in B\)
of pairwise distance \(> 2(2c{+}1)r'\).
If there is a \(\nu \in \brac{\ell}\)
such that \(\distS{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}{\bar{a}_I; b_\nu} \,{>}\, r'\),
then \(b_\nu\) serves as a witness certifying that
\(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \models \mu (\bar{a}_I)\).
\emph{For contradiction},
assume that, for each \(\nu \in \brac{\ell}\),
we do \emph{not} have
\(\distS{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}{\bar{a}_I;b_\nu} \,{>}\, r'\).
Then, for every \(\nu \in \brac{\ell}\),
there is an \(i(\nu) \in I\)
such that \(\distS{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}{a_{i(\nu)}, b_\nu} \,{\leqslant}\, r'\).
 
\begin{restatable}{claim}{rpGaifmanPwFarNearElements}
  \label{claim:proof:rp_gaifman:pw_far_near_elements}
  For all distinct \(\nu,\nu' \in \brac{\ell}\),
  we have \(\distS{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}{a_{i(\nu)}, a_{i(\nu')}} > 4cr'\).
\end{restatable}
\begin{claimproof}
  For distinct \(\nu, \nu' \in [\ell]\),
  apply \cref{claim:distance_by_double_triangle}
  with \(s = 4cr'\),
  \(t = r'\),
  and \((c_1, c_2, d_1, d_2) = (a_{i(\nu)}, a_{i(\nu')}, b_\nu, b_{\nu'})\).
  This yields
  \(\distS{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}{c_1, c_2} \,{>}\, s\),
  so
  \(\distS{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}{a_{i(\nu)}, a_{i(\nu')}} \,{>}\, 4cr'\).
\end{claimproof}
 
\Cref{claim:proof:rp_gaifman:pw_far_near_elements} implies that
\(i(\nu) \neq i(\nu')\) holds for all distinct \(\nu, \nu' \in [\ell]\).
Thus, for \(J \coloneqq \ensuremath{\ensuremath{\{i(\nu) : \nu\in[\ell]\}}}\),
we have \(\left\lvertJ\right\rvert = \ell\).
Furthermore, for each \(\nu \in \brac{\ell}\),
the element \(b_\nu\) serves as a witness certifying that
\(
(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}},\bar{a}_I) \models \exists x_{k+1}\,\paren[\big]{
  \operatorname{dist}(x_{i(\nu)},x_{k+1}) \,{\leqslant}\, r'
  \mathbin{\mathchoice{\ }{}{}{} \land \mathchoice{\ }{}{}{}}
  \beta(x_{k+1})
}
\).
Combining this with
\cref{claim:proof:rp_gaifman:pw_far_near_elements} proves
that \(J\) serves as a witness certifying that
\(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \models \psi^{(c)}_\ell (\bar{a}_I)\).
However, recall that we currently consider the case that
\(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \models \neg\,\psi^{(c)}_\ell (\bar{a}_I)\),
a contradiction!
Thus, there must exist a \(\nu \in \brac{\ell}\)
such that \(\distS{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}{\bar{a}_I; b_\nu} \,{>}\, r'\).
This \(b_\nu\) serves as a witness certifying that
\(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \models \mu (\bar{a}_I)\).

            \parsep\z@ \@plus\p@
            \topsep 8\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus4\p@

            \topsep 8\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus4\p@
            \itemsep \parsep\@xpt{13} 
   \abovedisplayskip 10\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus5\p@
   \abovedisplayshortskip \z@ \@plus3\p@
   \belowdisplayshortskip 6\p@ \@plus3\p@ \@minus3\p@
   \belowdisplayskip \abovedisplayskip
   \let\leftmargin\leftmargini
            \parsep\z@ \@plus\p@
            \topsep 8\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus4\p@
