            \parsep\z@ \@plus\p@
            \topsep 8\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus4\p@
            \itemsep \parsep\bfseries}{Case 3.3:}
For all \(c, \ell \in \brac{\left\lvertI\right\rvert}\)
with \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \models \xi^{(c)}_\ell \land \neg\,\xi^{(c-1)}_{\ell+1}\),
we have \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \not\models \delta_i (\bar{a}_I)\) for all \(i \in I\),
and \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \models \psi^{(c)}_\ell (\bar{a}_I)\).
This is the only remaining case.
Since we are still in Case~3,
we have \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \not\models \mu' (\bar{a}_I)\).
In the following,
we show that also \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \not\models \mu (\bar{a}_I)\).

Let us fix \(c, \ell \in \brac{\left\lvertI\right\rvert}\)
according to \cref{claim:proof:rp_gaifman:scattered_threshold}.
Thus, we have \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \models \xi^{(c)}_\ell \land \neg\,\xi^{(c-1)}_{\ell+1}\)
and \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \not\models \delta_i (\bar{a})\) for all \(i \in I\),
and \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \models \psi^{(c)}_\ell (\bar{a})\).
\emph{For contradiction}, assume that \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \models \mu (\bar{a}_I)\).
Then there exists a \(b_0 \in B\) such that \(\operatorname{dist}^{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}(\bar{a}_I; b_0) > r'\).
Since, for all \(i \in I\),
we have \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \not\models \delta_i(\bar{a}_I)\),
we obtain
\(\distS{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}{\bar{a}_I; b_0} > \hat{r}\).
From \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \models \psi^{(c)}_\ell (\bar{a}_I)\),
we obtain that there exists a set \(J \subseteq I\)
with \(\left\lvertJ\right\rvert = \ell\)
such that
\(\distS{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}{a_j, a_{j'}}
  >
  4cr'
\)
holds for all distinct \(j, j' \in J\);
and for each \(j \in J\),
there exists a \(b_j \in B\) with
\(\distS{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}{a_j, b_j}
  \leqslant
  r'
\).
We would like to use the elements \(b_0, (b_j)_{j \in J}\) as witnesses
certifying that \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \models \xi^{(c-1)}_{\ell+1}\).
All that remains to be done
is to show that they have pairwise distance \(> 2(2(c{-}1){+}1)r'\).
Note that \(2(2(c{-}1){+}1)r' = 4cr'-2r'\).

For distinct \(j,j' \in J\),
we apply \cref{claim:distance_by_double_triangle}
with \(s = 4cr'-2r'\),
\(t=r'\),
and \((c_1, c_2, d_1, d_2) = (b_j, b_{j'}, a_j, a_{j'})\).
This yields
\(\distS{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}{c_1, c_2} > s\),
so \(\distS{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}{b_j, b_{j'}} > 4cr'-2r'\).

All that remains to be done is to show that
\(\distS{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}{b_j, b_0} > 4cr'-2r'\) for all \(j \in J\).
Consider a \(j \in J\).
We have
\(
  \hat{r}
  <
  \distS{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}{a_j, b_0}
  \leqslant
  \distS{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}{a_j, b_j} + \distS{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}{b_j, b_0}
  \leqslant
  r' + \distS{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}{b_j, b_0}
\).
Thus,
\(
  \distS{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}{b_j, b_0}
  >
  \hat{r} - r'
\).
Furthermore, by our choice of \(\tilde{r}, \hat{r}, r'\),
we have
\(\hat{r} - r' = \tilde{r} - 2r' = \operatorname{r} ({q{+}1}, {k}, {d}) - 2r'\),
which, by \eqref{eq:def:radius_function},
is equal to \(4kr' - 2r'\),
and this is \(\geqslant 4cr'-2r'\),
since \(c \leqslant \left\lvertI\right\rvert \leqslant k\).

Finally, we have shown that \(b_0, (b_j)_{j \in J}\) serve as witnesses
certifying that \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \models \xi^{(c-1)}_{\ell+1}\).
However, this is a contradiction to the fact that
\(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \models \neg\,\xi^{(c-1)}_{\ell+1}\).
Therefore, we obtain that \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \not \models\mu (\bar{a}_I)\).
This completes the proof that \(\mu'(\bar{x}_I)\)
is equivalent to \(\mu(\bar{x}_I)\).
<!-- end proof-1 -->
<!-- claim-2: body_md -->
\label{claim:distance_by_double_triangle}
Let \(s, t \in \mathbb{N}\),
  and let \(c_1, c_2, d_1, d_2 \in A\)
  such that
  \(\distS{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}{d_1, d_2} \,{>}\, s{+}2t\)
  and
  \(\distS{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}{c_j, d_j} \,{\leqslant}\, t\) for all \(j \in \ensuremath{\{1,2\}}\).
  Then we have \(\distS{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}{c_1, c_2} \,{>}\, s\).
<!-- end claim-2 -->
<!-- claim-3: body_md -->
\label{claim:proof:rp_gaifman:scattered_threshold}
  There exist \(c, \ell \in \brac{\left\lvertI\right\rvert}\)
  such that \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \models \xi^{(c)}_\ell \land \neg\,\xi^{(c-1)}_{\ell+1}\).
<!-- end claim-3 -->
<!-- claim-4: body_md -->
\label{claim:proof:rp_gaifman:pw_far_near_elements}
  For all distinct \(\nu,\nu' \in \brac{\ell}\),
  we have \(\distS{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}{a_{i(\nu)}, a_{i(\nu')}} > 4cr'\).
<!-- end claim-4 -->
<!-- example-6: body_md -->
\label{ex:GraphsAsTrees}
  This example shows how to transform \(\ensuremath{\@empty{FO}}\) definable problems over graphs
  into \(\ensuremath{\ensuremath{\ensuremath{\@empty{ngFOW}}}^+}\) definable problems over vertex-weighted trees of height \(1\).

  Let \(\sigma \coloneqq \ensuremath{\{E\}}\) for a binary relation symbol \(E\),
  let \(\mathbb{S}\) consist of a single abelian group,
  the group \((\mathbb{Z}, +)\) of integers with natural addition,
  let \(\ensuremath{\mathbb{P}} \coloneqq \ensuremath{\{\ensuremath{\@empty{P}}_=\}}\) with \(\left\llbracket \ensuremath{\@empty{P}}_= \right\rrbracket \coloneqq \ensuremath{\ensuremath{\{(i,i) : i \in \mathbb{Z}\}}}\),
  and let \(\ensuremath{\mathbf{W}} \coloneqq \ensuremath{\{\ensuremath{\mathtt{w}}_1, \ensuremath{\mathtt{w}}_2\}}\)
  for two unary weight symbols with range $\mathbb{Z}$.
For variables $x,y,e$, we let
  \begin{align*}
    \varphi_V(x)
    &\ \coloneqq\ \big(\,0 = \ensuremath{\mathtt{w}}_2(x)\,\big),\\
    \varphi_{E,1}(x,e)
    &\ \coloneqq\ \Big(\,\ensuremath{\@empty{P}}_=\bigl(\ensuremath{\mathtt{w}}_1(x), \ensuremath{\mathtt{w}}_1(e)\bigr) \
      \und \ \operatorname{dist}(x,e) \,{\leqslant}\, 2 \,\Big),\\
    \varphi_{E,2}(y,e)
    &\ \coloneqq\ \Big(\, \ensuremath{\@empty{P}}_=\bigl(\ensuremath{\mathtt{w}}_1(y), \ensuremath{\mathtt{w}}_2(e)\bigr) \
      \und \ \operatorname{dist}(y,e) \,{\leqslant}\, 2\,\Big),\\
    \intertext{and}
    \varphi_E(x,y)
    &\ \coloneqq\
    \varphi_V(x) \land \varphi_V(y) \land
    \exists e\,\Bigl(
    \bigr(\varphi_{E,1}(x,e) \land \varphi_{E,2}(y,e)\bigr)
    \lor
    \bigr(\varphi_{E,1}(y,e) \land \varphi_{E,2}(x,e)\bigr)
    \Bigr).
  \end{align*}

  For a formula \(\varphi(\bar{x}) \in \ensuremath{\@empty{FO}}\) of signature $\sigma$,
  we let \(\psi(\bar{x}) \) be the $\ensuremath{\ensuremath{\ensuremath{\@empty{ngFOW}}}^+}$ formula obtained from \(\varphi\)
  by replacing every subformula of the form \(\exists x\, \varphi'\)
  by \(\exists x\, (\varphi_V(x) \land \varphi')\)
  and replacing every atomic formula of the form \(E(x,y)\)
  by \(\varphi_E(x,y)\).

  Now let \(G\) be an undirected graph with vertex set \(V(G) = [n]\) for some \(n \in \ensuremath{\mathbb{N}_{\scriptscriptstyle \geqslant 1}}\).
  We let \(\mathcal{T}\) be the \((\sigma, \ensuremath{\mathbf{W}})\)-structure with
  universe \(T \coloneqq [0,n] \cup E(G)\),
  edges
  \(E^{\mathcal{T}} \coloneqq \ensuremath{\{0\}} \times \bigl([n] \cup E(G)\bigr)
  \cup \bigl([n] \cup E(G)\bigr) \times \ensuremath{\{0\}}\),
  and weights \(\ensuremath{\mathtt{w}}_1(i) \coloneqq i\) for all \(i \in [0,n]\),
  \(\ensuremath{\mathtt{w}}_2(0) \coloneqq 1\),
  \(\ensuremath{\mathtt{w}}_2(j) \coloneqq 0\) for all \(j \in [n]\),
  and \(\ensuremath{\mathtt{w}}_1(\ensuremath{\{i,j\}}) \coloneqq i\) and \(\ensuremath{\mathtt{w}}_2(\ensuremath{\{i,j\}}) \coloneqq j\)
  for all \(\ensuremath{\{i,j\}} \in E(G)\) with \(i < j\).
It can easily be verified that,
  for all \(\bar{v} \in [n]^{\left\lvert\bar{x}\right\rvert}\),
  it holds that \(G \models \varphi(\bar{v})\)
  if and only if \(\mathcal{T} \models \psi(\bar{v})\).
<!-- end example-6 -->
<!-- corollary-1: body_md -->
\label{cor:GNF_FOplus}
Let $q,k,d\in\mathbb{N}$. Every $\varphi\in\ensuremath{\@empty{FO}}^+(q,k,d)$ is equivalent to a
formula $\varphi'$ in Gaifman normal form for $\ensuremath{\@empty{FO}}^+(q,k,d)$.
Furthermore, $\operatorname*{free}(\varphi')=\operatorname*{free}(\varphi)$,
the outer (inner) quantifier rank of $\varphi'$
is at most $\operatorname*{qr}(\varphi)$ (resp.\ $\operatorname*{qr}(\varphi){-}1$),
the outer (inner) radius of $\varphi'$
is at most $\operatorname{r_1} ({q}, {k}, {d})$
(resp.\ $\operatorname{r_1} ({q{-}1}, {k{+}1}, {d})$),
and the width (inner distance)
is at most $k{+}q$
(resp.\ $\operatorname{r_1} ({q}, {k}, {d})+2\operatorname{r_1} ({q{-}1}, {k{+}1}, {d})$).
Moreover,
such a $\varphi'$ can be computed from $\varphi,q,k,d$.
<!-- end corollary-1 -->
<!-- corollary-2: body_md -->
\label{cor:GNF_FOMODplus}
Let $q,k,d\in\mathbb{N}$. Every $\varphi\in\ensuremath{\ensuremath{\@empty{FO{+}MOD}}^{+}_{M}}(q,k,d)$ is equivalent to a
formula $\varphi'$ in Gaifman normal form for $\ensuremath{\ensuremath{\@empty{FO{+}MOD}}^{+}_{M}}(q,k,d)$.
Furthermore, $\operatorname*{free}(\varphi')=\operatorname*{free}(\varphi)$,
the outer (inner) quantifier rank of $\varphi'$
is at most $\operatorname*{qr}(\varphi)$ (resp.\ $\operatorname*{qr}(\varphi){-}1$),
the outer (inner) radius of $\varphi'$
is at most $\operatorname{r_1} ({q}, {k}, {d})$
(resp.\ $\operatorname{r_1} ({q{-}1}, {k{+}1}, {d})$),
and the width (inner distance)
is at most $k{+}q$
(resp.\ $\operatorname{r_1} ({q}, {k}, {d})+2\operatorname{r_1} ({q{-}1}, {k{+}1}, {d})$).
Moreover,
such a $\varphi'$ can be computed from $\varphi,q,k,d$.
<!-- end corollary-2 -->
<!-- lemma-3: body_md -->
\label{lemma:LocalityNotion}
  Let $r\in\mathbb{N}$, let $\varphi$ be a formula, and let $\bar x=(x_1,\ldots,x_k)$ be a
  non-empty tuple of variables such that $\operatorname*{free}(\varphi)\subseteq\ensuremath{\{x_1,\ldots,x_k\}}$.
  If $\varphi(\bar x)$ is $r$-local, then $\varphi(\bar y)$ is $r$-local for every non-empty tuple $\bar y=(y_1,\ldots,y_\ell)$ of variables such that $\operatorname*{free}(\varphi)\subseteq \ensuremath{\{y_1,\ldots,y_\ell\}}$.
<!-- end lemma-3 -->
<!-- proof-2: body_md -->
We assume that $\varphi(\bar x)$ is $r$-local.


Let $\bar y=(y_1,\ldots,y_\ell)$ be a non-empty tuple of variables such that $\operatorname*{free}(\varphi)\subseteq\ensuremath{\{y_1,\ldots,y_\ell\}}$.
Consider an arbitrary structure $\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}$ and a tuple $\bar b=(b_1,\ldots,b_\ell)\in A^\ell$ to which we assign the variables in $\bar y$. We have to show that
\begin{equation}\label{eq:LocalityNotion:goal}
 \ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}\models\varphi(\bar b) \ \iff \ \ensuremath{\ensuremath{\mathcal{N}_{r}^{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}(\bar{b})}} \models \varphi(\bar{b}).
\end{equation}
Let us choose an element $c\in \ensuremath{\{b_1,\ldots,b_\ell\}}$ that will be fixed henceforth. Let $\bar b'$ be the tuple obtained from $\bar b$ by replacing $b_j$ with $c$ for every $j\in[\ell]$ with $y_j\not\in\operatorname*{free}(\varphi)$.

By the \emph{coincidence lemma},
the assignment of variables that are not free in a formula is irrelevant.
Hence, we have
\begin{equation}\label{eq:fuenf}
  \ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}\models\varphi(\bar b) \ \iff \ \ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}\models\varphi(\bar b'),
\end{equation}
and
\begin{equation}\label{eq:sechs}
\ensuremath{\ensuremath{\mathcal{N}_{r}^{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}(\bar{b})}} \models \varphi(\bar{b}) \ \iff \ \ensuremath{\ensuremath{\mathcal{N}_{r}^{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}(\bar{b})}} \models \varphi(\bar{b}').
\end{equation}
Now, let $\bar a=(a_1,\ldots,a_k)$ be the assignment
for $\bar x=(x_1,\ldots,x_k)$,
where, for every $i\in[k]$, we let
\begin{itemize}
\item $a_i\coloneqq c$ if $x_i\not\in\operatorname*{free}(\varphi)$, and
\item $a_i \coloneqq b_j$ if $x_i\in \operatorname*{free}(\varphi)$ and $j\in[\ell]$ such that $y_j=x_i$.
\end{itemize}
Due to the coincidence lemma,
for the evaluation of the formula $\varphi$,
assigning $\bar a$ to $\bar x$ has the same effect
as assigning $\bar b'$ to $\bar y$.
In particular, we obtain that
\begin{equation}\label{eq:sieben}
  \ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}\models\varphi(\bar b') \ \iff \ \ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}\models\varphi(\bar a),
\end{equation}
and
\begin{equation}\label{eq:acht}
\ensuremath{\ensuremath{\mathcal{N}_{r}^{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}(\bar{b})}} \models \varphi(\bar{b}') \ \iff \ \ensuremath{\ensuremath{\mathcal{N}_{r}^{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}(\bar{b})}} \models \varphi(\bar{a}).
\end{equation}
According to the assumption of the lemma, $\varphi(\bar x)$ is $r$-local. Thus, we obtain that
\begin{equation}\label{eq:neun}
\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}\models\varphi(\bar a) \ \iff \ \ensuremath{\ensuremath{\mathcal{N}_{r}^{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}(\bar{a})}}\models\varphi(\bar a),
\end{equation}
and, using the $r$-locality also for the structure $\ensuremath{\mathcal{B}}\coloneqq\ensuremath{\ensuremath{\mathcal{N}_{r}^{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}(\bar{b})}}$,
we obtain that
$
  \ensuremath{\mathcal{B}}\models\varphi(\bar a) \iff
  \ensuremath{\ensuremath{\mathcal{N}_{r}^{\ensuremath{\mathcal{B}}}(\bar{a})}}\models\varphi(\bar a)$.
Since all entries of the tuple $\bar a$ also occur in the tuple $\bar b$,
it is straightforward to see that
\(\ensuremath{\ensuremath{\mathcal{N}_{r}^{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}(\bar{a})}} \subseteq \ensuremath{\ensuremath{\mathcal{N}_{r}^{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}(\bar{b})}} = \ensuremath{\mathcal{B}}\),
and thus,
$\ensuremath{\ensuremath{\mathcal{N}_{r}^{\ensuremath{\mathcal{B}}}(\bar{a})}}=\ensuremath{\ensuremath{\mathcal{N}_{r}^{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}(\bar{a})}}$.
Hence, we have
\begin{equation}\label{eq:zehn}
  \ensuremath{\ensuremath{\mathcal{N}_{r}^{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}(\bar{b})}}\models\varphi(\bar a) \ \iff \
  \ensuremath{\ensuremath{\mathcal{N}_{r}^{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}(\bar{a})}}\models\varphi(\bar a).
\end{equation}
In summary, we obtain the following equivalences:
\[
\begin{array}{ccrcc}
  \ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}\models\varphi(\bar b)
& \stackrel{\eqref{eq:fuenf},\eqref{eq:sieben}}{\iff}
& \ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}\models\varphi(\bar a)
& \stackrel{\eqref{eq:neun}}{\iff}
& \ensuremath{\ensuremath{\mathcal{N}_{r}^{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}(\bar{a})}}\models\varphi(\bar{a})
  \\
& \stackrel{\eqref{eq:zehn}}{\iff}
& \ensuremath{\ensuremath{\mathcal{N}_{r}^{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}(\bar{b})}}\models\varphi(\bar{a})
& \stackrel{\eqref{eq:acht},\eqref{eq:sechs}}{\iff}
& \ensuremath{\ensuremath{\mathcal{N}_{r}^{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}(\bar{b})}}\models\varphi(\bar{b}).
\end{array}
\]
This proves that equivalence \eqref{eq:LocalityNotion:goal} holds.
Hence, the proof of \cref{lemma:LocalityNotion} is complete.
<!-- end proof-2 -->
<!-- claim-5: body_md -->
The formula \(\varphi(x)\) is not equivalent to
  a Boolean combination \(\varphi'(x)\) of local formulas of quantifier rank at most \(2\)
  and of basic local sentences.
<!-- end claim-5 -->
<!-- definition-1: body_md -->
\label[definition]{def:fow}
  Let \(\sigma\) be a signature, \(\mathbb{S}\) a collection of rings and/or abelian groups,
  \(\ensuremath{\mathbf{W}}\) a finite set of weight symbols, and
  \((\ensuremath{\mathbb{P}}, \operatorname*{ar}, \ensuremath{\textup{type}}, \left\llbracket \cdot \right\rrbracket)\) an \(\mathbb{S}\)-predicate
  collection.
  The set of \emph{formulas} and \emph{\(\mathbb{S}\)-terms} for \(\ensuremath{\ensuremath{\ensuremath{\ensuremath{\@empty{FOW}}}^+}(\ensuremath{\mathbb{P}})[\sigma,\mathbb{S},\ensuremath{\mathbf{W}}]}\)
  is built according to the following rules.

  \begin{enumerate}[(WA)]
    \renewcommand{\textbf{(ngTA)}}{\textbf{(ngTA)}}
    \renewcommand{ngTA}{DA}
    \item\label{def:fowplus-dist}
      \(\operatorname{dist}(x_1,x_2)\,{\leqslant}\, d\) are formulas,
      for \(x_1, x_2 \in \ensuremath{\textsf{\upshape vars}}\) and \(d \in \mathbb{N}\).
    \renewcommand{ngTA}{RA}
    \item\label{def:fo-atomic}\label{def:fowplus-truefalse}
      \(\true\) and \(\false\) and
      \(x_1{=}x_2\) and \(R(x_1, \dots, x_k)\) are formulas for
      \(R \in \sigma\), \(k \coloneqq \operatorname*{ar}(R)\),
      and \(x_1, x_2, \dots, x_k \in \ensuremath{\textsf{\upshape vars}}\).
      In particular, if \(\operatorname*{ar}(R) = 0\), then \(R()\) is a formula.
    \renewcommand{ngTA}{WA}
    \item\label{def:fow-wsimple}
      \(\bigl(s = \ensuremath{\mathtt{w}}(\bar{x})\bigr)\) is a formula, for
      \(\ensuremath{\mathtt{w}} \in \ensuremath{\mathbf{W}}\),
      \(S = \ensuremath{\textup{type}}(\ensuremath{\mathtt{w}})\), \(s \in S\), \(\ell = \operatorname*{ar}(\ensuremath{\mathtt{w}})\),
      and \(\bar{x} = (x_1, \dots, x_\ell)\)
      a tuple of \(\ell\) pairwise distinct variables.
    \renewcommand{ngTA}{BC}
    \item\label{def:fo-bool}
      If \(\varphi\) and \(\psi\) are formulas,
      then \(\neg \varphi\) and \((\varphi \lor \psi)\) are formulas.
    \renewcommand{ngTA}{\(\exists\)}
    \item\label{def:fo-exists}
      If \(\varphi\) is a formula and \(x \in \ensuremath{\textsf{\upshape vars}}\),
      then \(\exists x\, \varphi\) is a formula.
    \renewcommand{ngTA}{\(\Sigma\)}
    \item\label{def:fow-finitegroup}
      If \(\varphi\) is a formula,
      \(\ensuremath{\mathtt{w}} \in \ensuremath{\mathbf{W}}\),
      \(S = \ensuremath{\textup{type}}(\ensuremath{\mathtt{w}})\) is \textbf{finite},
      \(s \in S\),
      \(\ell = \operatorname*{ar}(\ensuremath{\mathtt{w}})\),
      and \(\bar{x} = (x_1, \dots, x_\ell)\) is a tuple of
      \(\ell\) pairwise distinct variables,
      then \(\bigl( s = \sum \ensuremath{\mathtt{w}}(\bar{x}).\varphi \bigr)\) is a formula.
    \renewcommand{ngTA}{TA}
    \item\label{def:fow-P}
      \(\ensuremath{\@empty{P}}(t_1, \dots, t_m)\) is a formula for
      \(\ensuremath{\@empty{P}} \in \ensuremath{\mathbb{P}}\),
      \(m = \operatorname*{ar}(\ensuremath{\@empty{P}})\),
      and \(\mathbb{S}\)-terms \(t_1, \dots, t_m\)
      with \(\ensuremath{\textup{type}}(\ensuremath{\@empty{P}}) = \bigl(\ensuremath{\textup{type}}(t_1), \dots, \ensuremath{\textup{type}}(t_m)\bigr)\).
    \renewcommand{ngTA}{T1}
    \item\label{def:fow-constterm}
      \(s\) is an \(\mathbb{S}\)-term of type \(S\), for every \(S \in \mathbb{S}\) and every \(s \in S\).
    \renewcommand{ngTA}{T2}
    \item\label{def:fow-wsimpleterm}
      \(\ensuremath{\mathtt{w}}(x_1, \dots, x_\ell)\) is an \(\mathbb{S}\)-term of type \(S\),
      for every \(S \in \mathbb{S}\), every \(\ensuremath{\mathtt{w}} \in \ensuremath{\mathbf{W}}\) of type \(S\),
      and every tuple \((x_1, \dots, x_\ell)\)
      of \(\ell \coloneqq \operatorname*{ar}(\ensuremath{\mathtt{w}})\) pairwise distinct variables in \(\ensuremath{\textsf{\upshape vars}}\).
    \renewcommand{ngTA}{T3}
    \item\label{def:fow-plustimesterm}
      If \(t_1\) and \(t_2\) are \(\mathbb{S}\)-terms of the same type \(S\),
      then \((t_1 \ensuremath{+_{}} t_2)\) and \((t_1 \ensuremath{-_{}} t_2)\)
      are also \(\mathbb{S}\)-terms of type \(S\);
      furthermore, if \(S\) is a ring (and not just an abelian group),
      then also \((t_1 \ensuremath{{\cdot}_{}} t_2)\) is an \(\mathbb{S}\)-term of type \(S\).
  \end{enumerate}

  \noindent
  Let \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{I}} = (\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}, \beta)\) be a \((\sigma, \ensuremath{\mathbf{W}})\)-interpretation.
  For a formula or \(\mathbb{S}\)-term \(\xi\) from \(\ensuremath{\ensuremath{\ensuremath{\ensuremath{\@empty{FOW}}}^+}(\ensuremath{\mathbb{P}})[\sigma,\mathbb{S},\ensuremath{\mathbf{W}}]}\),
  the semantics \(\left\llbracket \xi \right\rrbracket^\ensuremath{\mathcal{I}}\) is defined as follows.

  \begin{enumerate}[(WA)]
    \renewcommand{\textbf{(ngTA)}}{\textbf{(ngTA)}}
    \renewcommand{ngTA}{DA}
    \item
      $\left\llbracket \operatorname{dist}(x_1,x_2)\,{\leqslant}\,d \right\rrbracket^{\ensuremath{\mathcal{I}}}=1$ if
      $\operatorname{dist}^{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}(\beta(x_1),\beta(x_2))\leqslant d$, and
      $\left\llbracket \operatorname{dist}(x_1,x_2)\,{\leqslant}\,d \right\rrbracket^{\ensuremath{\mathcal{I}}}=0$ otherwise.
    \renewcommand{ngTA}{RA}
    \item
      $\left\llbracket \true \right\rrbracket^{\ensuremath{\mathcal{I}}}=1$; $\left\llbracket \false \right\rrbracket^{\ensuremath{\mathcal{I}}}= 0$;
      \(\left\llbracket x_1{=}x_2 \right\rrbracket^\ensuremath{\mathcal{I}} = 1\) if \(\beta(x_1) = \beta(x_2)\),
      and \(\left\llbracket x_1{=}x_2 \right\rrbracket^\ensuremath{\mathcal{I}} = 0\) otherwise;
      \(\left\llbracket R(x_1, \dots, x_k) \right\rrbracket^\ensuremath{\mathcal{I}} = 1\) if
      \(\bigl(\beta(x_1), \dots, \beta(x_k)\bigr) \in R^{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}\), and
      \(\left\llbracket R(x_1, \dots, x_k) \right\rrbracket^\ensuremath{\mathcal{I}} = 0\) otherwise.
    \renewcommand{ngTA}{WA}
    \item
      \(\left\llbracket \bigl(s = \ensuremath{\mathtt{w}}(\bar{x})\bigr) \right\rrbracket^{\ensuremath{\mathcal{I}}} = 1\)
      if \(s= \ensuremath{\mathtt{w}}^\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \bigl(\beta(x_1), \dots, \beta(x_\ell)\bigr)\), and
      \(\left\llbracket \bigl(s = \ensuremath{\mathtt{w}}(\bar{x})\bigr) \right\rrbracket^\ensuremath{\mathcal{I}} = 0\) otherwise.
    \renewcommand{ngTA}{BC}
    \item
      \(\left\llbracket \neg \varphi \right\rrbracket^\ensuremath{\mathcal{I}} = 1 - \left\llbracket \varphi \right\rrbracket^\ensuremath{\mathcal{I}}\)
      \ and \ \(\left\llbracket (\varphi \lor \psi) \right\rrbracket = \max \ensuremath{\bigl\{ \left\llbracket \varphi \right\rrbracket^\ensuremath{\mathcal{I}}, \left\llbracket \psi \right\rrbracket^\ensuremath{\mathcal{I}} \bigr\}}\).
    \renewcommand{ngTA}{\(\exists\)}
    \item
      \(\left\llbracket \exists x\, \varphi \right\rrbracket^\ensuremath{\mathcal{I}} = \max \ensuremath{\bigl\{ \left\llbracket \varphi \right\rrbracket^{\ensuremath{\mathcal{I}}\frac{v}{x}} \ \mathrel{:} \  v \in A \bigr\}}\).
    \renewcommand{ngTA}{\(\Sigma\)}
    \item
      \(\left\llbracket \bigl(s=\sum \ensuremath{\mathtt{w}}(\bar{x}).\varphi\bigr) \right\rrbracket^\ensuremath{\mathcal{I}} = 1\)
      if \(s = \underset{\bar{v} \in M}{\sum_S} \ensuremath{\mathtt{w}}^\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}(\bar{v})\)
      for \(M \coloneqq \ensuremath{\bigl\{ (v_1, \dots, v_\ell) \in A^\ell \ \mathrel{:} \  \left\llbracket \varphi \right\rrbracket^{\ensuremath{\mathcal{I}} \frac{v_1, \dots, v_\ell}{x_1, \dots, x_\ell}}=1 \bigr\}}\),
      and \(\left\llbracket \bigl(s=\sum \ensuremath{\mathtt{w}}(\bar{x}).\varphi\bigr) \right\rrbracket^\ensuremath{\mathcal{I}} = 0\)
      otherwise.
      By convention, \(\underset{\bar{v} \in M}{\sum_S} \ensuremath{\mathtt{w}}^\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}(\bar{v}) = \ensuremath{0_{S}}\) if \(M = \emptyset\).
    \renewcommand{ngTA}{TA}
    \item
      \(\left\llbracket \ensuremath{\@empty{P}}(t_1, \dots, t_m) \right\rrbracket^\ensuremath{\mathcal{I}} = 1\) if
      \(\bigl(\left\llbracket t_1 \right\rrbracket^\ensuremath{\mathcal{I}}, \dots, \left\llbracket t_m \right\rrbracket^\ensuremath{\mathcal{I}}\bigr) \in \left\llbracket \ensuremath{\@empty{P}} \right\rrbracket\),
      and \(\left\llbracket \ensuremath{\@empty{P}}(t_1,\ldots,t_m) \right\rrbracket^\ensuremath{\mathcal{I}} = 0\) otherwise.
    \renewcommand{ngTA}{T1}
    \item
      \(\left\llbracket s \right\rrbracket^\ensuremath{\mathcal{I}} = s\) for \(s \in S\) for some \(S \in \mathbb{S}\).
    \renewcommand{ngTA}{T2}
    \item
      \(\left\llbracket \ensuremath{\mathtt{w}}(x_1, \dots, x_\ell) \right\rrbracket^\ensuremath{\mathcal{I}} =
      \ensuremath{\mathtt{w}}^\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}\bigl(\beta(x_1), \dots, \beta(x_\ell)\bigr)\).
    \renewcommand{ngTA}{T3}
    \item \(\left\llbracket (t_1 \ast t_2) \right\rrbracket^\ensuremath{\mathcal{I}} = \left\llbracket t_1 \right\rrbracket^\ensuremath{\mathcal{I}} \ast_S \left\llbracket t_2 \right\rrbracket^\ensuremath{\mathcal{I}}\),
      for \(\ast \in \ensuremath{\{\ensuremath{+_{}}, \ensuremath{-_{}}, \ensuremath{{\cdot}_{}}\}}\).
  \end{enumerate}
<!-- end definition-1 -->
<!-- claim-6: body_md -->
Let $\varphi(x)$ be the formula of Example~\ref{example:noGNFforFOW}.
  There exists no formula that is equivalent to
  $\varphi(x)$ and that is a Boolean combination of sentences and
  local formulas of $\ensuremath{\ensuremath{\ensuremath{\@empty{FOW}}}^+}$.
<!-- end claim-6 -->
<!-- proof-3: body_md -->
Note that $\ensuremath{\ensuremath{\@empty{ngFOW}}}$ is a logic in which the \emph{coincidence lemma}
holds.
Thus, for any $\varphi\in\ensuremath{\ensuremath{\@empty{ngFOW}}}$, the following it true:
if $\varphi(\bar x)$ is $r$-local for some non-empty tuple $\bar x$
that contains all the free variables of $\varphi$,
then $\varphi(\bar x)$ is $r$-local for \emph{every} non-empty tuple
$\bar x$ that contains all the free variables of $\varphi$
(cf.~\cref{lemma:LocalityNotion} in \cref{appendix:LocalityNotion}).
We will tacitly use this throughout the following proof.
\medskip

For \(k = 0\), by definition,
we have \(\operatorname{L} ({q}, {0}, {d}) = \emptyset\),
and there is nothing to prove.
We proceed by induction on $q$ to prove the assertion of the lemma for
$\operatorname{L} ({q}, {k}, {d})$ for all $k\geqslant 1$ and $d\geqslant 0$.
\medskip

For the induction base with $q=0$, consider arbitrary $k\geqslant 1$ and $d\geqslant 0$.
By definition, we have \(\operatorname{L} ({0}, {k}, {d}) = \ensuremath{\ensuremath{\@empty{F}}(0,k,d)}\).
Concerning the claimed locality of the formulas,
recall from \cref{eq:def:radius_function} that \(\operatorname{r} ({0}, {k}, {d}) = \max \ensuremath{\{1, d\}}\).

By definition, $\ensuremath{\ensuremath{\@empty{F}}(0,k,d)}$ consists of quantifier-free formulas
that have free variables among $\ensuremath{\{x_1,\ldots,x_k\}}$ and that are
Boolean combinations of

\begin{enumerate}[(a)]
\item\label{item:LocalityLemma:RA}
relational atoms,
\item\label{item:LocalityLemma:DA}
distance atoms with maximal distance at most $d$,
\item\label{item:LocalityLemma:WA}
weight atoms of the form $\big(s=\ensuremath{\mathtt{w}}(y_1,\ldots,y_\ell)\big)$,
with $y_1,\ldots,y_\ell\in\ensuremath{\{x_1,\ldots,x_k\}}$, $s\in \ensuremath{\textup{type}}(\ensuremath{\mathtt{w}})$
\item\label{item:LocalityLemma:ngTA}
  neighbourhood-guarded term-comparison atoms of the form\\
   \(\Big(
       \ensuremath{\@empty{P}}(t_1, \dots, t_m)
       \land
       \ensuremath{\bigwedge}_{y \in \bigcup_{i=1}^{m} \operatorname*{free}(t_i) \setminus \ensuremath{\{x\}}} \operatorname{dist}(x,y) \leqslant d'
       \Bigr)\),
   \ with $d'\leqslant d$.
\end{enumerate}

\noindent
It is straightforward to see that
\begin{itemize}
\item
  atoms of the form \eqref{item:LocalityLemma:RA} are 0-local
\item
  atoms of the form \eqref{item:LocalityLemma:DA} are $d$-local
\item
  atoms of the form \eqref{item:LocalityLemma:WA} are $1$-local\@gobble{We obtain 1-locality, but not 0-locality, due to the particular
   form of the \emph{locality condition} of weighted structures. E.g.,
   $\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}$ with $A=\ensuremath{\{1,2,3,4\}}$, $R^{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}=\ensuremath{\{(1,2,3,4)\}}$, and
   $\ensuremath{\mathtt{w}}^{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}(1,2)=s\in S$ with $s\neq 0_S$ is a weighted
   structure that witnesses that the formula
   $\big(s=\ensuremath{\mathtt{w}}(x_1,x_2)\big)$ is not $0$-local.
}\item
  atoms of the form \eqref{item:LocalityLemma:ngTA} are
  $\max\ensuremath{\{d,1\}}$-local.\@gobble{With the same reasoning as for the cases
  \eqref{item:LocalityLemma:WA} and \eqref{item:LocalityLemma:DA}, \enquote{$\ensuremath{\@empty{P}}(t_1, \dots, t_m)$} is
  1-local (but not necessarily 0-local), and the distance atoms are $d$-local.
}\end{itemize}

As locality is preserved under Boolean combinations (without increasing
the radius), this proves that all formulas in $\operatorname{L} ({0}, {k}, {d})$ are $\operatorname{r} ({0}, {k}, {d})$-local.
\medskip

For the induction step from $q$ to $q{+}1$, consider arbitrary
$q,d\in\mathbb{N}$. The induction hypothesis states that the
assertion of the lemma holds for all formulas in $\operatorname{L} ({q}, {k'}, {d})$ for
all $k'\in\mathbb{N}$. Now consider an arbitrary $k\in\mathbb{N}$ with $k\geqslant 1$.
Along the particular definition of the sets $\operatorname{L} ({q{+}1}, {k}, {d})$ and
$\ensuremath{\ensuremath{\@empty{F}}(q{+}1,k,d)}$, and by using the induction hypothesis, it is
straightforward to see that $\operatorname{L} ({q{+}1}, {k}, {d})\subseteq\ensuremath{\ensuremath{\@empty{F}}(q{+}1,k,d)}$.

To prove the claimed locality of the formulas in $\operatorname{L} ({q{+}1}, {k}, {d})$,
consider an arbitrary formula $\varphi\in \operatorname{L} ({q{+}1}, {k}, {d})$.
By the definition of the set $\operatorname{L} ({q{+}1}, {k}, {d})$, we have
$\operatorname*{free}(\varphi)\subseteq\ensuremath{\{x_1,\ldots,x_k\}}$, and $\varphi$
is a Boolean combination of formulas of
the forms
\ref{itm:def:loc:kind_previous_rank},
\ref{itm:def:loc:kind_distance_atom},
\ref{itm:def:loc:kind_near},
or
\ref{itm:def:loc:kind_weight_aggregation}.
As locality is preserved under Boolean combinations (without
increasing the radius), it suffices to consider the case that $\varphi$
itself is of one of the forms
\ref{itm:def:loc:kind_previous_rank},
\ref{itm:def:loc:kind_distance_atom},
\ref{itm:def:loc:kind_near},
or
\ref{itm:def:loc:kind_weight_aggregation}.

For the remainder of this proof let
\begin{equation*}k'\ \coloneqq \ k{+}\wmaxar
  \quad\text{and}\quad
  \tilde{r}\ \coloneqq\ \operatorname{r} ({q{+}1}, {k}, {d})
  \quad\text{and} \quad
  r'\ \coloneqq\ \operatorname{r} ({q}, {k'}, {d}).
\end{equation*}
Our goal is to show that $\varphi$ is $\tilde{r}$-local.

\begin{itemize}
\item
If $\varphi$ is of the form \ref{itm:def:loc:kind_previous_rank},
then $\varphi\in\operatorname{L} ({q}, {k'}, {d})$.
By the induction hypothesis, $\varphi$ is $r'$-local.
By \cref{eq:def:radius_function} we have $\tilde{r}=4k r'\geqslant r'$, and
thus $\varphi$ is also $\tilde{r}$-local.
\medskip

\item
If $\varphi$ is of the form \ref{itm:def:loc:kind_distance_atom}, we have
\[
  \varphi \ = \quad
  \operatorname{dist}(x_i,x_j)\,{\leqslant}\,\tilde{d}\,,
\]
with $i,j\in[k]$ and $\tilde{d}\leqslant \tilde{r}$.
Hence, $\varphi$ obviously is $\tilde{r}$-local.
\medskip

\item
  If $\varphi$ is of the form \ref{itm:def:loc:kind_near}, we have
  \[
    \varphi \ = \quad
        \exists x_{k+1}
        \,
        \paren[\big]{
          \
          \operatorname{dist}(x_i,x_{k+1}) \,{\leqslant}\,
          \hat{d}
          \;\mathbin{\mathchoice{\ }{}{}{} \land \mathchoice{\ }{}{}{}}\;
          \lambda
          \
        }
  \]
      with $i\in[k]$, \(\lambda \in \operatorname{L} ({q}, {k'}, {d})\),
      $\hat{d}\leqslant\hat{r} \coloneqq \tilde{r}-r'$, and $\operatorname*{free}(\lambda)\subseteq\operatorname*{free}(\varphi)\cup\ensuremath{\{x_{k+1}\}}$.
      Let $\bar x=(x_{i_1},\ldots,x_{i_\ell})$ be a non-empty tuple of variables that contains all the free
      variables of $\varphi$. We extend this tuple by the variable
      $x_{k+1}$ to $\bar
      x'\coloneqq (x_{i_1},\ldots,x_{i_\ell},x_{k+1})$.

      The induction hypothesis yields that $\lambda(\bar x')$ is
      $r'$-local around $\bar x'$.

      The formula $\big(\operatorname{dist}(x_i,x_{k+1})\,{\leqslant}\,\hat{d}
      \;\und\;\lambda\big)$ enforces in its models that the
      $r'$-neighbourhood around $\bar x'$ is contained in the
      $(r'{+}\hat{d})$-neighbourhood around $\bar x$.
      Since $r'{+}\hat{d}\leqslant \tilde{r}$, this implies that $\varphi$ is
      $\tilde{r}$-local.
      \medskip

\item
If $\varphi$ is of the form \ref{itm:def:loc:kind_weight_aggregation}, we have
\[
  \varphi \ = \quad
\Big(
  s =
  \sum \ensuremath{\mathtt{w}}(\bar y).
  \big(\;
     \lambda \; \und \!\!
     \Oder_{i\in I, j\in[\ell]}
 \!\!        \operatorname{dist}(x_i,y_j)\,{\leqslant}\, r'
  \,\big)
\Big)\,, \quad\text{where}
\]
 $\ensuremath{\mathtt{w}}\in\ensuremath{\mathbf{W}}$,
  $S=\ensuremath{\textup{type}}(\ensuremath{\mathtt{w}})$ is finite, $s\in S$, $\ell=\operatorname*{ar}(\ensuremath{\mathtt{w}})\geqslant 1$,  $\bar
  y=(y_1,\ldots,y_\ell)=(x_{k+1},\ldots,x_{k+\ell})$,
  $\emptyset\neq I\subseteq [k]$,
  and
  $\lambda\in\operatorname{L} ({q}, {k'}, {d})$ with $\operatorname*{free}(\lambda)\subseteq\operatorname*{free}(\varphi)\cup\ensuremath{\{x_{k+1},\ldots,x_{k+\ell}\}}$.

Recall that all weighted structures $\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}$ satisfy the \emph{locality
  condition}, and hence $\ensuremath{\mathtt{w}}^{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}(\bar b)=0_S$ holds for all
tuples $\bar b$ that do not form a clique in the Gaifman graph of
$\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}$. This implies that
$\varphi$ is equivalent to the formula
$\varphi'\coloneqq \big(s=\sum\ensuremath{\mathtt{w}}(\bar y).\lambda' \big)$ with
\[
  \lambda' \ \coloneqq \quad
  \Big(\;
     \lambda \ \und \!\!
     \Oder_{i\in I, j\in[\ell]}
 \!\!        \operatorname{dist}(x_i,y_j)\,{\leqslant}\, r'
   \ \und \!\!
      \Und_{1\leqslant j<j'\leqslant \ell} \operatorname{dist}(x_{k+j},x_{k+j'})\,{\leqslant}\,1
 \,\Big)\,.
\]
      Let $\bar x=(x_{i_1},\ldots,x_{i_\ell})$ be a non-empty tuple of variables
      that contains all the free variables of $\varphi$.
      We extend $\bar x$ by the variables
      $x_{k+1},\ldots,x_{k+\ell}$ to $\bar
      x'\coloneqq (x_{i_1},\ldots,x_{i_\ell},x_{k+1},\ldots,\allowbreak x_{k+\ell})$.

      The induction hypothesis yields that $\lambda(\bar x')$ is
      $r'$-local around $\bar x'$.
      Since $r'\geqslant 1$,
      this implies that also $\lambda'(\bar x')$ is $r'$-local.

      Note that the formula $\lambda'$ enforces in its models that the
      $r'$-neighbourhood around $\bar x'$ is contained in the
      $(r'{+}r'{+}1)$-neighbourhood around $\bar x$.
      This implies that the formula $\varphi'$ is
      $\paren{2r'{+}1}$-local.
      Since $\tilde{r}=4kr'\geqslant 2r'{+} 1$, this implies that $\varphi'$ is
      $\tilde{r}$-local.
      As $\varphi$ is equivalent to $\varphi'$, we obtain that
      $\varphi$ is $\tilde{r}$-local.
    \end{itemize}
    \smallskip

\noindent
This completes the proof of \cref{lemma:LocalityLemma}.
<!-- end proof-3 -->
<!-- lemma-4: body_md -->
\label{lemma:PropertiesOfSqkd}
Let $q,k,d\in\mathbb{N}$.
Every formula in $\operatorname{S} ({q}, {k}, {d})$ is
\begin{itemize}
\item
  an atomic sentence in $\operatorname{S} ({0}, {0}, {0})$, or
\item
  a local aggregation sentence for $\ensuremath{\ensuremath{\@empty{F}}(q,k,d)}$ of inner radius as most
  $\operatorname{r} ({q{-}1}, {k{+}\wmaxar}, {d})$, or
\item
  a basic local sentence for $\ensuremath{\ensuremath{\@empty{F}}(q,k,d)}$ of
  inner radius at most $\operatorname{r} ({q{-}1}, {k{+}\wmaxar}, {d})$,
  of width at most $k{+}1{+}(q{-}1)\wmaxar$,
  and
  of inner distance at most $\operatorname{r} ({q}, {k}, {d})+2\operatorname{r} ({q{-}1}, {k{+}\wmaxar}, {d})$.
\end{itemize}
<!-- end lemma-4 -->
<!-- proof-4: body_md -->
We proceed by induction on $q$. For the induction base with $q=0$,
note that for all $k,d\in\mathbb{N}$, the \emph{atomic sentences in $\ensuremath{\ensuremath{\@empty{F}}(0,k,d)}$} are exactly the sentences of the form
\begin{itemize}
\item
  $\true$, $\false$,
\item
  $R()$ for a relation symbol $R\in\sigma$ of arity 0,
\item
  $\big(s=\ensuremath{\mathtt{w}}()\big)$ for a weight symbol $\ensuremath{\mathtt{w}}\in\ensuremath{\mathbf{W}}$ of arity 0,
  and
\item
  $\ensuremath{\@empty{P}}(t_1,\ldots,t_m)$ for $\ensuremath{\@empty{P}}\in\ensuremath{\mathbb{P}}$
and terms
$t_1,\ldots,t_m$ with $\bigcup_{i\in[m]}\operatorname*{free}(t_i)=\emptyset$.
\end{itemize}
By definition, $\operatorname{S} ({0}, {0}, {0})$ consists of exactly these atomic sentences, and $\operatorname{S} ({0}, {k}, {d})=\operatorname{S} ({0}, {0}, {0})$ for all $k,d\in\mathbb{N}$.
This proves the assertion of the lemma for the particular case that $q=0$.
\medskip

For the induction step from $q$ to $q{+}1$, consider arbitrary $q,d\in\mathbb{N}$. The induction hypothesis states that the assertion of the lemma holds for $\operatorname{S} ({q}, {k'}, {d})$ for all $k'\in\mathbb{N}$. Now consider an arbitrary $k\in\mathbb{N}$ and an arbitrary formula $\varphi\in\operatorname{S} ({q{+}1}, {k}, {d})$, and let $k'\coloneqq k{+}\wmaxar$.
\\
We have to show that $\varphi$ is
\begin{enumerate}[(a)]
\item\label{item:Sqkd:goal:a}
  an atomic sentence in $\operatorname{S} ({0}, {0}, {0})$, or
  \item\label{item:Sqkd:goal:b}
  a local aggregation sentence for $\ensuremath{\ensuremath{\@empty{F}}(q{+}1,k,d)}$ of inner radius as most
  $\operatorname{r} ({q}, {k'}, {d})$, or
\item\label{item:Sqkd:goal:c}
  a basic local sentence for $\ensuremath{\ensuremath{\@empty{F}}(q{+}1,k,d)}$ of
  inner radius at most $\operatorname{r} ({q}, {k'}, {d})$,
  of width at most $k{+}1{+}q\wmaxar$,
  and
  of inner distance at most $\operatorname{r} ({q{+}1}, {k}, {d})+2\operatorname{r} ({q}, {k'}, {d})$.
\end{enumerate}

According to the definition of $\operatorname{S} ({q{+}1}, {k}, {d})$, we know that one of the following cases applies:
\begin{enumerate}
\item\label{item:Sqkd:one}
  $\varphi$ is a sentence in $\operatorname{S} ({q}, {k'}, {d})$.
\item\label{item:Sqkd:two}
  $\varphi$ is a sentence of the form
\[
    \exists y_1\cdots\exists y_\ell\ \Big(
      \Und_{1 \leqslant j < j' \leqslant \ell} \operatorname{dist}(y_j,y_{j'}) > 2(2c{+}1)r' \mathbin{\mathchoice{\ }{}{}{} \land \mathchoice{\ }{}{}{}}
      \Und_{j \in [\ell]} \lambda(y_j)
    \ \Big)
\]
  with \(\ell \in [k{+}1]\), $\ell$
  variables $y_1,\ldots,y_\ell$,
  \(c \in [0,k]\),
  \(r' = \operatorname{r} ({q}, {k'}, {d})\),
  and \(\lambda \paren{x_1} \in \operatorname{L} ({q}, {k'}, {d})\).
\item\label{item:Sqkd:three}
  $\varphi$ is a sentence of the form
\[
\big(
  s = \sum \ensuremath{\mathtt{w}}(y_1,\ldots,y_\ell).\lambda
\big),
\]
where
$\ensuremath{\mathtt{w}}\in\ensuremath{\mathbf{W}}$,
$\ell=\operatorname*{ar}(\ensuremath{\mathtt{w}})\geqslant 1$,
the range \(S\) of \(\ensuremath{\mathtt{w}}\) is finite,
$s\in S$,
$y_1,\ldots,y_\ell$ are $\ell$
distinct variables,
and
$\lambda(y_1,\ldots,y_\ell)\in\operatorname{L} ({q}, {k'}, {d})$.
\end{enumerate}
\medskip

\noindent
In \textbf{Case~\ref{item:Sqkd:one}}, we can apply the induction hypothesis and obtain that $\varphi$ is
\begin{enumerate}[(i)]
 \item\label{item:Sqkd:IH:i}
   an atomic sentence in $\operatorname{S} ({0}, {0}, {0})$, or
 \item\label{item:Sqkd:IH:ii}
   a local aggregation sentence for $\ensuremath{\ensuremath{\@empty{F}}(q,k',d)}$ of inner radius at most
   $\operatorname{r} ({q{-}1}, {k'{+}\wmaxar}, {d})$, or
 \item\label{item:Sqkd:IH:iii}
   a basic local sentence for $\ensuremath{\ensuremath{\@empty{F}}(q,k',d)}$ of inner radius at most
   $\operatorname{r} ({q{-}1}, {k'{+}\wmaxar}, {d})$, of width at most $k'{+}1+(q{-}1)\wmaxar$, and of inner distance at most $\operatorname{r} ({q}, {k'}, {d})+2\operatorname{r} ({q{-}1}, {k'{+}\wmaxar}, {d})$.
\end{enumerate}

\noindent
In case~\eqref{item:Sqkd:IH:i}, statement~\eqref{item:Sqkd:goal:a} holds and we are done.
\medskip
\\
In case~\eqref{item:Sqkd:IH:ii}, $\varphi$ is of the form $\big(s=\sum\ensuremath{\mathtt{w}}(\bar y).\lambda\big)$, where $\ensuremath{\mathtt{w}}\in\ensuremath{\mathbf{W}}$, $\ell=\operatorname*{ar}(\ensuremath{\mathtt{w}})\geqslant 1$, the range $S$ of $\ensuremath{\mathtt{w}}$ is finite, $s\in S$, $\bar y=(y_1,\ldots,y_\ell)$ is a tuple of $\ell$ pairwise distinct variables, and $\lambda(\bar y)\in\ensuremath{\ensuremath{\@empty{F}}(q{-}1,k'{+}\wmaxar,d)}$ is $\tilde{r}$-local for some $\tilde{r}\leqslant \operatorname{r} ({q{-}1}, {k'{+}\wmaxar}, {d})$.
Since $\lambda(\bar y)$ has at most $\ell\leqslant\wmaxar\leqslant k'$ free variables, we obtain that $\lambda(\bar y)\in \ensuremath{\ensuremath{\@empty{F}}(q,k',d)}$. This implies that $\varphi$ is a local aggregation sentence for $\ensuremath{\ensuremath{\@empty{F}}(q{+}1,k,d)}$. Its inner radius is at most $\tilde{r}\leqslant \operatorname{r} ({q{-}1}, {k'{+}\wmaxar}, {d})\leqslant 4k'\operatorname{r} ({q{-}1}, {k'{+}\wmaxar}, {d}) = \operatorname{r} ({q}, {k'}, {d})$. Thus, statement \eqref{item:Sqkd:goal:b} holds and we are done.
\medskip
\\
In case~\eqref{item:Sqkd:IH:iii}, $\varphi$ is of the form
$\exists y_1\cdots\exists y_\ell\ \Big(\bigwedge_{1\le i<j\le\ell}
\operatorname{dist}(y_i,y_j) \,{>}\, 2r\ \wedge\ \bigwedge_{j\in [\ell]}\lambda(y_j)\Big)$,
where $\ell\geqslant 1$, $y_1,\ldots,y_\ell$ are $\ell$
variables, $r\geqslant \tilde{r}\geqslant 0$,
$\lambda(x_1)\in\ensuremath{\ensuremath{\@empty{F}}(q{-}1,k'{+}\wmaxar,d)}$ is
\emph{$\tilde{r}$-local} and has only one free variable, and
$\ell\leqslant k'{+}1+(q{-}1)\wmaxar$, $\tilde{r}\leqslant \operatorname{r} ({q{-}1}, {k'{+}\wmaxar}, {d})$, and $2r\leqslant \operatorname{r} ({q}, {k'}, {d})+2\operatorname{r} ({q{-}1}, {k'{+}\wmaxar}, {d})$.
Since $\lambda(x_1)$ has only one free variable and $k'\geqslant \wmaxar\geqslant 1$, we obtain that $\lambda(x_1)\in\ensuremath{\ensuremath{\@empty{F}}(q,k',d)}$. This implies that $\varphi$ is a basic local sentence for $\ensuremath{\ensuremath{\@empty{F}}(q{+}1,k,d)}$.
\\
Its inner radius is at most $\tilde{r}\leqslant \operatorname{r} ({q{-}1}, {k'{+}\wmaxar}, {d})\leqslant 4k'\operatorname{r} ({q{-}1}, {k'{+}\wmaxar}, {d}) = \operatorname{r} ({q}, {k'}, {d})$.
\\
Its width is $\ell \leqslant k'{+}1+(q{-}1)\wmaxar= k{+}1+q\wmaxar$.
\\
Its inner distance is at most $\operatorname{r} ({q}, {k'}, {d})+2\operatorname{r} ({q{-}1}, {k'{+}\wmaxar}, {d})$.
Combining this with the following claim
proves that statement  \eqref{item:Sqkd:goal:c} holds.

\begin{claim}
$\operatorname{r} ({q}, {k'}, {d})+2\operatorname{r} ({q{-}1}, {k'{+}\wmaxar}, {d})
~ \leqslant ~
\operatorname{r} ({q{+}1}, {k}, {d})+2\operatorname{r} ({q}, {k'}, {d})$.
\end{claim}
\begin{claimproof}
We show a stronger statement, namely,  $\operatorname{r} ({q}, {k'}, {d})+2\operatorname{r} ({q{-}1}, {k'{+}\wmaxar}, {d})
~ \leqslant ~
2\operatorname{r} ({q}, {k'}, {d})$, as follows:
\[
 \begin{array}{rcl}
  2\operatorname{r} ({q}, {k'}, {d})
  ~ = ~
  \operatorname{r} ({q}, {k'}, {d}) + \operatorname{r} ({q}, {k'}, {d})
  & \stackrel{\eqref{eq:def:radius_function}}{=} &
                                                   \operatorname{r} ({q}, {k'}, {d}) + 4k'\operatorname{r} ({q{-}1}, {k'{+}\wmaxar}, {d})
\\
  & \stackrel{k'\geqslant 1}{\geqslant} &
  \operatorname{r} ({q}, {k'}, {d}) + 2\operatorname{r} ({q{-}1}, {k'{+}\wmaxar}, {d}).
\end{array}
\]
\end{claimproof}
In summary, we obtain that statement \eqref{item:Sqkd:goal:c} holds, and we are done with \textbf{Case~\ref{item:Sqkd:one}}.
\bigskip

\noindent
In \textbf{Case~\ref{item:Sqkd:two}}, we obtain from \cref{lemma:LocalityLemma} that $\lambda(x_1)$ belongs to $\ensuremath{\ensuremath{\@empty{F}}(q,k',d)}$ and is \(r'\)-local for $r'=\operatorname{r} ({q}, {k'}, {d})$. This implies that
$\varphi$ is a basic local sentence for $\ensuremath{\ensuremath{\@empty{F}}(q{+}1,k,d)}$ of inner radius at most
$r'$. Furthermore, its width is $\ell\leqslant k{+}1\leqslant k{+}1{+}q\wmaxar$, and its inner distance is $2(2c{+}1)r'\leqslant 2(2k{+}1)r' = 4kr'+2r' = \operatorname{r} ({q{+}1}, {k}, {d})+2\operatorname{r} ({q}, {k'}, {d})$.
Hence, statement \eqref{item:Sqkd:goal:c} holds, and we are done.
\bigskip

\noindent
In \textbf{Case~\ref{item:Sqkd:three}}, we obtain from \cref{lemma:LocalityLemma} that $\lambda(\bar y)$ belongs to $\ensuremath{\ensuremath{\@empty{F}}(q,k',d)}$ and is \(r'\)-local for $r'=\operatorname{r} ({q}, {k'}, {d})$. This implies that
$\varphi$ is a local aggregation sentence for $\ensuremath{\ensuremath{\@empty{F}}(q{+}1,k,d)}$ of inner radius at most
$r'$.
Hence, statement \eqref{item:Sqkd:goal:b} holds, and we are done.
\bigskip

\noindent
This completes the proof of \cref{lemma:PropertiesOfSqkd}.
<!-- end proof-4 -->
<!-- claim-7: body_md -->
$\operatorname{r} ({q}, {k'}, {d})+2\operatorname{r} ({q{-}1}, {k'{+}\wmaxar}, {d})
~ \leqslant ~
\operatorname{r} ({q{+}1}, {k}, {d})+2\operatorname{r} ({q}, {k'}, {d})$.
<!-- end claim-7 -->
<!-- lemma-5: body_md -->
\label{lemma:fv_mux}
  Let \(q,\myk,d \in \mathbb{N}\) with \(\myk \geqslant 2\).
Let \(\vec{x} \coloneqq (x_1,\ldots,x_{\myk})\),
\(r' \coloneqq \operatorname{r} ({q}, {\myk}, {d})\),
\(K \subseteq \brac{\myk}\),
and \(I \subseteq K\) with \(\emptyset \neq I \neq K\).
For every finite and non-empty set \(\Delta\) of pairs of formulas
  \(\big(\alpha\paren{{\myvec{x}}_{I}},\beta\paren{{\myvec{x}}_{K \setminus I}}\big)\)
  such that \(\alpha\paren{{\myvec{x}}_{I}},
  \beta\paren{{\myvec{x}}_{K \setminus I}}\in \operatorname{L} ({q}, {\myk}, {d})\),
  there exists a finite and non-empty set $\tildeDelta$ of pairs of
  formulas
  \(\big(\tildealpha\paren{{\myvec{x}}_{I}},\tildebeta\paren{{\myvec{x}}_{K \setminus I}}\big)\)
  such that \(\tildealpha\paren{{\myvec{x}}_{I}},
  \tildebeta\paren{{\myvec{x}}_{K \setminus I}} \in
  \operatorname{L} ({q}, {\myk}, {d})\),
  they are Boolean combinations
  of formulas occurring in $\Delta$,
  \begin{equation}\label{eq:lemma:mux}
    \begin{array}{ll}
      & \displaystyle
    \Oder_{(\alpha,\beta) \in \Delta}
   \paren[\Big]{
      \alpha \paren{{\myvec{x}}_{I}}
      \und
      \beta \paren{{\myvec{x}}_{K \setminus I}}
      }
     \mathbin{\mathchoice{\ }{}{}{} \land \mathchoice{\ }{}{}{}}
     \operatorname{dist}({\myvec{x}}_{I};{\myvec{x}}_{K \setminus I}) \,{>}\, r'
     \\ \equiv & \displaystyle
        \Oder_{(\tildealpha,\tildebeta) \in \tildeDelta}
   \paren[\Big]{
      \tildealpha \paren{{\myvec{x}}_{I}}
      \und
      \tildebeta \paren{{\myvec{x}}_{K \setminus I}}
      }
     \mathbin{\mathchoice{\ }{}{}{} \land \mathchoice{\ }{}{}{}}
                 \operatorname{dist}({\myvec{x}}_{I};{\myvec{x}}_{K \setminus I}) \,{>}\, r'\,,
    \end{array}
  \end{equation}
  and the first entries in $\tildeDelta$ are \emph{mutually exclusive},
  i.e., for every two distinct $(\tildealpha,\tildebeta)$ and $(\tildealphaStrich,\tildebetaStrich)$
  in $\tildeDelta$, the formula $(\tildealpha\und\tildealphaStrich)$ is unsatisfiable.
  Furthermore, there is an algorithm that, given \(\Delta\), computes \(\tildeDelta\).

  Analogously, we can also compute a $\tildeDelta$ for which
  \eqref{eq:lemma:mux} holds, but where, instead of the first entries,
  the second entries in $\tildeDelta$ are mutually exclusive, i.e.,
  for every two distinct $(\tildealpha,\tildebeta)$ and $(\tildealphaStrich,\tildebetaStrich)$
  in $\tildeDelta$, the formula $(\tildebeta\und\tildebetaStrich)$ is unsatisfiable.
<!-- end lemma-5 -->
<!-- proof-5: body_md -->
Let $\myAlphas\coloneqq\ensuremath{\ensuremath{\{\alpha : \text{there exists a } \beta \text{ such that }
    (\alpha,\beta)\in \Delta\}}}$. For every $\alpha\in\myAlphas$, let
  $\myBetas(\alpha)\coloneqq\ensuremath{\ensuremath{\{\beta : (\alpha,\beta)\in\Delta\}}}$.
  For every $J\subseteq\myAlphas$, let
  \[
    \alpha_J\ \coloneqq \ \Und_{\alpha\in J}\alpha \ \und  \Und_{\alpha\in
      \myAlphas\setminus J}\nicht\alpha
    \qquad\text{and}\qquad
    \beta_J \ \coloneqq \ \Oder_{\alpha\in J}\Oder_{\beta\in\myBetas(\alpha)}\beta\,.
  \]
  Note that we have $\alpha_J(\bar{x}_I)\in\operatorname{L} ({q}, {\myk}, {d})$ for every $J\subseteq \myAlphas$;
  and for $J\neq\emptyset$, we also have
  $\beta_J(\bar{x}_{K\setminus I})\in\operatorname{L} ({q}, {\myk}, {d})$.
  Clearly, the formulas $(\alpha_J)_{J\subseteq\myAlphas}$ are
  mutually exclusive, i.e., for $J,J'\subseteq\myAlphas$ with $J\neq
  J'$, the formula $(\alpha_J\und\alpha_{J'})$ is unsatisfiable.
  Let
  \[
    \tildeDelta\ \coloneqq \ \ensuremath{\ensuremath{\{(\alpha_J,\beta_J) : J\subseteq\myAlphas,
    \ J\neq\emptyset\}}}.
  \]
  It can easily be verified that \eqref{eq:lemma:mux} is satisfied.

  The analogous statement,
  aiming at a $\tildeDelta$ where the \emph{second} entries are mutually exclusive,
  follows by symmetry.
  This completes the proof of \cref{lemma:fv_mux}.
<!-- end proof-5 -->
<!-- proof-6: body_md -->
We proceed by induction on \(q\).

   \belowdisplayshortskip 6\p@ \@plus3\p@ \@minus3\p@
   \belowdisplayskip \abovedisplayskip
   \let\@listi\@listI\@xpt{13} 
   \abovedisplayskip 10\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus5\p@
   \abovedisplayshortskip \z@ \@plus3\p@
   \belowdisplayshortskip 6\p@ \@plus3\p@ \@minus3\p@

            \parsep\z@ \@plus\p@

            \parsep\z@ \@plus\p@

            \parsep\z@ \@plus\p@

            \itemsep \parsep\@xpt{13} 
   \abovedisplayskip 10\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus5\p@
   \abovedisplayshortskip \z@ \@plus3\p@

   \belowdisplayskip \abovedisplayskip
   \let\leftmargin\leftmargini
            \parsep\z@ \@plus\p@
            \topsep 8\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus4\p@

            \parsep\z@ \@plus\p@

            \itemsep \parsep\@xpt{13} 
   \abovedisplayskip 10\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus5\p@
   \abovedisplayshortskip \z@ \@plus3\p@
   \belowdisplayshortskip 6\p@ \@plus3\p@ \@minus3\p@
   \belowdisplayskip \abovedisplayskip
   \let\leftmargin\leftmargini
            \parsep\z@ \@plus\p@
            \topsep 8\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus4\p@
            \itemsep \parsep\leftmargin\leftmargini
            \parsep\z@ \@plus\p@
            \topsep 8\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus4\p@
            \itemsep \parsep\@xpt{13} 
   \abovedisplayskip 10\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus5\p@
   \abovedisplayshortskip \z@ \@plus3\p@
   \belowdisplayshortskip 6\p@ \@plus3\p@ \@minus3\p@
   \belowdisplayskip \abovedisplayskip
   \let\leftmargin\leftmargini
            \parsep\z@ \@plus\p@
            \topsep 8\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus4\p@
            \itemsep \parsep\leftmargin\leftmargini
            \parsep\z@ \@plus\p@
            \topsep 8\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus4\p@
            \itemsep \parsep\bfseries}{Base Cases.\ }
For the induction base with \(q = 0\),
consider arbitrary \(\myk,d \in \mathbb{N}\) with \(\myk \geqslant 2\). Note that
$r'\coloneqq \operatorname{r} ({0}, {k'}, {d})\stackrel{\eqref{eq:def:radius_function}}{=}\max\ensuremath{\{d,1\}}$.
Let $\bar x\coloneqq(x_1,\ldots,x_{k'})$, and consider arbitrary $K,I$
such that $K\subseteq [k']$, $I\subseteq K$, and $\emptyset\neq I\neq K$.
Note that any formula $\lambda(\bar x_K)\in
 \operatorname{L} ({0}, {\myk}, {d})=\ensuremath{\ensuremath{\@empty{F}}(0,\myk,d)}$
is a Boolean combination of \emph{atomic} \(\ensuremath{\ensuremath{\ensuremath{\@empty{ngFOW}}}^+}\) formulas $\psi$
with \(\freeS{\psi} \subseteq\ensuremath{\ensuremath{\{x_j : j\in K\}}} \)
and \(\operatorname*{md} \paren{{\psi}} \leqslant d\).
We proceed by induction on the construction of \(\lambda(\bar x_K)\).


\begin{enumerate}[{Case} 1]
  \item
  \label{case:fv_decomp:base_case:all_free_vars_in_component}
  If \(\lambda\) is an atomic \(\ensuremath{\ensuremath{\ensuremath{\@empty{ngFOW}}}^+}\) formula
  with \(\operatorname*{free}(\lambda) \subseteq \ensuremath{\ensuremath{\{x_i : i \in I\}}}\),
  then we are done by letting
  \[
    \Delta
    \mathchoice{\ \; }{}{}{} \coloneqq \mathchoice{\ \; }{}{}{}
    \setS*{\paren{\lambda,\, \true}}
    .
  \]
\item\label{case:fv_decomp:base_case:all_free_vars_in_other_comp}
  Otherwise, if \(\lambda\) is an atomic \(\ensuremath{\ensuremath{\ensuremath{\@empty{ngFOW}}}^+}\) formula with
  \(\freeS{\lambda}\subseteq \ensuremath{\ensuremath{\{x_j : j \in K\setminus I\}}}\),
  then we are done by letting
  \[
    \Delta
    \mathchoice{\ \; }{}{}{} \coloneqq \mathchoice{\ \; }{}{}{}
    \setS*{\paren{\true,\, \lambda}}
  .
  \]
\end{enumerate}
Note that atoms of the form \(\true\),
 \(\false\),
 \(R \paren*{  }\) for a \(0\)-ary relation
symbol \(R \in \sigma\), or of the form
\(\big(s = \ensuremath{\mathtt{w}} \paren*{  }\big)\) for a \(0\)-ary weight symbol \(\ensuremath{\mathtt{w}} \in \ensuremath{\mathbf{W}}\)
are already dealt with in Case~\ref{case:fv_decomp:base_case:all_free_vars_in_component}.
For the remaining cases, recall that
\(r' = \max\ensuremath{\{d,1\}}\).

\begin{enumerate}[{Case} 1]
    \setcounter{enumi}{2}
\item\label{case:fv_decomp:base_case:distance_atom}
  Consider the case that \(\lambda\)
  is of the form \(\distS{}{x_i,x_j} \leqslant d'\)
  or of the form \(\distS{}{x_j,x_i} \leqslant d'\)
  with \(i \in I\) and \(j \in K\setminus I\).
Since \(\lambda \in \operatorname{L} ({0}, {\myk}, {d})\), we know that \(d' \leqslant d\).
For all \(\paren*{ \sigma, \ensuremath{\mathbf{W}} }\)-structures \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}\) and all tuples \(\vec{a} \in A^{\myk}\)
  with \(\distS{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}{{\myvec{a}}_{I};{\myvec{a}}_{K\setminus I}} > r'\),
  we have \(\distS{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}{a_{i},a_{j}} > r' \geqslant d\geqslant d'\),
so \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}\not\models\lambda (\vec{a}_K)\).
Thus, we are done by letting
  \[
    \Delta
    \mathchoice{\ \; }{}{}{} \coloneqq \mathchoice{\ \; }{}{}{}
    \setS*{\paren{\false,\ \false}}
    .
  \]
\item
  Consider the case that \(\lambda\) is of the form \(x_i {=} x_j\)
  or of the form \(x_j {=} x_i\)
  with \(i \in I\) and \(j \in K\setminus I\).
Then, for all \(\paren*{ \sigma, \ensuremath{\mathbf{W}} }\)-structures \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}\)
  and all tuples \(\vec{a} \in A^{\myk}\)
  with \(\distS{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}{{\myvec{a}}_{I};{\myvec{a}}_{K\setminus I}} > r'\),
  we have \(\distS{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}{a_{i},a_{j}} > r' \geqslant 1\),
and therefore \(a_i\neq a_j\).
  Thus, \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}\not\models\lambda (\vec{a}_K)\),
and we are done by letting
  \[
    \Delta
    \mathchoice{\ \; }{}{}{} \coloneqq \mathchoice{\ \; }{}{}{}
    \setS*{\paren{\false,\ \false}}
    .
  \]
\item
  Consider the case that \(\lambda\)
  is of the form \(R \paren*{  x_{\ell_1}, \dots, x_{\ell_m}  }\)
  with \(m = \arS{R}\geqslant 1\) and \( \ell_1, \dots, \ell_m  \in K\)
  such that there exist \(i,j \in \brac{m}\)
  with \(\ell_i \in I\) and \(\ell_j \in K\setminus I\).
Then, for all \(\paren*{ \sigma, \ensuremath{\mathbf{W}} }\)-structures \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}\)
  and all tuples \(\vec{a} \in A^{\myk}\)
  with \(\distS{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}{{\myvec{a}}_{I};{\myvec{a}}_{K\setminus I}} > r'\),
  we have \(\distS{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}{a_{\ell_i},a_{\ell_j}} > r' \geqslant 1\),
and therefore \((a_{\ell_1},\ldots,a_{\ell_m})\not \in R^{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}\).
Hence,
  \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \not\models \lambda (\vec{a}_K)\),
and we are done by letting
  \[
    \Delta
    \mathchoice{\ \; }{}{}{} \coloneqq \mathchoice{\ \; }{}{}{}
    \setS*{\paren{\false,\ \false}}
    .
  \]
\item
  Consider the case that \(\lambda\)
  is of the form \(\big(s = \ensuremath{\mathtt{w}} \paren*{  x_{\ell_1}, \dots, x_{\ell_m}  }\big)\)
  with
  \(\ensuremath{\mathtt{w}} \in \ensuremath{\mathbf{W}}\),
  \(S = \wtypeS{\ensuremath{\mathtt{w}}}\),
  \(s \in S\),
  \(m = \arS{\ensuremath{\mathtt{w}}}\),
  and \( \ell_1, \dots, \ell_m  \in K\)
  such that there exist \(i,j \in \brac{m}\)
  with \(\ell_i \in I\) and \(\ell_j \in K\setminus I\).
Then, for all \(\paren*{ \sigma, \ensuremath{\mathbf{W}} }\)-structures \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}\)
  and all tuples \(\vec{a} \in A^{\myk}\)
  with \(\distS{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}{{\myvec{a}}_{I};{\myvec{a}}_{K\setminus I}} > r'\),
  we have \(\distS{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}{a_{\ell_i},a_{\ell_j}} > r' \geqslant 1\).
Hence,
  by the locality condition on weighted structures,
  we have \(\ensuremath{\mathtt{w}}^{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}} \paren*{  a_{\ell_1}, \dots, a_{\ell_m}  } = 0_S\).
Thus,
  \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \models \lambda (\vec{a}_K)\) $\iff$ \(s = 0_S\),
and we are done by letting
  \[
    \Delta
    \mathchoice{\ \; }{}{}{} \coloneqq \mathchoice{\ \; }{}{}{}
    \setS*{\paren{\true,\ \true}}
    ~~
    \text{if } s = 0_S, \qquad
    \text{and}
    ~~
    \Delta
    \mathchoice{\ \; }{}{}{} \coloneqq \mathchoice{\ \; }{}{}{}
    \setS*{\paren{\false,\ \false}}
    ~~
    \text{otherwise.}
  \]
\item Consider the case that \(\lambda\)
    is of the form
    \[
      \paren[\Big]{
        \ensuremath{\@empty{P}} \paren*{  t_1, \dots, t_m  }
        \land\textstyle
        \bigwedge_{y \in \bigcup_{\nu=1}^m \freeS{t_\nu} \setminus \ensuremath{\{z\}}}
        \distS{}{z, y} \leqslant d'
      }
    \]
    with
    \(\ensuremath{\@empty{P}} \in \ensuremath{\mathbb{P}}\),
    \(m = \arS{\ensuremath{\@empty{P}}}\),
    \(\mathbb{S}\)-terms \( t_1, \dots, t_m \)
    with \(\ptypeS{\ensuremath{\@empty{P}}} = \paren*{  \wtypeS{t_1}, \dots, \wtypeS{t_m}  }\),
    $d'\leqslant d$,
    $z\in\ensuremath{\textsf{\upshape vars}}$,
    $\operatorname*{free}(\lambda)\subseteq\ensuremath{\ensuremath{\{x_j : j\in K\}}}$, and there exist $i\in I$ and $j\in K\setminus I$
    such that $x_i,x_j\in\operatorname*{free}(\lambda)$.
In particular, \(\left\lvert\operatorname*{free}(\lambda)\right\rvert \geqslant 2\), and this implies that the
    conjunction over the distance atoms actually is present in
    $\lambda$, and hence $z\in\operatorname*{free}(\lambda)$.
    As \(z \in \operatorname*{free}(\lambda)
    \subseteq \ensuremath{\ensuremath{\{x_\nu : \nu \in I\}}} \uplus \ensuremath{\ensuremath{\{x_\nu : \nu \in K \setminus I\}}}\),
    we can assume w.l.o.g.\ that \(z = x_i\) or \(z = x_j\).
    Thus, we either have
    \(x_i = z\) and \(x_j \in \bigcup_{\nu=1}^m\operatorname*{free}(t_\nu) \setminus \ensuremath{\{z\}}\),
    or \(x_j = z\) and \(x_i \in \bigcup_{\nu=1}^m\operatorname*{free}(t_\nu)\setminus\ensuremath{\{z\}}\).
Note that, for all \(\paren*{ \sigma, \ensuremath{\mathbf{W}} }\)-structures \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}\)
    and all tuples \(\bar{a} \in A^{\myk}\)
    with \(\distS{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}{{\myvec{a}}_{I};{\myvec{a}}_{K\setminus I}} > r'\),
    we have \(\distS{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}{a_{i},a_{j}} > r' \geqslant d \geqslant d'\).
    Hence, \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \not\models \lambda(\bar{a}_K)\),
    and we are done by letting
    \[
      \Delta
      \mathchoice{\ \; }{}{}{} \coloneqq \mathchoice{\ \; }{}{}{}
      \setS*{\paren{\false,\ \false}}.
    \]
\end{enumerate}
So far, we have proved that the assertion of the lemma holds for all
\emph{atomic} formulas in \(\operatorname{L} ({0}, {\myk}, {d})\).
To handle Boolean combinations,
it suffices to consider disjunction and negation.


\begin{enumerate}[{Case} 1]
    \setcounter{enumi}{7}
  \item\label{case:proof:fv_decomp:base_case:disjunction}
  Consider the case that \(\lambda\) is of the form \((\lambda_1\oder\lambda_2)\).
By the induction hypothesis, for each
  \(\ell \in \ensuremath{\{1,2\}}\), we obtain a finite and non-empty set \(\Delta_\ell\) of pairs of
  formulas \(\paren{\alpha(\bar x_I),\beta(\bar x_{K\setminus I})}\)
  such that \(\alpha(\bar x_I), \beta(\bar x_{K\setminus I}) \in \operatorname{L} ({q}, {\myk}, {d})\),
  their quantifier rank is at most $\operatorname*{qr}(\lambda_\ell)$, and
  \[
    \lambda_\ell\paren{\vec{x}_K}
    \mathbin{\mathchoice{\ }{}{}{} \land \mathchoice{\ }{}{}{}}
    \distS{}{{\myvec{x}}_{I};{\myvec{x}}_{K\setminus I}} > r'
    \mathchoice{\ \; }{}{}{} \equiv \mathchoice{\ \; }{}{}{}
    
    \hspace{0.75 em}
    \bigvee_{
        \mathclap{
            \substack{
                \paren{\alpha,\beta} \in \Delta_\ell
            }
        }
    }
    \hspace{0.75 em}

    \paren[\Big]{
      \alpha\paren{{\myvec{x}}_{I}} \und \beta\paren{{\myvec{x}}_{K\setminus I}}
    }
    \mathbin{\mathchoice{\ }{}{}{} \land \mathchoice{\ }{}{}{}}
    \distS{}{{\myvec{x}}_{I};{\myvec{x}}_{K\setminus I}} > r'.
  \]
  It can easily be verified that we are done by choosing
  \(\Delta \mathchoice{\ \; }{}{}{} \coloneqq \mathchoice{\ \; }{}{}{} \Delta_1\cup\Delta_2\) and afterwards applying
  Lemma~\ref{lemma:fv_mux} to turn $\Delta$ into a $\tildeDelta$ where the
  first entries in $\tildeDelta$ are mutually exclusive.\bigskip

\item
\label{case:proof:fv_decomp:base_case:negation}
Next, consider the case that \(\lambda\) is of the form
\(\nicht\lambda'\).
By the induction hypothesis, we obtain a finite and non-empty set
\(\Delta'\) of pairs of formulas \(\paren{\alpha(\bar x_I),\beta(\bar
  x_{K\setminus I})}\) such that
\(\alpha(\bar x_I), \beta(\bar x_{K\setminus I}) \in \operatorname{L} ({q}, {\myk}, {d})\),
their quantifier rank is at most $\operatorname*{qr}(\lambda')$, and
\begin{equation}
  \label{eq:proof:fv_decomp:base_case:negation}
  \begin{array}{ll}
 & \lambda'\paren{\vec{x}_K}
  ~ \mathbin{\mathchoice{\ }{}{}{} \land \mathchoice{\ }{}{}{}} ~
  \distS{}{{\myvec{x}}_{I};{\myvec{x}}_{K\setminus I}} \,{>}\, r'
  \medskip\\ \mathchoice{\ \; }{}{}{} \equiv \mathchoice{\ \; }{}{}{} & \displaystyle
  \Oder_{\paren{\alpha,\beta} \in \Delta'}
  \paren[\Big]{
    \alpha\paren{{\myvec{x}}_{I}} \und \beta\paren{{\myvec{x}}_{K\setminus I}}
  }
  \mathbin{\mathchoice{\ }{}{}{} \land \mathchoice{\ }{}{}{}}
  \distS{}{{\myvec{x}}_{I};{\myvec{x}}_{K\setminus I}} > r'.
 \end{array}
\end{equation}
Let \(m \mathchoice{\ \; }{}{}{} \coloneqq \mathchoice{\ \; }{}{}{} |\Delta'|\)
and let \((\alpha_1,\beta_1),\ldots,(\alpha_m,\beta_m)\)
be an enumeration of the elements in \(\Delta'\).
For every set \(H\subseteq\brac{m}\) with \(\emptyset\neq H\neq \brac{m}\) let

\newcommand{0.75 em}{0.75 em}
\begin{alignat*}{5}
  \alpha_H
  &\mathchoice{\ \; }{}{}{} \coloneqq \mathchoice{\ \; }{}{}{}
  
    \hspace{0.75 em}
    \bigwedge_{
        \mathclap{
            \substack{
                h \in H
            }
        }
    }
    \hspace{0.75 em}
 \nicht \alpha_h
  \qquad && \text{and} \qquad
  &
  \beta_H
  & \mathchoice{\ \; }{}{}{} \coloneqq \mathchoice{\ \; }{}{}{}
  
    \hspace{0.75 em}
    \bigwedge_{
        \mathclap{
            \substack{
                h' \in \brac{m}\setminus H
            }
        }
    }
    \hspace{0.75 em}
 \nicht\beta_{h'}
  .
\intertext{We let
        }
  \alpha_\emptyset
  & \mathchoice{\ \; }{}{}{} \coloneqq \mathchoice{\ \; }{}{}{}
        \true
  \qquad &&\text{and} \qquad
  &
  \beta_\emptyset
  & \mathchoice{\ \; }{}{}{} \coloneqq \mathchoice{\ \; }{}{}{}
  
    \hspace{0.75 em}
    \bigwedge_{
        \mathclap{
            \substack{
                h' \in \brac{m}
            }
        }
    }
    \hspace{0.75 em}
 \nicht \beta_{h'}
\shortintertext{and}
\alpha_{\brac{m}}
  & \mathchoice{\ \; }{}{}{} \coloneqq \mathchoice{\ \; }{}{}{}
  
    \hspace{0.75 em}
    \bigwedge_{
        \mathclap{
            \substack{
                h \in \brac{m}
            }
        }
    }
    \hspace{0.75 em}
 \nicht \alpha_h
  \qquad &&\text{and} \qquad
  &
  \beta_{\brac{m}}
  & \mathchoice{\ \; }{}{}{} \coloneqq \mathchoice{\ \; }{}{}{}
        \true
  .
\shortintertext{We let}
\Delta
  & \mathchoice{\ \; }{}{}{} \coloneqq \mathchoice{\ \; }{}{}{}
  \mathrlap{
    \ensuremath{\ensuremath{\{\paren{\alpha_H,\, \beta_H} :  H \subseteq \brac{m}\}}}
    .
  }
\end{alignat*}
Then, \(\Delta\) is a finite, non-empty set of pairs of formulas
\((\alpha_H,\beta_H)\) such that \(\alpha_H(\bar x_I) \in \operatorname{L} ({q}, {\myk}, {d})\)
and \(\beta_H(\bar x_{K\setminus I}) \in \operatorname{L} ({q}, {\myk}, {d})\).
Consider an arbitrary \(\paren*{ \sigma, \ensuremath{\mathbf{W}} }\)-structure \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}\)
and a tuple \(\vec{a} \in A^{\myk}\)
such that \(\distS{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}{{\myvec{a}}_{I};{\myvec{a}}_{K\setminus I}} > r'\).
We have to show that
\[
  \ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \models \lambda (\vec{a}_K)
  ~~ \iff ~~
  \text{there is a
  \(H\subseteq \brac{m}\) such that \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \models \alpha_H({\myvec{a}}_{I})\) and
  \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \models \beta_H({\myvec{a}}_{K\setminus I})\)}.
\]
Note that the following holds.
\[
\begin{array}{rcl}
     \ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \models \lambda (\vec{a}_K)
  & \iff
  & \ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}\not\models\lambda' (\vec{a}_K)\\
  & \stackrel{\eqref{eq:proof:fv_decomp:base_case:negation}}{\iff}
  & \text{for all } h \in \brac{m}
  ~ : ~
  \ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}\not\models\alpha_h({\myvec{a}}_{I})
  \text{ \ or \ }
  \ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}\not\models\beta_h({\myvec{a}}_{K\setminus I})
  .
\end{array}
\]
Therefore, it suffices to show that the following statements are
equivalent.
\begin{romanenumerate}
  \item
  \label{itm:proof:fv_decomp:base_case:negation:existence}
  There is an
  \(H\subseteq \brac{m}\) such that \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \models \alpha_H({\myvec{a}}_{I})\)
  and \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \models \beta_H({\myvec{a}}_{K\setminus I})\).

  \item
  \label{itm:proof:fv_decomp:base_case:negation:universality}
  For all \(h \in \brac{m}\)
  we have \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}\not\models\alpha_h({\myvec{a}}_{I})\)
  or \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}\not\models\beta_h({\myvec{a}}_{K\setminus I})\).
\end{romanenumerate}
For the direction
\enquote{(i)\(\Rightarrow\)(ii)},
consider an arbitrary \(h \in \brac{m}\).
If \(h \in H\),
then, from \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \models \alpha_H({\myvec{a}}_{I})\),
we obtain that \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}\not\models\alpha_h({\myvec{a}}_{I})\).
If \(h\not \in H\),
then, from \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \models \beta_H({\myvec{a}}_{K\setminus I})\),
we obtain that \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}\not\models\beta_h({\myvec{a}}_{K\setminus I})\).
Hence, (ii) is satisfied.

For the direction \enquote{(ii)\(\Rightarrow\)(i)},
let \(H \mathchoice{\ \; }{}{}{} \coloneqq \mathchoice{\ \; }{}{}{} \ensuremath{\ensuremath{\{h \in \brac{m} : \ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}\not\models\alpha_h({\myvec{a}}_{I})\}}}\).
Then, \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \models \alpha_H({\myvec{a}}_{I})\).
Furthermore, for every \(h' \in \brac{m}\setminus H\)
we have \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}\not\models\beta_{h'}(\vec{a}_{K\setminus I})\).
Thus, \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \models \beta_H({\myvec{a}}_{K\setminus I})\).
Hence, \(H\) witnesses that (i) is satisfied.

This proves that the statements (i) and (ii) are equivalent.
\smallskip

Applying \cref{lemma:fv_mux}, we can turn $\Delta$ into a
$\tildeDelta$ where the first entries in $\tildeDelta$ are mutually exclusive.
This completes the case that \(\lambda\) is of the form \(\nicht\lambda'\).
\end{enumerate}

In summary, we have finished the induction base for \(q = 0\).

   \belowdisplayshortskip 6\p@ \@plus3\p@ \@minus3\p@
   \belowdisplayskip \abovedisplayskip
   \let\@listi\@listI\@xpt{13} 
   \abovedisplayskip 10\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus5\p@
   \abovedisplayshortskip \z@ \@plus3\p@
   \belowdisplayshortskip 6\p@ \@plus3\p@ \@minus3\p@
   \belowdisplayskip \abovedisplayskip
   \let\leftmargin\leftmargini
            \parsep\z@ \@plus\p@
            \topsep 8\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus4\p@
            \itemsep \parsep\@listi\@xpt{13} 
   \abovedisplayskip 10\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus5\p@
   \abovedisplayshortskip \z@ \@plus3\p@
   \belowdisplayshortskip 6\p@ \@plus3\p@ \@minus3\p@
   \belowdisplayskip \abovedisplayskip
   \let\leftmargin\leftmargini
            \parsep\z@ \@plus\p@
            \topsep 8\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus4\p@
            \itemsep \parsep\leftmargin\leftmargini
            \parsep\z@ \@plus\p@
            \topsep 8\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus4\p@
            \itemsep \parsep\@xpt{13} 
   \abovedisplayskip 10\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus5\p@
   \abovedisplayshortskip \z@ \@plus3\p@
   \belowdisplayshortskip 6\p@ \@plus3\p@ \@minus3\p@
   \belowdisplayskip \abovedisplayskip
   \let\leftmargin\leftmargini
            \parsep\z@ \@plus\p@
            \topsep 8\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus4\p@
            \itemsep \parsep\leftmargin\leftmargini
            \parsep\z@ \@plus\p@
            \topsep 8\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus4\p@
            \itemsep \parsep\@xpt{13} 
   \abovedisplayskip 10\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus5\p@
   \abovedisplayshortskip \z@ \@plus3\p@
   \belowdisplayshortskip 6\p@ \@plus3\p@ \@minus3\p@
   \belowdisplayskip \abovedisplayskip
   \let\leftmargin\leftmargini
            \parsep\z@ \@plus\p@
            \topsep 8\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus4\p@
            \itemsep \parsep\leftmargin\leftmargini
            \parsep\z@ \@plus\p@
            \topsep 8\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus4\p@
            \itemsep \parsep\@xpt{13} 
   \abovedisplayskip 10\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus5\p@
   \abovedisplayshortskip \z@ \@plus3\p@
   \belowdisplayshortskip 6\p@ \@plus3\p@ \@minus3\p@
   \belowdisplayskip \abovedisplayskip
   \let\leftmargin\leftmargini
            \parsep\z@ \@plus\p@
            \topsep 8\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus4\p@
            \itemsep \parsep\leftmargin\leftmargini
            \parsep\z@ \@plus\p@
            \topsep 8\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus4\p@
            \itemsep \parsep\bfseries}{Induction Step.\ }
For the induction step from \(q\) to \(q{+}1\), consider \(q,d \in
\mathbb{N}\).
\medskip

The \textbf{induction hypothesis} states that the assertion of
\cref{lemma:fv} holds for all $k'\geqslant 2$, for
$r'=\operatorname{r} ({q}, {k'}, {d})$, $\bar{x}'=(x_1,\ldots,x_{k'})$, and for all
$K'\subseteq[k']$ and $I'\subseteq K'$ with $\emptyset\neq I'\neq
K'$. That is, for every $\lambda(\bar x'_{K'})\in\operatorname{L} ({q}, {k'}, {d})$, we can
compute a finite and non-empty set $\Delta'$ of pairs of formulas
$\big(\alpha(\bar{x}'_{I'}),\beta(\bar{x}'_{K'\setminus I'})\big)$
such that $\alpha(\bar x'_{I'}), \beta(\bar
x'_{K'\setminus I'})\in\operatorname{L} ({q}, {k'}, {d})$, their quantifier rank is at
most $\operatorname*{qr}(\lambda)$,
\[
  \lambda\paren{{\vec{x}'}_{K'}}
  \mathbin{\mathchoice{\ }{}{}{} \land \mathchoice{\ }{}{}{}}
  \operatorname{dist}({\vec{x}'}_{I'}; {\vec{x}'}_{K' \setminus I'})> r'
  ~~ \equiv ~~
  \Oder_{\mathclap{\paren{\alpha,\beta} \in \Delta'}}
  ~
  \paren[\Big]{
    \alpha \paren{{\vec{x}'}_{I'}}
    \und
    \beta \paren{{\vec{x}'}_{K' \setminus I'}}
  }
  \mathbin{\mathchoice{\ }{}{}{} \land \mathchoice{\ }{}{}{}}
  \operatorname{dist}({\vec{x}'}_{I'}; {\vec{x}'}_{K' \setminus I'})> r'
  ,
\]
and the first entries in \(\Delta'\) are mutually exclusive.
\medskip

Now consider an arbitrary \(k \in \mathbb{N}\) with \(k \geqslant 2\),
an arbitrary \(K \subseteq \brac{k}\) with \(|K| \geqslant 2\),
and an arbitrary \(I \subseteq K\) with \(\emptyset \neq I\neq K\).
Let $\bar x=(x_1,\ldots,x_k)$.
Let
\begin{equation}\label{eq:rs_fv}
 \tilde{r} \mathchoice{\ \; }{}{}{} \coloneqq \mathchoice{\ \; }{}{}{} \operatorname{r} ({q{+}1}, {k}, {d})
  \quad \text{and}\quad
  r'  \mathchoice{\ \; }{}{}{} \coloneqq \mathchoice{\ \; }{}{}{} \operatorname{r} ({q}, {k{+}\wmaxar}, {d})
  \quad
  \text{and}
  \quad
  \hat{r} \mathchoice{\ \; }{}{}{} \coloneqq \mathchoice{\ \; }{}{}{} \tilde{r} - r'\,.
\end{equation}

Note that the formulas \(\psi(\bar x_K)\in\operatorname{L} ({q{+}1}, {k}, {d})\)
are Boolean combinations
of formulas that have free variables in \(\ensuremath{\ensuremath{\{x_j : j\in K\}}}\)
and are of one of the forms
\ref{itm:def:loc:kind_previous_rank},
\ref{itm:def:loc:kind_distance_atom},
\ref{itm:def:loc:kind_near},
or
\ref{itm:def:loc:kind_weight_aggregation}.

Our goal is to construct a finite and non-empty set $\Delta$ of pairs
of formulas $(\alpha(\bar{x}_I),\beta(\bar{x}_{K\setminus I}))$ such that
$\alpha(\bar{x}_I), \beta(\bar{x}_{K \setminus I})\in\operatorname{L} ({q{+}1}, {k}, {d})$,
their quantifier rank is at most $\operatorname*{qr}(\psi)$,
and
\begin{equation}\label{eq:fv-proof_goal_in_IndStep}
  \psi\paren{{\vec{x}}_{K}}
  \mathbin{\mathchoice{\ }{}{}{} \land \mathchoice{\ }{}{}{}}
  \operatorname{dist}({\vec{x}}_{I}; {\vec{x}}_{K \setminus I})> \tilde{r}
  ~~ \equiv ~~
  \Oder_{\mathclap{\paren{\alpha,\beta} \in \Delta}}
  ~
  \paren[\Big]{
    \alpha \paren{{\vec{x}}_{I}}
    \und
    \beta \paren{{\vec{x}}_{K \setminus I}}
  }
  \mathbin{\mathchoice{\ }{}{}{} \land \mathchoice{\ }{}{}{}}
  \operatorname{dist}({\vec{x}}_{I}; {\vec{x}}_{K \setminus I})> \tilde{r}\ ;
\end{equation}
once having achieved this, we can use \cref{lemma:fv_mux} to
turn $\Delta$ into a $\tildeDelta$ such that the first entries in
$\tildeDelta$ are mutually exclusive.
We proceed by induction on the construction of \(\psi(\bar{x}_K)\).

   \belowdisplayskip \abovedisplayskip
   \let\leftmargin\leftmargini
            \parsep\z@ \@plus\p@
            \topsep 8\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus4\p@
            \itemsep \parsep\leftmargin\leftmargini
            \parsep\z@ \@plus\p@
            \topsep 8\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus4\p@
            \itemsep \parsep\@xpt{13} 
   \abovedisplayskip 10\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus5\p@
   \abovedisplayshortskip \z@ \@plus3\p@
   \belowdisplayshortskip 6\p@ \@plus3\p@ \@minus3\p@
   \belowdisplayskip \abovedisplayskip
   \let\leftmargin\leftmargini
            \parsep\z@ \@plus\p@
            \topsep 8\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus4\p@
            \itemsep \parsep\leftmargin\leftmargini
            \parsep\z@ \@plus\p@
            \topsep 8\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus4\p@
            \itemsep \parsep\bfseries}{Case~\ref{itm:def:loc:kind_previous_rank}:}
\(\psi\) is of the form \ref{itm:def:loc:kind_previous_rank}.
Then \(\psi\) belongs to \(\operatorname{L} ({q}, {k{+}\wmaxar}, {d})\)
(with \(\freeS{\psi} \subseteq \ensuremath{\ensuremath{\{x_j : j\in K\}}}\)),
and we use the induction hypothesis
with \(\lambda\coloneqq\psi\), \(k' \mathchoice{\ \; }{}{}{} \coloneqq \mathchoice{\ \; }{}{}{} k{+}\wmaxar\),
\(K' \mathchoice{\ \; }{}{}{} \coloneqq \mathchoice{\ \; }{}{}{} \brac{k}\) and $I'\coloneqq I$ to construct a \(\Delta'\).
Setting \(\Delta\coloneqq\Delta'\) finishes this case, because
\(\tilde{r}\geqslant r'\).

   \abovedisplayshortskip \z@ \@plus3\p@
   \belowdisplayshortskip 6\p@ \@plus3\p@ \@minus3\p@
   \belowdisplayskip \abovedisplayskip
   \let\leftmargin\leftmargini
            \parsep\z@ \@plus\p@
            \topsep 8\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus4\p@
            \itemsep \parsep\leftmargin\leftmargini
            \parsep\z@ \@plus\p@
            \topsep 8\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus4\p@
            \itemsep \parsep\@xpt{13} 
   \abovedisplayskip 10\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus5\p@
   \abovedisplayshortskip \z@ \@plus3\p@
   \belowdisplayshortskip 6\p@ \@plus3\p@ \@minus3\p@
   \belowdisplayskip \abovedisplayskip
   \let\leftmargin\leftmargini
            \parsep\z@ \@plus\p@
            \topsep 8\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus4\p@
            \itemsep \parsep\leftmargin\leftmargini
            \parsep\z@ \@plus\p@
            \topsep 8\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus4\p@
            \itemsep \parsep\@xpt{13} 
   \abovedisplayskip 10\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus5\p@
   \abovedisplayshortskip \z@ \@plus3\p@
   \belowdisplayshortskip 6\p@ \@plus3\p@ \@minus3\p@
   \belowdisplayskip \abovedisplayskip
   \let\leftmargin\leftmargini
            \parsep\z@ \@plus\p@
            \topsep 8\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus4\p@
            \itemsep \parsep\leftmargin\leftmargini
            \parsep\z@ \@plus\p@
            \topsep 8\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus4\p@
            \itemsep \parsep\bfseries}{Case~\ref{itm:def:loc:kind_distance_atom}:}
\(\psi\) is of the form \ref{itm:def:loc:kind_distance_atom}.
Then it is of the form \(\distS{}{x_i, x_j} \leqslant \tilde{d}\)
with \(i, j \in K\)
and \(\tilde{d} \leqslant \tilde{r}\).
This case can be dealt with in the same way
as the distance atoms in the base case (Cases
\ref{case:fv_decomp:base_case:all_free_vars_in_component},
\ref{case:fv_decomp:base_case:all_free_vars_in_other_comp},
\ref{case:fv_decomp:base_case:distance_atom}).

\@startsection{subparagraph}{5}{\z@} 
                                       {3.25ex \@plus1ex \@minus .2ex}

                                      {\color{lipicsGray}\sffamily

   \abovedisplayskip 10\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus5\p@
   \abovedisplayshortskip \z@ \@plus3\p@
   \belowdisplayshortskip 6\p@ \@plus3\p@ \@minus3\p@
   \belowdisplayskip \abovedisplayskip
   \let\@listi\@listI\@xpt{13} 
   \abovedisplayskip 10\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus5\p@
   \abovedisplayshortskip \z@ \@plus3\p@

   \belowdisplayskip \abovedisplayskip
   \let\leftmargin\leftmargini
            \parsep\z@ \@plus\p@
            \topsep 8\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus4\p@

   \abovedisplayskip 10\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus5\p@
