   \belowdisplayskip \abovedisplayskip
   \let\leftmargin\leftmargini
            \parsep\z@ \@plus\p@
            \topsep 8\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus4\p@
            \itemsep \parsep\leftmargin\leftmargini
            \parsep\z@ \@plus\p@
            \topsep 8\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus4\p@
            \itemsep \parsep\@xpt{13} 
   \abovedisplayskip 10\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus5\p@
   \abovedisplayshortskip \z@ \@plus3\p@
   \belowdisplayshortskip 6\p@ \@plus3\p@ \@minus3\p@
   \belowdisplayskip \abovedisplayskip
   \let\leftmargin\leftmargini
            \parsep\z@ \@plus\p@
            \topsep 8\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus4\p@
            \itemsep \parsep\leftmargin\leftmargini
            \parsep\z@ \@plus\p@
            \topsep 8\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus4\p@
            \itemsep \parsep\@xpt{13} 
   \abovedisplayskip 10\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus5\p@
   \abovedisplayshortskip \z@ \@plus3\p@
   \belowdisplayshortskip 6\p@ \@plus3\p@ \@minus3\p@
   \belowdisplayskip \abovedisplayskip
   \let\leftmargin\leftmargini
            \parsep\z@ \@plus\p@
            \topsep 8\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus4\p@
            \itemsep \parsep\leftmargin\leftmargini
            \parsep\z@ \@plus\p@
            \topsep 8\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus4\p@
            \itemsep \parsep\@xpt{13} 
   \abovedisplayskip 10\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus5\p@
   \abovedisplayshortskip \z@ \@plus3\p@
   \belowdisplayshortskip 6\p@ \@plus3\p@ \@minus3\p@
   \belowdisplayskip \abovedisplayskip
   \let\leftmargin\leftmargini
            \parsep\z@ \@plus\p@
            \topsep 8\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus4\p@
            \itemsep \parsep\leftmargin\leftmargini
            \parsep\z@ \@plus\p@
            \topsep 8\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus4\p@
            \itemsep \parsep\@xpt{13} 
   \abovedisplayskip 10\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus5\p@
   \abovedisplayshortskip \z@ \@plus3\p@
   \belowdisplayshortskip 6\p@ \@plus3\p@ \@minus3\p@
   \belowdisplayskip \abovedisplayskip
   \let\leftmargin\leftmargini
            \parsep\z@ \@plus\p@
            \topsep 8\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus4\p@
            \itemsep \parsep\leftmargin\leftmargini
            \parsep\z@ \@plus\p@
            \topsep 8\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus4\p@
            \itemsep \parsep\bfseries}{Case~\ref{itm:def:loc:kind_near}:}
\(\psi\) is of the form \ref{itm:def:loc:kind_near}.
Then we have
\begin{equation*}
  \psi \paren{\bar{x}_K}
  ~ = ~
  \exists x_{k+1}\,
  \paren[\big]{
    \, \distS{}{x_i, x_{k+1}} \leqslant \hat{d}
    \, \und \,
    \lambda \paren{\bar{x}_K, x_{k+1}} \,
  }
\end{equation*}
with \(i \in K\),
\(\lambda \in \operatorname{L} ({q}, {k{+}\wmaxar}, {d})\), and $\hat{d}\leqslant\hat{r}$.
We use the induction hypothesis for \(k' \mathchoice{\ \; }{}{}{} \coloneqq \mathchoice{\ \; }{}{}{} k{+}\wmaxar\),
\(K' \mathchoice{\ \; }{}{}{} \coloneqq \mathchoice{\ \; }{}{}{} K\cup\ensuremath{\{k{+}1\}}\),
the formula \(\lambda\),
and each of the sets \(I_1 \mathchoice{\ \; }{}{}{} \coloneqq \mathchoice{\ \; }{}{}{} I\cup\ensuremath{\{k{+}1\}}\) and \(I_2 \mathchoice{\ \; }{}{}{} \coloneqq \mathchoice{\ \; }{}{}{} I\).
This yields finite, non-empty sets \(\Delta_1\) and \(\Delta_2\)
such that, for each \(\ell \in \ensuremath{\{1,2\}}\), the set \(\Delta_\ell\)
consists of pairs of formulas \(\paren{\alpha,\beta}\)
with \(\alpha \paren{{\myvec{x}}_{I_{\ell}}},
\beta \paren{{\myvec{x}}_{K' \setminus I_{\ell}}} \in \operatorname{L} ({q}, {k'}, {d})\)
and of quantifier rank $\leqslant\operatorname*{qr}(\lambda)$
such that, for \(\bar{x}' \mathchoice{\ \; }{}{}{} \coloneqq \mathchoice{\ \; }{}{}{} (x_1,\ldots,x_{k'})\), we have
\begin{equation}
  \label{eq:proof:fv_decomp:case:kind_near:indhyp_on_disjunction}
  \begin{array}{ll}
    & \lambda \paren{{\vec{x}'}_{K'}}
    \mathbin{\mathchoice{\ }{}{}{} \land \mathchoice{\ }{}{}{}}
    \operatorname{dist}({\vec{x}'}_{I_\ell}; {\vec{x}'}_{K' \setminus I_\ell}) > r'
    \smallskip\\
    \mathchoice{\ \; }{}{}{} \equiv \mathchoice{\ \; }{}{}{} & \displaystyle
    \Oder_{\mathclap{\paren{\alpha,\beta} \in \Delta_{\ell}}}
    ~
    \paren[\Big]{
      \alpha \paren{{\vec{x}'}_{I_\ell}}
      \und
      \beta \paren{{\vec{x}'}_{K' \setminus I_\ell}}
    }
    \mathbin{\mathchoice{\ }{}{}{} \land \mathchoice{\ }{}{}{}}
    \operatorname{dist}({\vec{x}'}_{I_\ell}; {\vec{x}'}_{K' \setminus I_\ell}) > r'
    .
  \end{array}
\end{equation}
We use these two index sets \(I_1\) and \(I_2\),
because they allow us to distinguish between the two cases
\(i \in I\) and \(i \in K\setminus I\).
In the first case, we add the quantified variable $x_{k+1}$ to the component indexed by \(I\),
and in the second case, to the other component.
For each \(\paren{\alpha, \beta} \in \Delta_1\),
we define
\begin{align}
  \label{eq:proof:fv_decomp:kind_near__def_alpha_close_to_first_component}
  \alpha' \paren{{\myvec{x}}_{I}}
  & \mathchoice{\ \; }{}{}{} \coloneqq \mathchoice{\ \; }{}{}{}
  \exists x_{k+1}
  \paren[\big]{
    \distS{}{x_i, x_{k+1}} \leqslant \hat{d}
    \, \und \,
    \alpha \paren{{\myvec{x}}_{I}, x_{k+1}}
  },
  \intertext{
and for each \(\paren{\alpha, \beta} \in \Delta_2\)
    we define}
  \beta' \paren{{\myvec{x}}_{K\setminus I}}
  & \mathchoice{\ \; }{}{}{} \coloneqq \mathchoice{\ \; }{}{}{}
  \exists x_{k+1}
  \paren[\big]{
    \distS{}{x_i, x_{k+1}} \leqslant \hat{d}
    \,\und\,
    \beta \paren{{\myvec{x}}_{K\setminus I},x_{k+1}}
  }
  .
\end{align}
If \(i \in I\), we let
\begin{align*}
  \Delta
  & \mathchoice{\ \; }{}{}{} \coloneqq \mathchoice{\ \; }{}{}{}
  \Delta_1'
  \mathchoice{\ \; }{}{}{} \coloneqq \mathchoice{\ \; }{}{}{}
  \setcompS[\Big]{
    \paren[\big]{
      \alpha' \paren{{\myvec{x}}_{I}}
      \;,\;
      \beta \paren{{\myvec{x}}_{K \setminus I}}
      }
  }{
    \paren{\alpha,\beta} \in \Delta_1
  }
  \, ,
\shortintertext{and otherwise we let}
\Delta
  & \mathchoice{\ \; }{}{}{} \coloneqq \mathchoice{\ \; }{}{}{}
  \Delta_2'
  \mathchoice{\ \; }{}{}{} \coloneqq \mathchoice{\ \; }{}{}{}
  \setcompS[\Big]{
    \paren[\big]{
      \alpha \paren{\vec{x}_{I}}
      \;,\;
        \beta' \paren{{\myvec{x}}_{K \setminus I}}
    }
  }{\paren{\alpha, \beta} \in \Delta_2}
  \, .
\end{align*}

For all \(\paren{\alpha, \beta} \in \Delta_1 \cup \Delta_2\),
we have \(\alpha, \beta \in \operatorname{L} ({q}, {k'}, {d})\).
Since they only have free variables in \(\setS*{ x_1, \dots, x_k }\),
by formation rule~\ref{itm:def:loc:kind_previous_rank},
they are also contained in \(\operatorname{L} ({q {+} 1}, {k}, {d})\);
and, since \(\hat{d} \leqslant \hat{r}\),
we also have \(\alpha', \beta' \in \operatorname{L} ({q {+} 1}, {k}, {d})\)
by formation rule~\ref{itm:def:loc:kind_near}.
Furthermore, it holds that
\(\qrS{\alpha'} = \qrS{\alpha} + 1 \stackrel{\text{ind.\,hyp.}}{\leqslant} \qrS{\lambda} + 1 = \qrS{\psi}\)
and
\(\qrS{\beta'} = \qrS{\beta} + 1 \stackrel{\text{ind.\,hyp.}}{\leqslant} \qrS{\lambda} + 1 = \qrS{\psi}\).
Thus, \(\Delta\) is a finite, non-empty set of pairs of formulas
\(\paren{\hat{\alpha}, \hat{\beta}}\)
with \(\hat{\alpha} \in \operatorname{L} ({q{+}1}, {k}, {d})\)
and \(\hat{\beta} \in \operatorname{L} ({q{+}1}, {k}, {d})\) and of quantifier
rank at most $\operatorname*{qr}(\psi)$.
All that remains to be done is to prove
that equivalence \eqref{eq:fv-proof_goal_in_IndStep} holds.

Consider an arbitrary \(\paren*{ \sigma, \ensuremath{\mathbf{W}} }\)-structure \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}\)
and a tuple \(\vec{a} \in A^k\)
with \(\distS{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}{{\myvec{a}}_{I};{\myvec{a}}_{K\setminus I}} > \tilde{r}\).
We want to show that
\[
  \ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}\mathchoice{\ \; }{}{}{} \models \mathchoice{\ \; }{}{}{}\psi(\bar a_K)
  ~~ \iff ~~
  \text{there is a }
  (\hat{\alpha},\hat{\beta}) \in \Delta
  \text{ such that }
  \ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \models \hat{\alpha}(\bar a_I)
  \text{ and }
  \ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \models \hat{\beta}(\bar a_{K\setminus I}).
\]
First, suppose \(i \in I\), so $\Delta=\Delta'_1$.
For the direction \enquote{\(\Longrightarrow\)}, assume that
\(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \models \psi (\vec{a}_K)\).
This implies that there exists an \(a_{k+1} \in A\)
with \(\distS{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}{a_i, a_{k+1}} \leqslant \hat{d}\)
and \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \models \lambda (\vec{a}_K, a_{k+1})\).
Then, for every \(j \in K \setminus I\),
we have
\[
  \tilde{r}\ <\ \distS{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}{a_i,a_j}\ \leqslant\ \distS{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}{a_i, a_{k+1}} + \distS{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}{a_{k+1}, a_j}
  \ \leqslant\ \hat{d} + \distS{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}{a_{k+1}, a_j}.
\]
Thus, \(\distS{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}{a_{k+1}, a_j}\,>\,\tilde{r} - \hat{d}
\,\geqslant\,\tilde{r} - \hat{r}\,\stackrel{\eqref{eq:rs_fv}}{=}\, r'\).
Since this holds for all \(j \in K \setminus I\),
we have shown that \(\distS{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}{{\myvec{a}}_{K\setminus I}; a_{k+1}} > r'\).
Furthermore, since \(\tilde{r} \geqslant r'\),
we also have \(\distS{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}{{\myvec{a}}_{I};{\myvec{a}}_{K\setminus I}} > r'\).
Hence, for \(\bar{a}' \mathchoice{\ \; }{}{}{} \coloneqq \mathchoice{\ \; }{}{}{} \paren*{  a_1, \dots, a_{k+1}  }\),
since \(I_1 = I \cup \ensuremath{\{k{+}1\}}\)
and \(K' \setminus I_1 = K \setminus I\),
we have
\(\distS{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}{{{\vec{a}}'}_{I_1};
{{\vec{a}}'}_{K' \setminus I_1}} > r'\).
Thus, from equivalence \eqref{eq:proof:fv_decomp:case:kind_near:indhyp_on_disjunction}
(for \(\ell = 1\))
and from the fact that \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \models \lambda ({{\vec{a}}'}_{K'})\),
we obtain that there exists a tuple \(\paren{\alpha,\beta} \in \Delta_1\)
such that \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \models \alpha ({{\vec{a}}'}_{I_1})\)
and \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \models \beta ({{\vec{a}}'}_{K' \setminus I_1})\).
Let \(\alpha' \paren{{\myvec{x}}_{I}}\) be the formula
associated with \(\alpha \paren{{\vec{x}'}_{I_1}}\)
according to \eqref{eq:proof:fv_decomp:kind_near__def_alpha_close_to_first_component},
and note that \(a_{k+1}\) witnesses that \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \models \alpha' ({\myvec{a}}_{I})\).

In summary,
\((\alpha',\beta) \in \Delta'_1 = \Delta\),
and we have \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \models \alpha' ({\myvec{a}}_{I})\)
and \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \models \beta ({\myvec{a}}_{K\setminus I})\).
This completes the direction \enquote{\(\Longrightarrow\)}.
\medskip

For the direction \enquote{\(\Longleftarrow\)},
let \(\paren{\hat{\alpha}, \hat{\beta}} \in \Delta\)
such that \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \models \hat{\alpha} ({\myvec{a}}_{I})\)
and \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \models \hat{\beta} (\vec a_{K\setminus I})\).
We have to show that \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \models \psi (\vec{a}_K)\).
Since we are considering the case \(i \in I\),
we know that \(\Delta = \Delta'_1\).
Thus, there is a tuple \(\paren{\alpha,\beta} \in \Delta_1\)
such that \(\hat{\alpha}\) is the formula \(\alpha' \paren{{\myvec{x}}_{I}}\)
associated with \(\alpha \paren{{\myvec{x}}_{I},x_{k+1}}\)
according to \eqref{eq:proof:fv_decomp:kind_near__def_alpha_close_to_first_component},
and \(\hat{\beta}\) is the formula \(\beta \paren{{\myvec{x}}_{K\setminus I}}\).

Since \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \models \alpha' ({\myvec{a}}_{I})\),
there exists an \(a_{k+1} \in A\)
such that \(\distS{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}{a_i, a_{k+1}} \leqslant \hat{d}\)
and \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \models \alpha ({\myvec{a}}_{I}, a_{k+1})\),
so \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \models \alpha ({\vec{a}'}_{I_1})\),
where $\bar{a}' \coloneqq (a_1,\ldots,a_{k+1})$.
Furthermore,
since \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \models \hat{\beta} ({\myvec{a}}_{K\setminus I})\),
and because \(\hat{\beta}\) is the formula \(\beta \paren{{\myvec{x}}_{K\setminus I}}\),
and \({\myvec{x}}_{K\setminus I} = {\vec{x}'}_{K' \setminus I_1}\),
we have \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \models \beta ({{\vec{a}}'}_{K' \setminus I_1})\).
As above for the direction \enquote{\(\Longrightarrow\)},
having \(\distS{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}{a_i, a_{k+1}} \leqslant \hat{d}\)
implies that \(\distS{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}{{\myvec{a}}_{K \setminus I}; a_{k+1}} > r'\).
Moreover, we already know that
\(\distS{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}{{\myvec{a}}_{I}; {\myvec{a}}_{K \setminus I}} > \tilde{r} \geqslant r'\).
Combined, this shows that
\(\operatorname{dist}^{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}({{\vec{a}}'}_{I_1}; {{\vec{a}}'}_{K' \setminus I_1}) > r'\).

Applying equivalence~\eqref{eq:proof:fv_decomp:case:kind_near:indhyp_on_disjunction}
(for \(\ell = 1\)),
since \(\paren{\alpha,\beta} \in \Delta_1\),
we obtain that \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \models \lambda ({\vec{a}'}_{K'})\).
Since \(\distS{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}{a_i, a_{k+1}} \leqslant \hat{d}\),
we obtain that \(a_{k+1}\) witnesses that \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \models \psi (\vec{a}_K)\).
This completes the proof in case \(i \in I\).
\medskip

In the other case, we have \(i \in K \setminus I\), so $\Delta=\Delta'_2$.
This case follows analogously to the previous case,
with the roles of \(I\) and \(K \setminus I\) interchanged
and using \(\Delta_2\), \(\Delta_2'\)
instead of \(\Delta_1\), \(\Delta_1'\).
\medskip

Now, the induction step is completed for the case that \(\psi\) is of
the form~\ref{itm:def:loc:kind_near}. We proceed with the next case.

\@startsection{subparagraph}{5}{\z@} 
                                       {3.25ex \@plus1ex \@minus .2ex} 
                                       {-1em} 
                                      {\color{lipicsGray}\sffamily 
   \@setfontsize 
   \@setfontsize 
   \@setfontsize 
   \@setfontsize 
   \@setfontsize 
   \@setfontsize 
   \@setfontsize\normalsize\@xpt{13} 
   \abovedisplayskip 10\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus5\p@
   \abovedisplayshortskip \z@ \@plus3\p@
   \belowdisplayshortskip 6\p@ \@plus3\p@ \@minus3\p@
   \belowdisplayskip \abovedisplayskip
   \let\@listi\@listI\@xpt{13} 
   \abovedisplayskip 10\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus5\p@
   \abovedisplayshortskip \z@ \@plus3\p@
   \belowdisplayshortskip 6\p@ \@plus3\p@ \@minus3\p@
   \belowdisplayskip \abovedisplayskip
   \let\leftmargin\leftmargini
            \parsep\z@ \@plus\p@
            \topsep 8\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus4\p@
            \itemsep \parsep\@listi\@xpt{13} 
   \abovedisplayskip 10\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus5\p@
   \abovedisplayshortskip \z@ \@plus3\p@
   \belowdisplayshortskip 6\p@ \@plus3\p@ \@minus3\p@
   \belowdisplayskip \abovedisplayskip
   \let\leftmargin\leftmargini
            \parsep\z@ \@plus\p@
            \topsep 8\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus4\p@
            \itemsep \parsep\leftmargin\leftmargini
            \parsep\z@ \@plus\p@
            \topsep 8\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus4\p@
            \itemsep \parsep\@xpt{13} 
   \abovedisplayskip 10\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus5\p@
   \abovedisplayshortskip \z@ \@plus3\p@
   \belowdisplayshortskip 6\p@ \@plus3\p@ \@minus3\p@
   \belowdisplayskip \abovedisplayskip
   \let\leftmargin\leftmargini
            \parsep\z@ \@plus\p@
            \topsep 8\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus4\p@
            \itemsep \parsep\leftmargin\leftmargini
            \parsep\z@ \@plus\p@
            \topsep 8\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus4\p@
            \itemsep \parsep\@xpt{13} 
   \abovedisplayskip 10\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus5\p@
   \abovedisplayshortskip \z@ \@plus3\p@
   \belowdisplayshortskip 6\p@ \@plus3\p@ \@minus3\p@
   \belowdisplayskip \abovedisplayskip
   \let\leftmargin\leftmargini
            \parsep\z@ \@plus\p@
            \topsep 8\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus4\p@
            \itemsep \parsep\leftmargin\leftmargini
            \parsep\z@ \@plus\p@
            \topsep 8\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus4\p@
            \itemsep \parsep\@xpt{13} 
   \abovedisplayskip 10\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus5\p@
   \abovedisplayshortskip \z@ \@plus3\p@
   \belowdisplayshortskip 6\p@ \@plus3\p@ \@minus3\p@
   \belowdisplayskip \abovedisplayskip
   \let\leftmargin\leftmargini
            \parsep\z@ \@plus\p@
            \topsep 8\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus4\p@
            \itemsep \parsep\leftmargin\leftmargini
            \parsep\z@ \@plus\p@
            \topsep 8\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus4\p@
            \itemsep \parsep\@xpt{13} 
   \abovedisplayskip 10\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus5\p@
   \abovedisplayshortskip \z@ \@plus3\p@
   \belowdisplayshortskip 6\p@ \@plus3\p@ \@minus3\p@
   \belowdisplayskip \abovedisplayskip
   \let\leftmargin\leftmargini
            \parsep\z@ \@plus\p@
            \topsep 8\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus4\p@
            \itemsep \parsep\leftmargin\leftmargini
            \parsep\z@ \@plus\p@
            \topsep 8\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus4\p@
            \itemsep \parsep\bfseries}{Case~\ref{itm:def:loc:kind_weight_aggregation}:}
\(\psi\) is of the form \ref{itm:def:loc:kind_weight_aggregation}.
Then, we have
\[
  \psi\paren{{\myvec{x}}_{K}} \ = \ \Bigl(
    s =
    \sum \ensuremath{\mathtt{w}}(\bar{y}).\underbrace{
    \bigl(\;
      \lambda
      \; \und
      \Oder_{\substack{j\in J,\\ \nu\in[\ell]}}
      \operatorname{dist}(x_j,x_{k+\nu})\,{\leqslant}\, r'
     \;\bigr)}_{\eqqcolon \ \lambda'(\bar{x}'_{K'})}
  \;\Bigr),
\]
where $\ensuremath{\mathtt{w}}\in\ensuremath{\mathbf{W}}$,
$S=\ensuremath{\textup{type}}(\ensuremath{\mathtt{w}})$ is finite,
$s\in S$, $\ell=\operatorname*{ar}(\ensuremath{\mathtt{w}})\geqslant 1$,
$\bar{y}=
(x_{k+1},\ldots,x_{k+\ell})$,
$\emptyset\neq J \subseteq K$,
and
$\lambda\in\operatorname{L} ({q}, {k{+}\wmaxar}, {d})$ is a formula with free variables
among $\ensuremath{\ensuremath{\{x_\kappa : \kappa\in K'\}}}$, where $K'\coloneqq
K\cup\ensuremath{\{k{+}1,\ldots,k{+}\ell\}}$.
Let $k'\coloneqq k{+}\wmaxar$ and $\bar{x}'\coloneqq (x_1,\ldots,x_{k'})$.

If $J\cap I\neq \emptyset$, then, for every $t\in S$, we set
\[
  \varrho_t(\bar{x}_K) \ \coloneqq\ \Bigl(
    t =
    \sum \ensuremath{\mathtt{w}}(\bar{y}).\underbrace{
      \bigl(\;
        \lambda
        \; \und \!\!
        \Oder_{\substack{j\in J\cap I,\\ \nu\in[\ell]}}
        \operatorname{dist}(x_j,x_{k+\nu})\,{\leqslant}\, r'
    \;\bigr)}_{\eqqcolon \ \lambda_1(\bar{x}'_{K'})}
  \Bigr).
\]
Note that this formula is similar to $\psi$ but replaces $s$ with $t$
and ensures that
one of the quantified variables
$x_{k+1},\ldots,x_{k+\ell}$
is close to an $x_j$ with $j\in J\cap I$.

Analogously, if $J\setminus I\neq \emptyset$, we set
\[
  \vartheta_t(\bar{x}_K) \ \coloneqq \ \Bigl(
    t =
    \sum \ensuremath{\mathtt{w}}(\bar{y}).\underbrace{
      \bigl(\;
        \lambda
        \; \und \!\!
        \Oder_{\substack{j\in J\setminus I,\\ \nu\in[\ell]}}
        \operatorname{dist}(x_j,x_{k+\nu})\,{\leqslant}\, r'
      \;\bigr)}_{\eqqcolon \ \lambda_2(\bar{x}'_{K'})}
  \Bigr);
\]
this formula ensures that one of the quantified variables
$x_{k+1},\ldots,x_{k+\ell}$
is close to an $x_j$ with $j\in J\setminus I$.
Note that
the formulas $\lambda_1(\bar{x}'_{K'})$ and
$\lambda_2(\bar{x}'_{K'})$ belong to $\operatorname{L} ({q}, {k{+}\wmaxar}, {d})$ and have
the same quantifier rank as $\lambda$.

Clearly, if $J\cap I=\emptyset$, then $J\setminus I=J\neq \emptyset$,
and $\vartheta_t$ is defined for all $t\in S$. Analogously, if
$J\setminus I=\emptyset$, then $J\subseteq I$ and $J\cap
I=J\neq\emptyset$, and $\varrho_t$ is defined for all $t\in S$.

Let $T\coloneqq \ensuremath{\ensuremath{\{(t_1,t_2)\in S\times S : t_1 +_S t_2 = s\}}}$ (this set
is finite because $S$ is finite).
Let
\[
  \hat{\psi}
  ~ \coloneqq ~
  \left\{
    \begin{array}{ll}
       \vartheta_s
    & \text{if } J\cap I=\emptyset,
    \\
        \varrho_s
    & \text{if } J\setminus I=\emptyset,
    \\
       \Oder_{(t_1,t_2)\in T} \big( \varrho_{t_1} \und \vartheta_{t_2} \big)
    & \text{otherwise.}
    \end{array}
  \right.
\]

We fix the following notation. For any
 \(\paren*{ \sigma, \ensuremath{\mathbf{W}} }\)-structure \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}\)
and any tuple \(\bar{a} \in A^k\)
with \(\distS{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}{{\myvec{a}}_{I};{\myvec{a}}_{K\setminus I}} > \tilde{r}\),
we let

\begin{itemize}
\item
 $M^\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}_{\bar{a}} \ \ \coloneqq \ \ensuremath{\bigl\{ \,\bar{b} \in A^\ell\, \ \mathrel{:} \  \,\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \models \lambda'(\bar{c}_K) \text{ and } \ensuremath{\mathtt{w}}^\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}(\bar{b}) \neq 0_S,
  \text{ where } \bar{c} \coloneqq \bar{a} \bar{b}\, \bigr\}},$
\item
$M^\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}_{\bar{a},1} \ \coloneqq \ \ensuremath{\bigl\{ \,\bar{b} \in A^\ell\, \ \mathrel{:} \  \,\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \models \lambda_1(\bar{c}_K) \text{ and } \ensuremath{\mathtt{w}}^\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}(\bar{b}) \neq 0_S,
  \text{ where } \bar{c} \coloneqq \bar{a} \bar{b}\, \bigr\}},$
if \(J \cap I \neq \emptyset\),
\item
$M^\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}_{\bar{a},2} \ \coloneqq \ \ensuremath{\bigl\{ \,\bar{b} \in A^\ell\, \ \mathrel{:} \  \,\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \models \lambda_2(\bar{c}_K) \text{ and } \ensuremath{\mathtt{w}}^\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}(\bar{b}) \neq 0_S,
\text{ where } \bar{c} \coloneqq \bar{a} \bar{b}\, \bigr\}}$,
if \(J \setminus I \neq \emptyset\).
\end{itemize}

\noindent
Clearly, we have
\begin{equation*}
  \ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \models \psi(\bar{a}_K)
  ~ \iff ~
  s = \sum_{\bar{b} \in M^\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}_{\bar{a}}} \ensuremath{\mathtt{w}}^\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}(\bar{b});
\end{equation*}
and for all \(t \in S\) we have
\begin{equation}
  \label{eq:fv:varrhot}
  \ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \models \varrho_t(\bar{a}_K)
  ~ \iff ~
  t = \sum_{\bar{b} \in M^\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}_{\bar{a},1}} \ensuremath{\mathtt{w}}^\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}(\bar{b})
\end{equation}
(if $J\cap I\neq \emptyset$), and
\begin{equation*}
  \ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \models \vartheta_t(\bar{a}_K)
  ~ \iff ~
  t = \sum_{\bar{b} \in M^\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}_{\bar{a},2}} \ensuremath{\mathtt{w}}^\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}(\bar{b})
\end{equation*}
(if $J\setminus I\neq \emptyset$).

\begin{claim}\label{claim:fv_agg_M}
  Let \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}\) be a \(\paren*{ \sigma, \ensuremath{\mathbf{W}} }\)-structure, and
  let \(\bar{a} \in A^k\)
  with \(\distS{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}{{\myvec{a}}_{I};{\myvec{a}}_{K\setminus I}} >
  \tilde{r}\).
  \\
  If \(J \cap I \neq \emptyset\),
  then, for all \(\bar{b} \in M^\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}_{\bar{a},1}\)
  we have \(\distS{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}{{\myvec{a}}_{I}\bar{b}; {\myvec{a}}_{K \setminus I}}
  > r'\).
  \\
  If \(J \setminus I \neq \emptyset\),
  then, for all \(\bar{b} \in M^\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}_{\bar{a},2}\)
  we have \(\distS{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}{{\myvec{a}}_{I}; {\myvec{a}}_{K \setminus I}\bar{b}}
  > r'\).
  \\
  If \(J \cap I \neq \emptyset\) and \(J \setminus I \neq \emptyset\),
  then we have \(M^\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}_{\bar{a}} = M^\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}_{\bar{a},1} \uplus M^\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}_{\bar{a},2}\).
\end{claim}
\begin{claimproof}
  First, suppose \(J \cap I \neq \emptyset\),
  and let \(\bar{b} \in M^\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}_{\bar{a},1}\).
  Then there exist $i\in J\cap I$ and \(\nu \in [\ell]\) such that
  \(\distS{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}{a_i, b_\nu} \leqslant r'\).
  Moreover, for all \(\mu \in [\ell]\), since \(\ensuremath{\mathtt{w}}^\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}(\bar{b}) \neq 0_S\),
  we have \(\distS{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}{b_\nu, b_\mu} \leqslant 1\) by the \emph{locality
    condition}
  that is satisfied by the weighted structure \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}\).

  We have to show that
  \(\distS{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}{{\myvec{a}}_{I}\bar{b}; {\myvec{a}}_{K\setminus I}} > r'\),
  and we already know that
  \(\distS{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}{{\myvec{a}}_{I}; {\myvec{a}}_{K\setminus I}}
  > \tilde{r} \geqslant r'\).
  All that remains to be done is to consider arbitrary
  \(\kappa \in K \setminus I\) and \(\mu \in [\ell]\)
  and show that
  \(\distS{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}{b_\mu, a_\kappa} > r'\).
  Since \(i\in I\) and \(\kappa \in K\setminus I\),
  we have
  \(\distS{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}{a_i, a_\kappa} > \tilde{r}\).
  Thus,
  \[
    \tilde{r}
    ~ < ~
    \operatorname{dist}^{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}(a_i,a_\kappa)
    ~ \leqslant ~
    \operatorname{dist}^{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}(a_i,b_\nu) + \operatorname{dist}^{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}(b_\nu,b_\mu) +
    \operatorname{dist}^{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}(b_\mu,a_\kappa)
    ~ \leqslant ~
    r'+1+\operatorname{dist}^{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}(b_\mu,a_\kappa)\,.
  \]
  Hence, we have
  \(
    \distS{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}{b_\mu, a_\kappa}
    >
    \tilde{r}-r'-1
    \overset{\eqref{eq:def:radius_function}}{=}
    4kr' - r' - 1
    \geqslant
    r'
  \).
  This holds for all \(\mu \in [\ell]\)
  and all \(\kappa \in K \setminus I\).
  In summary, we obtain that
  \(\distS{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}{{\myvec{a}}_{I}\bar{b}; {\myvec{a}}_{K \setminus I}} > r'\).

  For the case \(J \setminus I \neq \emptyset\),
  we can analogously prove
  \(\distS{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}{{\myvec{a}}_{I}; {\myvec{a}}_{K \setminus I}\bar{b}} > r'\)
  for all \(\bar{b} \in M^\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}_{\bar{a}, 2}\).
  Lastly, for the case \(J \cap I \neq \emptyset\)
  and \(J \setminus I \neq \emptyset\),
  it is easy to see that \(M^\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}_{\bar{a}} = M^\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}_{\bar{a},1} \cup M^\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}_{\bar{a},2}\).
  Moreover, if \(\bar{b} \in M^\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}_{\bar{a},1}\),
  then \(\distS{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}{\bar{b}; {\myvec{a}}_{K \setminus I}} > r'\),
  which implies that \(\bar{b} \not\in M^\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}_{\bar{a},2}\),
  Hence, \(M^\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}_{\bar{a},1} \cap M^\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}_{\bar{a},2} = \emptyset\),
  so \(M^\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}_{\bar a} = M^\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}_{\bar{a},1} \uplus M^\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}_{\bar{a},2}\).
\end{claimproof}

\begin{claim}\label{claim:fv_agg_one}
  \(\psi(\bar{x}_K)\; \und\; \operatorname{dist}({\myvec{x}}_{I}; {\myvec{x}}_{K\setminus I})
  \,{>}\,\tilde{r}
  \ \ \equiv
  \ \ \hat{\psi}({\myvec{x}}_{K}) \;\und\; \operatorname{dist}({\myvec{x}}_{I};{\myvec{x}}_{K\setminus I})
  \,{>}\,\tilde{r}\)\,.
\end{claim}
\begin{claimproof}
  If $J\cap I=\emptyset$, then $J=J\setminus I$ and $\psi=\vartheta_s=\hat{\psi}$.
  If $J\setminus I =\emptyset$, then $J=J\cap I$ and
  $\psi=\varrho_s=\hat{\psi}$.
  Hence, in both cases the claim holds trivially.

  Now consider the remaining case that $J\cap I\neq \emptyset$
  and $J\setminus I\neq \emptyset$.
  Then, for an arbitrary \(\paren*{ \sigma, \ensuremath{\mathbf{W}} }\)-structure \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}\)
  and a tuple \(\bar{a} \in A^k\)
  with \(\distS{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}{{\myvec{a}}_{I};{\myvec{a}}_{K\setminus I}} > \tilde{r}\),
  we have \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \models \psi(\bar{a}_K)\) $\iff$
  \(s = \sum_{\bar{b} \in M^\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}_{\bar{a}}} \ensuremath{\mathtt{w}}^\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}(\bar{b})
  \overset{\text{\cref{claim:fv_agg_M}}}{=}
  \sum_{\bar{b} \in M^\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}_{\bar{a},1}} \ensuremath{\mathtt{w}}^\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}(\bar{b})
  \ +_S \ \sum_{\bar{b} \in M^\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}_{\bar{a},2}} \ensuremath{\mathtt{w}}^\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}(\bar{b})\),
  where \(M^\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}_{\bar{a}}\), \(M^\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}_{\bar{a},1}\),
  and \(M^\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}_{\bar{a},2}\) are as defined above.
  Thus, \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \models \psi(\bar{a}_K)\) $\iff$
  there are \(t_1, t_2 \in S\) with \(s = t_1 +_S t_2\),
  \(t_1 = \sum_{\bar{b} \in M^\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}_{\bar{a},1}} \ensuremath{\mathtt{w}}^\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}(\bar{b})\), and
  \(t_2 = \sum_{\bar{b} \in M^\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}_{\bar{a},2}} \ensuremath{\mathtt{w}}^\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}(\bar{b})\).
  The latter holds if and only if
  \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \models \varrho_{t_1}(\bar{a}_K)\)
  and
  \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \models \vartheta_{t_2}(\bar{a}_K)\).
  According to our choice of $T$ and $\hat{\psi}$, we obtain that
  $\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}\models\psi(\bar a_K)\iff\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}\models\hat{\psi}(\bar a_K)$.
\end{claimproof}

The next two claims provide the main step needed for finishing the proof
for the case that $\psi$ is of the form
\ref{itm:def:loc:kind_weight_aggregation}.

\begin{claim}
  \label{claim:fv_agg_varrho}
  If $J\cap I\neq \emptyset$, then, for every $t\in S$,
  we can compute a finite, non-empty set $\Delta_t$ of pairs of formulas
  $\big(\alpha(\bar{x}_I),\beta(\bar{x}_{K\setminus I})\big)$
  such that $\alpha(\bar{x}_I)\in\operatorname{L} ({q{+}1}, {k}, {d})$,
  $\beta(\bar{x}_{K\setminus I})\in\operatorname{L} ({q{+}1}, {k}, {d})$, their quantifier
  rank is $\leqslant \operatorname*{qr}(\varrho_t)$, and
  \[
    \varrho_t\paren{{\vec{x}}_{K}}
    \mathbin{\mathchoice{\ }{}{}{} \land \mathchoice{\ }{}{}{}}
    \operatorname{dist}({\vec{x}}_{I}; {\vec{x}}_{K \setminus I})> \tilde{r}
    ~~ \equiv ~~
    \Oder_{\mathclap{\paren{\alpha,\beta} \in \Delta_t}}
    ~~
    \paren[\Big]{
      \alpha \paren{{\vec{x}}_{I}}
      \und
      \beta \paren{{\vec{x}}_{K \setminus I}}
    }
    \mathbin{\mathchoice{\ }{}{}{} \land \mathchoice{\ }{}{}{}}
    \operatorname{dist}({\vec{x}}_{I}; {\vec{x}}_{K \setminus I})> \tilde{r}\,.
  \]
\end{claim}
\begin{claimproof}
  We use the induction hypothesis for
  $k'= k{+}\wmaxar$,
  $K'= K\cup\ensuremath{\{x_{k+1},\ldots,x_{k+\ell}\}}$, the formula
  $\lambda_1(\bar{x}'_{K'})$, and
  the set $I_1\coloneqq I\cup\ensuremath{\{x_{k+1},\ldots,x_{k+\ell}\}}$.
  This yields a finite, non-empty set \(\Delta'_1\)
  that
  consists of pairs of formulas \(\paren{\alpha,\beta}\)
  with \(\alpha \paren{{\myvec{x}}_{I_{1}}} \in \operatorname{L} ({q}, {k'}, {d})\) and
  \(\beta \paren{{\myvec{x}}_{K' \setminus I_{1}}} \in \operatorname{L} ({q}, {k'}, {d})\)
  and of quantifier rank $\leqslant \operatorname*{qr}(\lambda_1)$,
  such that, for \(\vec{x}' = (x_1,\ldots,x_{k'})\), we have
\begin{equation}
    \label{eq:proof:fv_decomp:case:kind_weight_aggregation:indhyp_one}
    \begin{array}{ll}
    & \lambda_1 \paren{{\vec{x}'}_{K'}}
    \mathbin{\mathchoice{\ }{}{}{} \land \mathchoice{\ }{}{}{}}
    \operatorname{dist}({\vec{x}'}_{I_1}; {\vec{x}'}_{K' \setminus I_1}) > r'
    \smallskip\\
    \mathchoice{\ \; }{}{}{} \equiv \mathchoice{\ \; }{}{}{} & \displaystyle
    \Oder_{\mathclap{\paren{\alpha,\beta} \in
                                  \Delta'_{1}}} \quad
    \paren[\Big]{
      \alpha \paren{{\vec{x}'}_{I_1}}
      \und
      \beta \paren{{\vec{x}'}_{K' \setminus I_1}}
    }
    \mathbin{\mathchoice{\ }{}{}{} \land \mathchoice{\ }{}{}{}}
    \operatorname{dist}({\vec{x}'}_{I_1}; {\vec{x}'}_{K' \setminus I_1}) > r'
            .
          \end{array}
  \end{equation}
Note that,
  by our choice of $K'$ and $I_1$, we have
  $K'\setminus I_1=K\setminus I$,
  so $\bar{x}'_{K'\setminus I_1} = \bar{x}_{K\setminus I}$.
  Furthermore, we have $\bar{x}'_{I_1} = \bar{x}_I \bar{y}$,
  where $\bar{y}=(x_{k+1},\ldots,x_{k+\ell})$.
Our choice of $I_1$ reflects the fact that the formula $\varrho_t$
  aims at tuples $\bar{y}$ where at least one of the variables in $\bar
  y$ is close to an $x_j$ with $j\in J\cap I\subseteq I$.
By \cref{lemma:fv_mux}, we can assume w.l.o.g.\ that
  the \emph{second} entries in $\Delta'_1$ are mutually exclusive.

  Now fix a $t\in S$.
  For each $(\alpha,\beta)\in\Delta'_1$, we define
  \[
    \alpha_t(\bar{x}_I)
    ~ \coloneqq ~
    \Big(
        t =
        \sum \ensuremath{\mathtt{w}}(\bar{y}).
        \big(\;
          \alpha(\bar{x}'_{I_1})
          \; \und
          \Oder_{\substack{j\in J\cap I,\\ \nu\in[\ell]}} \operatorname{dist}(x_j,x_{k+\nu})\,{\leqslant}\, r'
        \;\big)
    \Big).
  \]

  Let \(\tilde{\Delta}_t \coloneqq \ensuremath{\bigl\{ (\alpha_t,\beta) \ \mathrel{:} \  (\alpha,\beta) \in \Delta'_1 \bigr\}}\),
  \(\myBetas \coloneqq \ensuremath{\ensuremath{\{\beta : \text{there exists an } \alpha
  \text{ such that } (\alpha,\beta)\in \Delta'_1\}}}\), and
  \[
    \Delta_t\ \coloneqq\ \begin{cases}
    \tilde{\Delta}_t
    & \text{if }  t\neq 0_S,\\
    \tilde{\Delta}_t \,\cup\,\ensuremath{\bigl\{ \big(\true\,,\,\ensuremath{\bigwedge}_{\beta\in\myBetas} \neg\beta\big) \bigr\}}
    & \text{if } t=0_S.
    \end{cases}
  \]
  Note that $\Delta_t$ is a finite, non-empty set of pairs of formulas
  $(\alpha',\beta')$ such that $\alpha'(\bar{x}_I)\in\operatorname{L} ({q{+}1}, {k}, {d})$ and
  $\beta'(\bar{x}_{K\setminus I})\in\operatorname{L} ({q{+}1}, {k}, {d})$ and their
  quantifier rank is $\leqslant \operatorname*{qr}(\varrho_t)$.

  All that remains to be done is to prove
  that the equivalence stated in the claim holds.
  Consider a \(\paren{\sigma,\ensuremath{\mathbf{W}}}\)-structure \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}\)
  and a tuple \(\bar{a} \in A^k\) such that
  \(\distS{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}{\bar{a}_I; \bar{a}_{K\setminus I}} > \tilde{r}\).
  We have to show that
  \begin{equation}\label{eq:fv:varrho:goal}
    \ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}\models\varrho_t(\bar{a}_K)
    \iff
    \text{there is an } (\alpha',\beta')\in\Delta_t \text{ with }
    \ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}\models\alpha'(\bar{a}_I)
    \text{ and }
    \ensuremath{\mathcal{B}}\models\beta'(\bar{a}_{K\setminus I}).
  \end{equation}

  Since the second entries in \(\Delta'_1\) are mutually exclusive,
  there exists at most one \(\beta \in \myBetas\)
  such that \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \models \beta({\myvec{a}}_{K\setminus I})\).
  Hence, one of the following two cases applies.

  \bigskip
  \noindent
  \emph{Case~1:}
  There is exactly one \(\beta \in \myBetas\)
  such that \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \models \beta({\myvec{a}}_{K \setminus I})\).
In this case,
  the particular definition of the notion of
  \enquote{the second entries in \(\Delta'_1\) being mutually exclusive}
  implies that there exists exactly one \(\alpha\)
  such that \((\alpha,\beta) \in \Delta'_1\).
  Using this \(\alpha\), we let
  \[\lambda_\alpha(\bar{x}'_{I_1}) \ \coloneqq \ \alpha(\bar{x}'_{I_1})
  \land \ensuremath{\bigvee}_{\substack{j  \in J \cap I,\\ \nu \in [\ell]}}
  \operatorname{dist}(x_j,x_{k + \nu}) \,{\leqslant}\, r'\]
  and
  \[N^\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}_{\bar{a}} \ \coloneqq \ \ensuremath{\bigl\{ \,\bar{b} \in A^\ell\, \ \mathrel{:} \  \,\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \models \lambda_\alpha(\bar{c}_{I_1}) \text{ and } \ensuremath{\mathtt{w}}^\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}(\bar{b}) \neq 0_S,
    \text{ where } \bar{c} \coloneqq \bar{a} \bar{b}\, \bigr\}}.\]
In the following, we will show that
$N^{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}_{\bar{a}}=M^{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}_{\bar{a},1}$.
Note that
  \begin{equation}
    \label{eq:fv:alphat}
    \ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}\models\alpha_t(\bar{a}_I)
    ~ \iff ~
    t = \sum_{\bar{b} \in N^\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}_{\bar{a}}} \ensuremath{\mathtt{w}}^{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}(\bar{b}).
  \end{equation}
  Analogously to \cref{claim:fv_agg_M},
  we can show that
  \begin{equation}
    \label{eq:fv:agg_dist_N}
    \distS{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}{{\myvec{a}}_{I}\bar{b}; {\myvec{a}}_{K \setminus I}} > r',
    ~
    \text{ for all }
    \bar{b} \in N^\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}_{\bar{a}}.
  \end{equation}
Now let \(\bar{b} \in A^\ell\) with \(\ensuremath{\mathtt{w}}^\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}(\bar{b}) \neq 0_S\),
  and set \(\bar{c} \coloneqq \bar{a} \bar{b}\).
  Then, by \cref{claim:fv_agg_M},
  we have
  \[\bar{b} \in M^\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}_{\bar{a}, 1} \ \iff \ \ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \models \lambda_1(\bar{c}_K) \text{ and }
  \distS{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}{{\myvec{c}}_{I_1}; {\myvec{c}}_{K' \setminus I_1}} > r'.\]
  According to our choice of \(\Delta_t\),
  the tuple \((\alpha_t, \beta)\) is the only \((\alpha', \beta') \in \Delta_t\)
  such that \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \models \beta'({\myvec{a}}_{K\setminus I})\).
  Therefore,
  by \cref{eq:proof:fv_decomp:case:kind_weight_aggregation:indhyp_one},
  we have
  \[
    \ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \models \lambda_1(\bar{c}_K) \text{ and }
    \distS{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}{{\myvec{c}}_{I_1}; {\myvec{c}}_{K' \setminus I_1}} > r'
    ~ \iff ~
    \ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \models \alpha(\bar{c}_{I_1}) \text{ and }
    \distS{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}{{\myvec{c}}_{I_1}; {\myvec{c}}_{K' \setminus I_1}} > r'.
  \]
  Moreover, if \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \models \lambda_1(\bar{c}_K)\),
  then, in particular, by the definition of \(\lambda_1\),
  there are \(j \in J \cap I\) and \(\nu \in [\ell]\)
  such that \(\distS{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}{a_j, b_\nu} \leqslant r'\).
  Hence,
  \[
    \ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \models \lambda_1(\bar{c}_K) \text{ and }
    \distS{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}{{\myvec{c}}_{I_1}; {\myvec{c}}_{K' \setminus I_1}} > r'
    ~ \iff ~
    \ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \models \lambda_\alpha(\bar{c}_{I_1}) \text{ and }
    \distS{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}{{\myvec{c}}_{I_1}; {\myvec{c}}_{K' \setminus I_1}} > r'.
  \]
  Finally, by \cref{eq:fv:agg_dist_N}, we have
  \[
    \ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \models \lambda_\alpha(\bar{c}_{I_1}) \text{ and }
    \distS{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}{{\myvec{c}}_{I_1}; {\myvec{c}}_{K' \setminus I_1}} > r'
    \ \iff \ \bar{b} \in N^\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}_{\bar{a}}.
  \]
  All in all, this shows that
  \(N^\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}_{\bar{a}} = M^{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}_{\bar{a},1}\).

  Combined with \cref{eq:fv:alphat,eq:fv:varrhot},
  this proves the following equivalence:
  \[
    \begin{array}{rcl}
      \text{there is an \((\alpha',\beta') \in \Delta_t\)
        with \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \models \alpha'(\bar{a}_I)\)
      and \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \models \beta'(\bar{a}_{K\setminus I}\))}
      &
      \!\!\!\!\iff
      & \ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}\models\alpha_t(\bar{a}_I)
      \\
      &
      \!\!\!\!\stackrel{\eqref{eq:fv:alphat}}{\iff}
      & t = \sum_{\bar{b} \in N^{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}_{\bar{a}}} \ensuremath{\mathtt{w}}^{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}(\bar{b})
      \\
      &
      \!\!\!\!\!\!\stackrel{N^{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}_{\bar{a}}=M^{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}_{\bar{a},1}}{\iff}\!\!
      & t = \sum_{\bar{b} \in M^{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}_{\bar{a},1}} \ensuremath{\mathtt{w}}^{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}(\bar{b})
      \\
      &
      \!\!\!\!\stackrel{\eqref{eq:fv:varrhot}}{\iff}
      & \ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \models \varrho_t(\bar{a}_K).
    \end{array}
  \]
  This proves that equivalence \eqref{eq:fv:varrho:goal} holds and
  completes Case~1.

  \bigskip
  \noindent
  \emph{Case~2:}
  For all \(\beta \in \myBetas\),
  we have
  \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \not\models \beta(\bar{a}_{K\setminus I})\).
  As above, for every \(\bar{b} \in A^\ell\) with \(\ensuremath{\mathtt{w}}^\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}(\bar{b}) \neq 0_S\)
  and \(\bar{c} \coloneqq \bar{a}\bar{b}\),
  by \cref{claim:fv_agg_M},
  we have that
    \[\bar{b} \in M^\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}_{\bar{a}, 1} \ \iff \ \ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \models \lambda_1(\bar{c}_K) \text{ and }
  \distS{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}{{\myvec{c}}_{I_1}; {\myvec{c}}_{K' \setminus I_1}} > r'.\]
  By \cref{eq:proof:fv_decomp:case:kind_weight_aggregation:indhyp_one},
  since there is no \(\beta \in \myBetas\)
  with \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \models \beta({\myvec{a}}_{K \setminus I})\),
  this implies that
  \(M^\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}_{\bar{a}, 1} = \emptyset\).
  Using \cref{eq:fv:varrhot} yields that
  \[
    \ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}\models\varrho_t(\bar{a}_K)\ \iff \ t=0_S.
  \]
  Now, recall our choice of $\Delta_t$. If $t=0_S$, then
  $\Delta_t$ contains the tuple $\big(\true\,,
  \Und_{\beta\in\myBetas}\nicht\beta\,\big)$, and we have
  $\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}\models\true$ and $\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}\models \Und_{\beta\in\myBetas}\nicht\beta\,
  (\bar{a}_{K\setminus I})$.
  Hence, equivalence
  \eqref{eq:fv:varrho:goal} holds.
  On the other hand, if $t\neq 0_S$, then all tuples in $\Delta_t$ are
  of the form $(\alpha',\beta')$ for some $\beta'\in\myBetas$. Thus, since we
  are in Case~2, there exists no tuple $(\alpha',\beta')\in\Delta_t$
  with $\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}\models\beta'(\bar{a}_{K\setminus I})$.
  Again, equivalence \eqref{eq:fv:varrho:goal} holds,
  and Case~2 is completed.

  This completes the proof of \cref{claim:fv_agg_varrho}.
\end{claimproof}

\begin{claim}
  \label{claim:fv_agg_vartheta}
  If $J\setminus I\neq \emptyset$, then, for every $t\in S$,
  we can compute a finite, non-empty set $\altDelta_t$ of pairs of formulas
  $\big(\alpha(\bar{x}_I),\beta(\bar{x}_{K\setminus I})\big)$
  such that $\alpha(\bar{x}_I)\in\operatorname{L} ({q{+}1}, {k}, {d})$,
  $\beta(\bar{x}_{K\setminus I})\in\operatorname{L} ({q{+}1}, {k}, {d})$, their quantifier
  rank is $\leqslant \operatorname*{qr}(\vartheta_t)$, and
  \[
    \vartheta_t\paren{{\vec{x}}_{K}}
    \mathbin{\mathchoice{\ }{}{}{} \land \mathchoice{\ }{}{}{}}
    \operatorname{dist}({\vec{x}}_{I}; {\vec{x}}_{K \setminus I})> \tilde{r}
    ~~ \equiv ~~
    \Oder_{\mathclap{\paren{\alpha,\beta} \in \altDelta_t}}
    ~~
    \paren[\Big]{
      \alpha \paren{{\vec{x}}_{I}}
      \und
      \beta \paren{{\vec{x}}_{K \setminus I}}
    }
    \mathbin{\mathchoice{\ }{}{}{} \land \mathchoice{\ }{}{}{}}
    \operatorname{dist}({\vec{x}}_{I}; {\vec{x}}_{K \setminus I})> \tilde{r}\,.
  \]
\end{claim}
\begin{claimproof}
  We proceed analogously as in our proof of \cref{claim:fv_agg_varrho},
  but we use the induction hypothesis for the formula \(\lambda_2\)
  and the set \(I_2 \coloneqq I\),
  yielding a finite, non-empty set \(\Delta'_2\).
  Our choice of \(I_2\) reflects the fact that the formula \(\vartheta_t\)
  aims at tuples \(\bar{y}\) where at least one of the variables in \(\bar{y}\)
  is close to an \(x_j\) with \(j \in J \setminus I \subseteq K \setminus I\).
  By \cref{lemma:fv_mux}, we can assume w.l.o.g.\ that
  the \emph{first} entries in \(\Delta'_2\) are mutually exclusive.

  Now fix a \(t \in S\).
  For each \((\alpha,\beta) \in \Delta'_2\), we define
  \[
    \beta_t(\bar{x}_{K\setminus I})
    ~ \coloneqq ~
    \Big(
        t =
        \sum \ensuremath{\mathtt{w}}(\bar{y}).
        \big(\;
          \beta(\bar{x}'_{K'\setminus I})
          \; \und
          \Oder_{\substack{j\in J\setminus I,\\ \nu\in[\ell]}} \operatorname{dist}(x_j,x_{k+\nu})\,{\leqslant}\, r'
        \;\big)
    \Big).
  \]
  Let \(\tilde{\altDelta}_t \coloneqq
  \ensuremath{\bigl\{ (\alpha,\beta_t) \ \mathrel{:} \  (\alpha,\beta) \in \Delta'_2 \bigr\}}\),
  \(\myAlphas \coloneqq \ensuremath{\ensuremath{\{\alpha : \text{there exists a } \beta
  \text{ such that } (\alpha,\beta)\in \Delta'_2\}}}\),
  and
  \[
    \altDelta_t\ \coloneqq \ \begin{cases}
      \tilde{\altDelta}_t
       & \text{if }  t\neq 0_S,\\
       \tilde{\altDelta}_t \,\cup\,\ensuremath{\bigl\{ \,\big(\Und_{\alpha\in\myAlphas} \neg\alpha\,,\,\true\,\big) \bigr\}}
       & \text{if } t=0_S.
    \end{cases}
  \]
  Note that \(\altDelta_t\) is a finite, non-empty set of pairs of formulas
  $(\alpha',\beta')$ such that $\alpha'(\bar{x}_I)\in\operatorname{L} ({q{+}1}, {k}, {d})$ and
  $\beta'(\bar{x}_{K\setminus I})\in\operatorname{L} ({q{+}1}, {k}, {d})$ and their
  quantifier rank is $\leqslant \operatorname*{qr}(\vartheta_t)$.

  All that remains to be done is to prove that the equivalence stated in
  \cref{claim:fv_agg_vartheta} holds.
  The proof follows along the same lines as the proof of
  \cref{claim:fv_agg_varrho},
  and this completes the proof of \cref{claim:fv_agg_vartheta}.
\end{claimproof}

We are now ready to finish the proof for the case that \(\psi\)
is of the form \ref{itm:def:loc:kind_weight_aggregation}.
Revisit \cref{claim:fv_agg_one} and the particular formula \(\hat{\psi}\).
If $J\cap I=\emptyset$ or $J\setminus I=\emptyset$,
then $\hat{\psi}$ is one of the formulas handled by
\cref{claim:fv_agg_varrho,claim:fv_agg_vartheta}, and we are done.
In the remaining case, $\hat{\psi}$ is equivalent to
$\Oder_{(t_1,t_2)\in T}\nicht (\nicht \varrho_{t_1}\oder \nicht\vartheta_{t_2})$.
Starting with the sets $\Delta_{t_1}$ and $\altDelta_{t_2}$
obtained for all $(t_1,t_2)\in T$
from \cref{claim:fv_agg_varrho,claim:fv_agg_vartheta} for the formulas
$\varrho_{t_1}$ and $\vartheta_{t_2}$, we can use the same
constructions for disjunctions and negations that we already used in
the induction base, see the Cases
\ref{case:proof:fv_decomp:base_case:disjunction}
and
\ref{case:proof:fv_decomp:base_case:negation}.
This, finally, completes the induction step for the case that $\psi$ is of
the form \ref{itm:def:loc:kind_weight_aggregation}.

To entirely complete the induction step,
all that remains to be done is to consider Boolean combinations,
i.e., disjunctions and negations.
These can be handled in exactly the same way as we had handled them in the
induction base, see the Cases
\ref{case:proof:fv_decomp:base_case:disjunction}
and
\ref{case:proof:fv_decomp:base_case:negation}.
In summary, this completes the proof of \cref{lemma:fv}.
<!-- end proof-6 -->
<!-- claim-8: body_md -->
\label{claim:fv_agg_M}
  Let \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}\) be a \(\paren*{ \sigma, \ensuremath{\mathbf{W}} }\)-structure, and
  let \(\bar{a} \in A^k\)
  with \(\distS{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}{{\myvec{a}}_{I};{\myvec{a}}_{K\setminus I}} >
  \tilde{r}\).
  \\
  If \(J \cap I \neq \emptyset\),
  then, for all \(\bar{b} \in M^\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}_{\bar{a},1}\)
  we have \(\distS{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}{{\myvec{a}}_{I}\bar{b}; {\myvec{a}}_{K \setminus I}}
  > r'\).
  \\
  If \(J \setminus I \neq \emptyset\),
  then, for all \(\bar{b} \in M^\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}_{\bar{a},2}\)
  we have \(\distS{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}{{\myvec{a}}_{I}; {\myvec{a}}_{K \setminus I}\bar{b}}
  > r'\).
  \\
  If \(J \cap I \neq \emptyset\) and \(J \setminus I \neq \emptyset\),
  then we have \(M^\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}_{\bar{a}} = M^\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}_{\bar{a},1} \uplus M^\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}_{\bar{a},2}\).
<!-- end claim-8 -->
<!-- claim-9: body_md -->
\label{claim:fv_agg_one}
  \(\psi(\bar{x}_K)\; \und\; \operatorname{dist}({\myvec{x}}_{I}; {\myvec{x}}_{K\setminus I})
  \,{>}\,\tilde{r}
  \ \ \equiv
  \ \ \hat{\psi}({\myvec{x}}_{K}) \;\und\; \operatorname{dist}({\myvec{x}}_{I};{\myvec{x}}_{K\setminus I})
  \,{>}\,\tilde{r}\)\,.
<!-- end claim-9 -->

  we can compute a finite, non-empty set $\Delta_t$ of pairs of formulas
  $\big(\alpha(\bar{x}_I),\beta(\bar{x}_{K\setminus I})\big)$
  such that $\alpha(\bar{x}_I)\in\operatorname{L} ({q{+}1}, {k}, {d})$,
  $\beta(\bar{x}_{K\setminus I})\in\operatorname{L} ({q{+}1}, {k}, {d})$, their quantifier
  rank is $\leqslant \operatorname*{qr}(\varrho_t)$, and
  \[
    \varrho_t\paren{{\vec{x}}_{K}}
    \mathbin{\mathchoice{\ }{}{}{} \land \mathchoice{\ }{}{}{}}
    \operatorname{dist}({\vec{x}}_{I}; {\vec{x}}_{K \setminus I})> \tilde{r}
    ~~ \equiv ~~
    \Oder_{\mathclap{\paren{\alpha,\beta} \in \Delta_t}}
    ~~
    \paren[\Big]{
      \alpha \paren{{\vec{x}}_{I}}
      \und

    \mathbin{\mathchoice{\ }{}{}{} \land \mathchoice{\ }{}{}{}}

  \]

  we can compute a finite, non-empty set $\altDelta_t$ of pairs of formulas
  $\big(\alpha(\bar{x}_I),\beta(\bar{x}_{K\setminus I})\big)$
  such that $\alpha(\bar{x}_I)\in\operatorname{L} ({q{+}1}, {k}, {d})$,
  $\beta(\bar{x}_{K\setminus I})\in\operatorname{L} ({q{+}1}, {k}, {d})$, their quantifier

    ~~ \equiv ~~

<!-- proof-7: body_md -->
We proceed by induction on \(q\).

For the induction base with \(q = 0\),
consider arbitrary \(k, d \in \mathbb{N}\),
and consider an arbitrary \(\varphi \in \ensuremath{\ensuremath{\@empty{F}}(0,k,d)}\).
If \(k \geqslant 1\),
then \(\ensuremath{\ensuremath{\@empty{F}}(0,k,d)} = \operatorname{L} ({0}, {k}, {d})\),
and we are done by choosing \(\varphi' \coloneqq \varphi\).
If \(k = 0\),
then \(\varphi\) is a Boolean combination
of \emph{atomic} formulas \(\psi\) in \(\ensuremath{\ensuremath{\ensuremath{\@empty{ngFOW}}}^+}\)
with \(\operatorname*{free}(\psi) = \emptyset\),
so \(\varphi\) is a Boolean combination of sentences in \(\operatorname{S} ({0}, {k}, {d})\)
and we are done by choosing \(\varphi' \coloneqq \varphi\).

For the induction step from \(q\) to \(q{+}1\),
consider arbitrary \(q, d \in\mathbb{N}\).
The induction hypothesis states that the assertion of the theorem
holds for all \(k' \in \mathbb{N}\) and all formulas in~\(\ensuremath{\ensuremath{\@empty{F}}(q,k',d)}\).
Now consider an arbitrary \(k \in \mathbb{N}\)
and a formula \(\varphi \in \ensuremath{\ensuremath{\@empty{F}}(q{+}1,k,d)}\);
in particular, \(\operatorname*{free}(\varphi) \subseteq \ensuremath{\{x_1, \dots, x_k\}}\).
For the remainder of this proof,
let
\begin{equation}\label{eq:GNF-choice-of-r}
  \tilde{r}\ \coloneqq\ \operatorname{r} ({q{+}1}, {k}, {d})
  \quad\text{and} \quad
  r'\ \coloneqq\ \operatorname{r} ({q}, {k{+}\wmaxar}, {d})
  \quad\text{and}\quad
  \hat{r} \ \coloneqq \ \tilde{r}-r'.
\end{equation}
By the definition of \(\ensuremath{\ensuremath{\@empty{F}}(q{+}1,k,d)}\),
it suffices to consider the following 5 cases.

            \topsep 8\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus4\p@
            \itemsep \parsep\@xpt{13} 
   \abovedisplayskip 10\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus5\p@
   \abovedisplayshortskip \z@ \@plus3\p@
   \belowdisplayshortskip 6\p@ \@plus3\p@ \@minus3\p@
   \belowdisplayskip \abovedisplayskip
   \let\leftmargin\leftmargini
            \parsep\z@ \@plus\p@
            \topsep 8\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus4\p@
            \itemsep \parsep\leftmargin\leftmargini
            \parsep\z@ \@plus\p@
            \topsep 8\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus4\p@
            \itemsep \parsep\@xpt{13} 
   \abovedisplayskip 10\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus5\p@
   \abovedisplayshortskip \z@ \@plus3\p@
   \belowdisplayshortskip 6\p@ \@plus3\p@ \@minus3\p@
   \belowdisplayskip \abovedisplayskip
   \let\leftmargin\leftmargini
            \parsep\z@ \@plus\p@
            \topsep 8\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus4\p@
            \itemsep \parsep\leftmargin\leftmargini
            \parsep\z@ \@plus\p@
            \topsep 8\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus4\p@
            \itemsep \parsep\bfseries}{Case~(\ref{def:fparam:previous}):}
\(\varphi \in \ensuremath{\ensuremath{\@empty{F}}(q,k{+}\wmaxar,d)}\).
By the induction hypothesis,
we obtain an equivalent formula \(\varphi'\)
with \(\operatorname*{free}(\varphi') = \operatorname*{free}(\varphi)\)
such that \(\varphi'\) is a Boolean combination of formulas
in \(\operatorname{S} ({q}, {k{+}\wmaxar}, {d}) \cup \operatorname{L} ({q}, {k{+}\wmaxar}, {d})\),
and the outer (inner) quantifier rank of \(\varphi'\)
is at most \(\operatorname*{qr}(\varphi)\) (resp. \(\operatorname*{qr}(\varphi){-}1\)).
Since \(\operatorname*{free}(\varphi') = \operatorname*{free}(\varphi) \subseteq \ensuremath{\{x_1, \dots, x_k\}}\),
by the definition of \(\operatorname{S} ({q{+}1}, {k}, {d})\) and \(\operatorname{L} ({q{+}1}, {k}, {d})\),
we obtain that \(\varphi'\) is a Boolean combination
of formulas in \(\operatorname{S} ({q{+}1}, {k}, {d}) \cup \operatorname{L} ({q{+}1}, {k}, {d})\),
and we are done.

            \parsep\z@ \@plus\p@

            \topsep 8\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus4\p@
            \itemsep \parsep\bfseries}{Case~(\ref{def:fparam:distance_atom}):}
\(\varphi\) is of the form \(\operatorname{dist}(x_i,x_j) \,{\leqslant}\, \tilde{d}\)
with \(\tilde{d} \leqslant \tilde{r}\) and \(i,j \in [k]\).
Then \(\varphi \in \operatorname{L} ({q{+}1}, {k}, {d})\),
and we are done by choosing \(\varphi' \coloneqq \varphi\).

\@startsection{subparagraph}{5}{\z@} 
                                       {3.25ex \@plus1ex \@minus .2ex}

   \abovedisplayskip 10\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus5\p@

   \belowdisplayshortskip 6\p@ \@plus3\p@ \@minus3\p@
   \belowdisplayskip \abovedisplayskip
   \let\@listi\@listI\@xpt{13} 
   \abovedisplayskip 10\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus5\p@
   \abovedisplayshortskip \z@ \@plus3\p@
   \belowdisplayshortskip 6\p@ \@plus3\p@ \@minus3\p@

            \parsep\z@ \@plus\p@
            \topsep 8\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus4\p@
            \itemsep \parsep\@listi\@xpt{13} 
   \abovedisplayskip 10\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus5\p@
   \abovedisplayshortskip \z@ \@plus3\p@
   \belowdisplayshortskip 6\p@ \@plus3\p@ \@minus3\p@
   \belowdisplayskip \abovedisplayskip
   \let\leftmargin\leftmargini
            \parsep\z@ \@plus\p@
            \topsep 8\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus4\p@
            \itemsep \parsep\leftmargin\leftmargini
            \parsep\z@ \@plus\p@
            \topsep 8\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus4\p@
            \itemsep \parsep\@xpt{13} 
   \abovedisplayskip 10\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus5\p@
   \abovedisplayshortskip \z@ \@plus3\p@
   \belowdisplayshortskip 6\p@ \@plus3\p@ \@minus3\p@
   \belowdisplayskip \abovedisplayskip
   \let\leftmargin\leftmargini
            \parsep\z@ \@plus\p@
            \topsep 8\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus4\p@
            \itemsep \parsep\leftmargin\leftmargini
            \parsep\z@ \@plus\p@
            \topsep 8\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus4\p@
            \itemsep \parsep\@xpt{13} 
   \abovedisplayskip 10\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus5\p@
   \abovedisplayshortskip \z@ \@plus3\p@
   \belowdisplayshortskip 6\p@ \@plus3\p@ \@minus3\p@
   \belowdisplayskip \abovedisplayskip
   \let\leftmargin\leftmargini
            \parsep\z@ \@plus\p@
            \topsep 8\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus4\p@
            \itemsep \parsep\leftmargin\leftmargini
            \parsep\z@ \@plus\p@
            \topsep 8\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus4\p@
            \itemsep \parsep\@xpt{13} 
   \abovedisplayskip 10\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus5\p@
   \abovedisplayshortskip \z@ \@plus3\p@
   \belowdisplayshortskip 6\p@ \@plus3\p@ \@minus3\p@
   \belowdisplayskip \abovedisplayskip
   \let\leftmargin\leftmargini
            \parsep\z@ \@plus\p@
            \topsep 8\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus4\p@
            \itemsep \parsep\leftmargin\leftmargini
            \parsep\z@ \@plus\p@
            \topsep 8\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus4\p@
            \itemsep \parsep\@xpt{13} 
   \abovedisplayskip 10\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus5\p@
   \abovedisplayshortskip \z@ \@plus3\p@
   \belowdisplayshortskip 6\p@ \@plus3\p@ \@minus3\p@
   \belowdisplayskip \abovedisplayskip
   \let\leftmargin\leftmargini
            \parsep\z@ \@plus\p@
            \topsep 8\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus4\p@
            \itemsep \parsep\leftmargin\leftmargini
            \parsep\z@ \@plus\p@
            \topsep 8\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus4\p@
            \itemsep \parsep\bfseries}{Case (\ref{def:fparam:existential_with_distance}):}
\(\varphi\) is of the form
\(\exists x_{k+1}\bigl(\operatorname{dist}(x_i,x_{k+1}) \,{\leqslant}\, \hat{d} \land \varphi_1\bigr)\)
with \(\varphi_1 \in \ensuremath{\ensuremath{\@empty{F}}(q,k{+}\wmaxar,d)}\),
\(i \in [k]\),
\(\hat{d} \leqslant \hat{r}\),
and
\(\operatorname*{free}(\varphi_1) \subseteq \operatorname*{free}(\varphi) \cup \ensuremath{\{x_{k+1}\}}
\subseteq \ensuremath{\{x_1, \dots, x_k, x_{k+1}\}}\).

By applying the induction hypothesis to \(\varphi_1\),
we obtain an equivalent formula \(\varphi_1'\)
with \(\operatorname*{free}(\varphi_1') = \operatorname*{free}(\varphi_1)\)
such that \(\varphi_1'\) is a Boolean combination of formulas
in \(\operatorname{S} ({q}, {k{+}\wmaxar}, {d}) \cup \operatorname{L} ({q}, {k{+}\wmaxar}, {d})\),
the outer quantifier rank of \(\varphi'_1\) is \(\leqslant \operatorname*{qr}(\varphi_1)=\operatorname*{qr}(\varphi)-1\),
and the inner quantifier rank of \(\varphi'\) is
\(\leqslant \operatorname*{qr}(\varphi_1)-1 \leqslant \operatorname*{qr}(\varphi)-2\).
We transfer \(\varphi_1'\) into disjunctive normal form
in order to obtain an equivalent formula of the form
\(\ensuremath{\bigvee}_{j = 1}^n \bigl(\chi_j \land \lambda_j\bigr)\),
where \(n \geqslant 1\) and,
for all \(j \in \brac{n}\),
\(\chi_j\) is a Boolean combination of sentences in
\(\operatorname{S} ({q}, {k{+}\wmaxar}, {d})\) of inner quantifier rank \(\leqslant \operatorname*{qr}(\varphi)-2\),
and \(\lambda_j \in \operatorname{L} ({q}, {k{+}\wmaxar}, {d})\) with
\(\operatorname*{free}(\lambda_j) \subseteq \operatorname*{free}(\varphi_1')\)
and \(\operatorname*{qr}(\lambda_j) \leqslant \operatorname*{qr}(\varphi)-1\).

Then, \(\varphi\) is equivalent to
\(
  \exists x_{k+1} \Bigl(
    \operatorname{dist}(x_i,x_{k+1}) \,{\leqslant}\, \hat{d}
    \,\land\,
    \ensuremath{\bigvee}_{j = 1}^n \bigl(\chi_j \land \lambda_j\bigr)
  \Bigr),
\)
which, in turn, is equivalent to
\(
  \varphi' \coloneqq \ensuremath{\bigvee}_{j = 1}^n
  \Bigl(\chi_j\, \land\, \exists x_{k+1}
    \bigl(\operatorname{dist}(x_i,x_{k+1}) \,{\leqslant}\, \hat{d}\, \land \lambda_j\bigr)
  \Bigr)
\).
By definition,
\(\operatorname{S} ({q}, {k{+}\wmaxar}, {d}) \subseteq \operatorname{S} ({q{+}1}, {k}, {d})\).
Furthermore,
for each \(j \in [n]\),
\(\exists x_{k+1}\bigl(\operatorname{dist}(x_i,x_{k+1}) \,{\leqslant}\, \hat{d}
\,\land \lambda_j\bigr)\)
is a formula in \(\operatorname{L} ({q{+}1}, {k}, {d})\)
and of quantifier rank \(\leqslant \operatorname*{qr}(\varphi)\).
Hence, \(\varphi'\) has the desired properties, and we are done.

   \belowdisplayshortskip 6\p@ \@plus3\p@ \@minus3\p@
   \belowdisplayskip \abovedisplayskip
   \let\@listi\@listI\@xpt{13} 
   \abovedisplayskip 10\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus5\p@
   \abovedisplayshortskip \z@ \@plus3\p@
   \belowdisplayshortskip 6\p@ \@plus3\p@ \@minus3\p@
   \belowdisplayskip \abovedisplayskip
   \let\leftmargin\leftmargini
            \parsep\z@ \@plus\p@
            \topsep 8\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus4\p@
            \itemsep \parsep\@listi\@xpt{13} 
   \abovedisplayskip 10\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus5\p@
   \abovedisplayshortskip \z@ \@plus3\p@
   \belowdisplayshortskip 6\p@ \@plus3\p@ \@minus3\p@
   \belowdisplayskip \abovedisplayskip
   \let\leftmargin\leftmargini
            \parsep\z@ \@plus\p@
            \topsep 8\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus4\p@
            \itemsep \parsep\leftmargin\leftmargini
            \parsep\z@ \@plus\p@
            \topsep 8\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus4\p@
            \itemsep \parsep\@xpt{13} 
   \abovedisplayskip 10\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus5\p@
   \abovedisplayshortskip \z@ \@plus3\p@
   \belowdisplayshortskip 6\p@ \@plus3\p@ \@minus3\p@
   \belowdisplayskip \abovedisplayskip
   \let\leftmargin\leftmargini
            \parsep\z@ \@plus\p@
            \topsep 8\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus4\p@
            \itemsep \parsep\leftmargin\leftmargini
            \parsep\z@ \@plus\p@
            \topsep 8\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus4\p@
            \itemsep \parsep\@xpt{13} 
   \abovedisplayskip 10\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus5\p@
   \abovedisplayshortskip \z@ \@plus3\p@
   \belowdisplayshortskip 6\p@ \@plus3\p@ \@minus3\p@
   \belowdisplayskip \abovedisplayskip
   \let\leftmargin\leftmargini
            \parsep\z@ \@plus\p@
            \topsep 8\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus4\p@
            \itemsep \parsep\leftmargin\leftmargini
            \parsep\z@ \@plus\p@
            \topsep 8\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus4\p@
            \itemsep \parsep\@xpt{13} 
   \abovedisplayskip 10\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus5\p@
   \abovedisplayshortskip \z@ \@plus3\p@
   \belowdisplayshortskip 6\p@ \@plus3\p@ \@minus3\p@
   \belowdisplayskip \abovedisplayskip
   \let\leftmargin\leftmargini
            \parsep\z@ \@plus\p@
            \topsep 8\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus4\p@
            \itemsep \parsep\leftmargin\leftmargini
            \parsep\z@ \@plus\p@
            \topsep 8\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus4\p@
            \itemsep \parsep\@xpt{13} 
   \abovedisplayskip 10\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus5\p@
   \abovedisplayshortskip \z@ \@plus3\p@
   \belowdisplayshortskip 6\p@ \@plus3\p@ \@minus3\p@
   \belowdisplayskip \abovedisplayskip
   \let\leftmargin\leftmargini
            \parsep\z@ \@plus\p@
            \topsep 8\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus4\p@
            \itemsep \parsep\leftmargin\leftmargini
            \parsep\z@ \@plus\p@
            \topsep 8\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus4\p@
            \itemsep \parsep\bfseries}{Case~(\ref{def:fparam:existential_plain}):}
\(\varphi\) is of the form \(\exists x_{k+1}\,\varphi_1\) with
\(\varphi_1 \in \ensuremath{\ensuremath{\@empty{F}}(q,k{+}\wmaxar,d)}\) and
\(\operatorname*{free}(\varphi_1) \subseteq \operatorname*{free}(\varphi) \cup \ensuremath{\{x_{k+1}\}}
\subseteq \ensuremath{\{x_1, \dots, x_k, x_{k+1}\}}\).

By applying the induction hypothesis to \(\varphi_1\),
we obtain an equivalent formula \(\varphi_1'\)
with \(\operatorname*{free}(\varphi_1') = \operatorname*{free}(\varphi_1)\)
such that \(\varphi_1'\) is a Boolean combination of formulas
in \(\operatorname{S} ({q}, {k{+}\wmaxar}, {d}) \cup \operatorname{L} ({q}, {k{+}\wmaxar}, {d})\),
the outer quantifier rank of \(\varphi_1'\) is \(\leqslant \operatorname*{qr}(\varphi_1)=\operatorname*{qr}(\varphi)-1\),
and the inner quantifier rank of \(\varphi'\) is
\(\leqslant \operatorname*{qr}(\varphi_1)-1 \leqslant \operatorname*{qr}(\varphi)-2\).
We transfer \(\varphi_1'\) into disjunctive normal form
in order to obtain an equivalent formula of the form
\(\ensuremath{\bigvee}_{j = 1}^n \bigl(\chi_j \land \lambda_j\bigr)\),
where \(n \geqslant 1\) and,
for all \(j \in \brac{n}\),
\(\chi_j\) is a Boolean combination of sentences in
\(\operatorname{S} ({q}, {k{+}\wmaxar}, {d})\) of inner quantifier rank \(\leqslant \operatorname*{qr}(\varphi)-2\),
and \(\lambda_j \in \operatorname{L} ({q}, {k{+}\wmaxar}, {d})\) with
\(\operatorname*{free}(\lambda_j) \subseteq \operatorname*{free}(\varphi_1')\)
and \(\operatorname*{qr}(\lambda_j) \leqslant \operatorname*{qr}(\varphi)-1\).

Then, \(\varphi\) is equivalent to
\(\exists x_{k+1} \ensuremath{\bigvee}_{j = 1}^n \bigl(\chi_j \land \lambda_j\bigr)\),
which, in turn, is equivalent to
\(\ensuremath{\bigvee}_{j = 1}^n \bigl(\chi_j \land \exists x_{k+1} \lambda_j\bigr)\).
By definition,
\(\operatorname{S} ({q}, {k{+}\wmaxar}, {d}) \subseteq \operatorname{S} ({q{+}1}, {k}, {d})\).
Therefore, all that remains to be done is to transform,
for each \(j \in [n]\),
the formula \(\exists x_{k+1} \lambda_j\) into an equivalent formula
that is a Boolean combination of formulas in
\(\operatorname{S} ({q{+}1}, {k}, {d}) \cup \operatorname{L} ({q{+}1}, {k}, {d})\)
and that has outer (inner) quantifier rank at most
\(\operatorname*{qr}(\varphi)\) (resp.~\(\operatorname*{qr}(\varphi)-1\)).
Consider an arbitrary \(j \in [n]\),
and let \(\lambda \coloneqq \lambda_j\).
Note that
\(\operatorname*{free}(\lambda) \subseteq \operatorname*{free}(\varphi_1)
\subseteq \operatorname*{free}(\varphi) \cup \ensuremath{\{x_{k+1}\}}\)
and \(\operatorname*{qr}(\lambda) \leqslant \operatorname*{qr}(\varphi)-1\).

If \(\operatorname*{free}(\varphi) = \emptyset\),
then \(\operatorname*{free}(\lambda) \subseteq \ensuremath{\{x_{k+1}\}}\).
Thus, in this case,
\(\exists x_{k+1}\lambda(x_{k+1})\) is a sentence in \(\operatorname{S} ({q{+}1}, {k}, {d})\)
of inner quantifier rank at most \(\operatorname*{qr}(\varphi)-1\),
and we are done.\@gobble{In fact, \(\exists x_{k+1}\lambda(x_{k+1})\)
  is a basic local sentence of width \(\ell=1\),
  which is why the assertion on the distance
  of the quantified variables vanishes.}
Henceforth, we consider the case that
\(\operatorname*{free}(\varphi) \neq \emptyset\);
in particular this means that \(k \geqslant 1\).
Let \(k'\coloneqq k{+}\wmaxar\), \(\bar{x} \coloneqq (x_1, \dots, x_{k'})\),
and \(I \coloneqq \ensuremath{\ensuremath{\{i \in [k] : x_i \in \operatorname*{free}(\varphi)\}}}\).

Clearly, \(\exists x_{k+1} \lambda\) is equivalent to
\(\psi \lor \ensuremath{\bigvee}_{i \in I} \vartheta_i\)
with \(\vartheta_i \coloneqq \exists x_{k+1}
\bigl(\operatorname{dist}(x_i,x_{k+1}) \,{\leqslant}\, r'\, \land \lambda\bigr)\) for all \(i \in I\),
and \(\psi \coloneqq \exists x_{k+1}
\bigl(\operatorname{dist}(\bar{x}_I;x_{k+1}) \, {>} \, r'\, \land \lambda\bigr)\).
By definition, for every \(i \in I\),
we have \(\vartheta_i \in \operatorname{L} ({q{+}1}, {k}, {d})\).
Hence, all that remains to be done is to transform \(\psi\)
into an equivalent formula that is a Boolean combination of formulas
in \(\operatorname{S} ({q{+}1}, {k}, {d}) \cup \operatorname{L} ({q{+}1}, {k}, {d})\)
and that has outer (inner) quantifier rank at most
\(\operatorname*{qr}(\varphi)\) (resp.~\(\operatorname*{qr}(\varphi)-1\)).

By applying \cref{lemma:fv} with \(K \coloneqq I \cup \ensuremath{\{k{+}1\}}\),
we obtain a finite and non-empty set \(\Delta\) of pairs of formulas
\(\bigl(\alpha(\bar x_I),\beta(x_{k+1})\big)\) such that
\(\alpha(\bar x_I) \in \operatorname{L} ({q}, {k'}, {d})\),
\(\beta(x_{k+1}) \in \operatorname{L} ({q}, {k'}, {d})\),
they have quantifier rank \(\leqslant \operatorname*{qr}(\lambda) \leqslant \operatorname*{qr}(\varphi)-1\),
and
\[
  \lambda(\bar{x}_K) \; \land\; \operatorname{dist}(\bar{x}_I;x_{k+1})\,{>}\,r'
  ~~ \equiv ~~
  \ensuremath{\bigvee}_{(\alpha,\beta) \in \Delta} \Bigl(
    \alpha(\bar x_I) \land \beta(x_{k+1})
  \Bigr)
  \;\land\;
  \operatorname{dist}(\bar{x}_I;x_{k+1})\,{>}\,r'.
\]
This implies that \(\psi\) is equivalent to
\(\ensuremath{\bigvee}_{(\alpha,\beta) \in \Delta}\Bigl(
  \alpha(\bar{x}_I) \land \exists x_{k+1}\bigl(
    \operatorname{dist}(\bar{x}_I;x_{k+1})\,{>}\,r' \land \beta(x_{k+1})
  \bigr)
\Bigr)\).

Note that \(\alpha(\bar{x}_I) \in \operatorname{L} ({q{+}1}, {k}, {d})\),
and all that remains to be done is to consider an arbitrary \(\beta\)
such that \((\alpha, \beta) \in \Delta\) for some \(\alpha\),
and transform the formula
\[
  \mu(\bar{x}_I)
  ~~ \coloneqq ~~
  \exists x_{k+1} \bigl(
   \operatorname{dist}(\bar{x}_I;x_{k+1})\,{>}\, r' \ \land \ \beta(x_{k+1})
  \bigr)
\]
into an equivalent formula \(\mu'(\bar{x}_I)\)
that is a Boolean combination of formulas
in \(\operatorname{S} ({q{+}1}, {k}, {d}) \cup \operatorname{L} ({q{+}1}, {k}, {d})\)
and that has outer (inner) quantifier rank at most
\(\operatorname*{qr}(\varphi)\) (resp.~\(\operatorname*{qr}(\varphi)-1\)).
Recall that \(\beta(x_{k+1}) \in \operatorname{L} ({q}, {k'}, {d})\)
with \(k'=k{+}\wmaxar\),
and \(\operatorname*{qr}(\beta) \leqslant \operatorname*{qr}(\varphi)-1\).

For constructing \(\mu'(\bar{x}_I)\), we use the following formulas:
\begin{itemize}
  \item
    \(\displaystyle
    \delta_i(\bar{x}_I) \coloneqq
    \exists x_{k+1} \bigl(
      \operatorname{dist}(x_i,x_{k+1}) \,{\leqslant}\, \hat{r}
      \mathbin{\mathchoice{\ }{}{}{} \land \mathchoice{\ }{}{}{}}
      \operatorname{dist}(\bar{x}_I; \bar{x}_{k+1})\, {>} \, r'
      \mathbin{\mathchoice{\ }{}{}{} \land \mathchoice{\ }{}{}{}}
      \beta(x_{k+1})
    \bigr)\),
    for all \(i \in I\),
  \item
    \(\displaystyle
    \psi_\ell^{(c)}(\bar{x}_I) \coloneqq
    \ensuremath{\bigvee}_{\substack{J \subseteq I,\\ \left\lvertJ\right\rvert = \ell}}
    \paren[\Big]{
      \ensuremath{\bigwedge}_{\substack{j,j' \in J,\\ j \neq j'}}
      \!\!\!\operatorname{dist}(x_j,x_{j'}) \, {>}\, 4cr'
      \; \land \;
      \ensuremath{\bigwedge}_{j \in J} \exists x_{k+1} \,
      \paren[\big]{
        \operatorname{dist}(x_j,x_{k+1}) \,{\leqslant}\, r' \mathbin{\mathchoice{\ }{}{}{} \land \mathchoice{\ }{}{}{}} \beta(x_{k+1})
      }
    }\),
    for all \(\ell, c \in [\left\lvertI\right\rvert]\), and
  \item
    \(\displaystyle
    \xi_\ell^{(c)} \coloneqq
    \exists y_1 \cdots \exists y_\ell\Big(\!
      \ensuremath{\bigwedge}_{1 \leqslant j < j' \leqslant \ell}
      \!\!\! \operatorname{dist}(y_j,y_{j'})\,{>}\,2(2c{+}1)r'
      \land
      \ensuremath{\bigwedge}_{j \in [\ell]}\!\beta(y_j)
    \Big)\),
    for all \(\ell \in[\left\lvertI\right\rvert{+}1]\) and \(c \in[0, \left\lvertI\right\rvert]\).
\end{itemize}

Note that \(\delta_i(\bar{x}_I) \in \operatorname{L} ({q{+}1}, {k}, {d})\) for all \(i \in I\),
and
\(\xi_\ell^{(c)} \in \operatorname{S} ({q{+}1}, {k}, {d})\)
for all \(\ell \in[\left\lvertI\right\rvert{+}1]\) and \(c \in [0,\left\lvertI\right\rvert]\).
Furthermore,
for all \(c \in [\left\lvertI\right\rvert]\), we have \(c \leqslant k\),
so \(4cr' \leqslant \tilde{r}\) by \cref{eq:def:radius_function}.
Thus, the formula \(\operatorname{dist}(x_j,x_{j'}) \,{\leqslant}\, 4cr'\) and its negation
\(\operatorname{dist}(x_j, x_{j'}) \,{>}\, 4cr'\) belong to \(\operatorname{L} ({q{+}1}, {k}, {d})\).
Moreover, \(r' \leqslant 4kr' - r' = \tilde{r} - r' = \hat{r}\),
so the formula
\(\exists x_{k+1} \, \paren[\big]{
  \operatorname{dist}(x_j,x_{k+1}) \,{\leqslant}\, r' \mathbin{\mathchoice{\ }{}{}{} \land \mathchoice{\ }{}{}{}} \beta(x_{k+1})
}\)
belongs to \(\operatorname{L} ({q{+}1}, {k}, {d})\).
This shows that
\(\psi_\ell^{(c)}(\bar{x}_I) \in \operatorname{L} ({q{+}1}, {k}, {d})\)
for all \(\ell, c\in [\left\lvertI\right\rvert]\).
We let
\[
  \mu'(\bar x_I) \quad \coloneqq \quad
  \xi^{(0)}_{\left\lvertI\right\rvert+1} \mathbin{\mathchoice{\ }{}{}{} \lor \mathchoice{\ }{}{}{}} \ensuremath{\bigvee}_{c,\ell \in [\left\lvertI\right\rvert]} \paren[\Big]{
    \xi^{(c)}_\ell \mathbin{\mathchoice{\ }{}{}{} \land \mathchoice{\ }{}{}{}} \neg\,\xi^{(c{-}1)}_{\ell+1}
    \mathbin{\mathchoice{\ }{}{}{} \land \mathchoice{\ }{}{}{}}
    \paren[\big]{
      \ensuremath{\bigvee}_{i\in I} \delta_i(\bar x_I)
      \mathbin{\mathchoice{\ }{}{}{} \lor \mathchoice{\ }{}{}{}}
      \neg\,\psi^{(c)}_\ell(\bar x_I)
    }
  }.
\]
Clearly, \(\mu'\) is a Boolean combination of formulas in
\(\operatorname{S} ({q{+}1}, {k}, {d}) \cup \operatorname{L} ({q{+}1}, {k}, {d})\),
\(\operatorname*{free}(\mu') = \ensuremath{\ensuremath{\{x_i : i \in I\}}} = \operatorname*{free}(\varphi)\),
and \(\mu'\) has outer quantifier rank \(\leqslant \operatorname*{qr}(\varphi)\)
and inner quantifier rank \(\leqslant \operatorname*{qr}(\varphi)-1\).
All that remains to be done is to show that \(\mu'(\bar x_I)\)
is equivalent to \(\mu(\bar x_I)\).
To this end,
consider an arbitrary \((\sigma,\ensuremath{\mathbf{W}})\)-structure \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}\)
and a tuple \(\bar{a} \in A^k\).
We have to show that
\(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \models \mu' (\bar{a}_I) \iff \ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \models \mu (\bar{a}_I)\).

In the proof, the following observation will be used twice.

\distanceByDoubleTriangle*

\begin{claimproof}
  By applying the triangle inequality twice, we obtain
  \(
    s + 2t
    <
    \distS{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}{d_1, d_2}
    \leqslant
    \distS{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}{d_1, c_1} +
    \distS{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}{c_1, c_2} +
    \distS{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}{c_2, d_2}
    \leqslant
    t + \distS{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}{c_1, c_2} + t
  \).
  Rearranging the inequality, we get the desired result
  \(\distS{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}{c_1, c_2} > s + 2t - 2t = s\).
\end{claimproof}

We now focus on proving that
\(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \models \mu' (\bar{a}_I) \iff \ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \models \mu (\bar{a}_I)\).
Throughout the remainder of this proof,
we let \(B \coloneqq \ensuremath{\ensuremath{\{b \in A : \ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \models \beta (b)\}}}\).

            \itemsep \parsep\@listi\@xpt{13}

   \let\leftmargin\leftmargini

   \abovedisplayskip 10\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus5\p@
   \abovedisplayshortskip \z@ \@plus3\p@
   \belowdisplayshortskip 6\p@ \@plus3\p@ \@minus3\p@
   \belowdisplayskip \abovedisplayskip
   \let\leftmargin\leftmargini
            \parsep\z@ \@plus\p@
            \topsep 8\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus4\p@
            \itemsep \parsep\leftmargin\leftmargini
            \parsep\z@ \@plus\p@
            \topsep 8\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus4\p@
            \itemsep \parsep\bfseries}{Case~(\ref{def:fparam:existential_plain}).1:}
\(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \models \xi^{(0)}_{\left\lvertI\right\rvert+1}\).
Then, \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \models \mu' (\bar{a}_I)\).
We have to show that \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \models \mu (\bar{a}_I)\).

Since \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \models \xi^{(0)}_{\left\lvertI\right\rvert+1}\),
there exist \( b_1, \dots, b_{\left\lvertI\right\rvert+1}  \in B\) of pairwise distance \(> 2r'\).
Let \(\pi\) be a mapping that maps every
\(b \in \ensuremath{\{ b_1, \dots, b_{\left\lvertI\right\rvert+1} \}}\)
to an element \(a \in \ensuremath{\ensuremath{\{a_i : i \in I\}}}\) that is closest to \(b\),
that is, an element that minimises \(\distS{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}{b,a}\),
where ties are broken arbitrarily.
Then, by the pigeonhole principle,
there are two distinct \(j, j' \in \brac{\left\lvertI\right\rvert{+}1}\)
with \(\pi(b_j) = \pi(b_{j'}) = a_i\) for some \(i \in I\).
Since \(\distS{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}{b_j, b_{j'}} > 2r'\),
by the triangle inequality,
we know that \(\distS{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}{a_i, b_j} > r'\)
or \(\distS{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}{a_i, b_{j'}} > r'\).
Say, w.l.o.g., \(\distS{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}{a_i, b_j} > r'\).
By the choice of \(\pi\),
we obtain that \(\distS{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}{\bar{a}_I; b_j} > r'\).
This \(b_j \in B\) witnesses that \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \models \mu (\bar{a})\).

\(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \not\models \xi^{(0)}_1\).
Then, there does not exist any \(b \in A\) such that
\(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \models \beta (b)\), so \(B = \emptyset\).
Hence, \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \not\models \mu (\bar{a}_I)\).
Furthermore, \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \not\models \xi^{(0)}_{\left\lvertI\right\rvert+1}\) and, moreover,
for all \(c, \ell \in \brac{\left\lvertI\right\rvert}\),
we have that \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \not\models \xi^{(c)}_\ell\).
Thus, \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \not\models \mu' (\bar{a}_I)\),
so we have that \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \not\models \mu' (\bar{a}_I)\)
and \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \not\models \mu (\bar{a}_I)\).

We are neither in
Case~(\ref{def:fparam:existential_plain}).1
nor in
Case~(\ref{def:fparam:existential_plain}).2.
Then, we have that
\(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \models \xi^{(0)}_1 \land \neg \xi^{(0)}_{\left\lvertI\right\rvert+1}\),
so \(B \neq \emptyset\),
but \(B\) does not contain \(\left\lvertI\right\rvert{+}1\) elements
of pairwise distance \(> 2r'\).

\rpGaifmanScatteredThreshold*
\begin{claimproof}
  For every \(c \in [0,\left\lvertI\right\rvert]\),
  let \(\ell^{(c)} \in \mathbb{N} \cup \ensuremath{\{\infty\}}\) be maximal
  such that there exists a set \(X \subseteq B\) of \(\ell^{\paren{c}}\) elements
  of pairwise distance \(> 2(2c{+}1)r'\).
From \(B \neq \emptyset\),
  we obtain that \(\ell^{\paren{c}} \geqslant 1\) for each \(c \in \brac{0,\left\lvertI\right\rvert}\).
Since $B$ does not contain $\left\lvertI\right\rvert{+}1$ elements of pairwise distance
  $>2r'$, we know that
  \(\ell^{\paren{c}} \leqslant \left\lvertI\right\rvert\) for each \(c \in \brac{0,\left\lvertI\right\rvert}\).
  Furthermore, for all \(c \geqslant 1\), we have \(\ell^{\paren{c}} \leqslant \ell^{(c-1)}\),
  because \(2(2c{+}1)r' \geqslant 2(2(c{-}1){+}1)r'\).
Hence, we have
  $\left\lvertI\right\rvert \geqslant \ell^{\paren{0}} \geqslant \ell^{\paren{1}}  \geqslant
    \cdots \geqslant  \ell^{\paren{\left\lvertI\right\rvert}} \geqslant 1$.
  By the pigeonhole principle,
  there exists a \(c \in \brac{\left\lvertI\right\rvert}\)
  such that \(\ell^{(c-1)} = \ell^{\paren{c}}\).
We choose such a \(c\) and let \(\ell \coloneqq \ell^{\paren{c}}\).
  Since \(\ell = \ell^{\paren{c}}\), we have that \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \models \xi^{(c)}_\ell\).
  Since \(\ell = \ell^{(c-1)}\) and \(\ell^{(c-1)}\) is maximal,
  we have that  \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \models \neg\,\xi^{(c-1)}_{\ell+1}\).
\end{claimproof}
 
Consider arbitrary \(c, \ell \in \brac{\left\lvertI\right\rvert}\)
such that \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \models \xi^{(c)}_\ell \land \neg \xi^{(c-1)}_{\ell+1}\),
which exist due to \cref{claim:proof:rp_gaifman:scattered_threshold}.

\(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \models \delta_i (\bar{a}_I)\) for some \(i \in I\).
This implies that \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \models \mu' (\bar{a}_I)\)
and \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \models \mu (\bar{a}_I)\).

   \abovedisplayshortskip \z@ \@plus3\p@
   \belowdisplayshortskip 6\p@ \@plus3\p@ \@minus3\p@
   \belowdisplayskip \abovedisplayskip
   \let\leftmargin\leftmargini
            \parsep\z@ \@plus\p@
            \topsep 8\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus4\p@
            \itemsep \parsep\leftmargin\leftmargini
            \parsep\z@ \@plus\p@
            \topsep 8\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus4\p@
            \itemsep \parsep\@xpt{13} 
   \abovedisplayskip 10\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus5\p@
   \abovedisplayshortskip \z@ \@plus3\p@
   \belowdisplayshortskip 6\p@ \@plus3\p@ \@minus3\p@
   \belowdisplayskip \abovedisplayskip
   \let\leftmargin\leftmargini
            \parsep\z@ \@plus\p@
            \topsep 8\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus4\p@
            \itemsep \parsep\leftmargin\leftmargini
            \parsep\z@ \@plus\p@
            \topsep 8\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus4\p@
            \itemsep \parsep\bfseries}{Case~(\ref{def:fparam:existential_plain}).3.2:}
\(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \models \neg\,\psi^{(c)}_\ell (\bar{a}_I)\).
Then \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \models \mu' (\bar{a}_I)\).
We have to show that \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \models \mu (\bar{a}_I)\).

From \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \models \xi^{(c)}_\ell\),
we know that there exist \( b_1, \dots, b_\ell  \in B\)
of pairwise distance \(> 2(2c{+}1)r'\).
If there is a \(\nu \in \brac{\ell}\)
such that \(\distS{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}{\bar{a}_I; b_\nu} \,{>}\, r'\),
then \(b_\nu\) serves as a witness certifying that
\(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \models \mu (\bar{a}_I)\).
\emph{For contradiction},
assume that, for each \(\nu \in \brac{\ell}\),
we do \emph{not} have
\(\distS{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}{\bar{a}_I;b_\nu} \,{>}\, r'\).
Then, for every \(\nu \in \brac{\ell}\),
there is an \(i(\nu) \in I\)
such that \(\distS{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}{a_{i(\nu)}, b_\nu} \,{\leqslant}\, r'\).
 
\rpGaifmanPwFarNearElements*
\begin{claimproof}
  For distinct \(\nu, \nu' \in [\ell]\),
  apply \cref{claim:distance_by_double_triangle}
  with \(s = 4cr'\),
  \(t = r'\),
  and \((c_1, c_2, d_1, d_2) = (a_{i(\nu)}, a_{i(\nu')}, b_\nu, b_{\nu'})\).
  This yields
  \(\distS{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}{c_1, c_2} \,{>}\, s\),
  so
  \(\distS{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}{a_{i(\nu)}, a_{i(\nu')}} \,{>}\, 4cr'\).
\end{claimproof}
 
\Cref{claim:proof:rp_gaifman:pw_far_near_elements} implies that
\(i(\nu) \neq i(\nu')\) holds for all distinct \(\nu, \nu' \in [\ell]\).
Thus, for \(J \coloneqq \ensuremath{\ensuremath{\{i(\nu) : \nu\in[\ell]\}}}\),
we have \(\left\lvertJ\right\rvert = \ell\).
Furthermore, for each \(\nu \in \brac{\ell}\),
the element \(b_\nu\) serves as a witness certifying that
\(
(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}},\bar{a}_I) \models \exists x_{k+1}\,\paren[\big]{
  \operatorname{dist}(x_{i(\nu)},x_{k+1}) \,{\leqslant}\, r'
  \mathbin{\mathchoice{\ }{}{}{} \land \mathchoice{\ }{}{}{}}
  \beta(x_{k+1})
}
\).
Combining this with
\cref{claim:proof:rp_gaifman:pw_far_near_elements} proves
that \(J\) serves as a witness certifying that
\(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \models \psi^{(c)}_\ell (\bar{a}_I)\).
However, recall that we currently consider the case that
\(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \models \neg\,\psi^{(c)}_\ell (\bar{a}_I)\),
a contradiction!
Thus, there must exist a \(\nu \in \brac{\ell}\)
such that \(\distS{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}{\bar{a}_I; b_\nu} \,{>}\, r'\).
This \(b_\nu\) serves as a witness certifying that
\(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \models \mu (\bar{a}_I)\).
 
\@startsection{subparagraph}{5}{\z@}

   \@setfontsize
