   \@setfontsize 
   \@setfontsize 
   \@setfontsize 
   \@setfontsize\normalsize\@xpt{13} 
   \abovedisplayskip 10\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus5\p@
   \abovedisplayshortskip \z@ \@plus3\p@
   \belowdisplayshortskip 6\p@ \@plus3\p@ \@minus3\p@
   \belowdisplayskip \abovedisplayskip
   \let\@listi\@listI\@xpt{13} 
   \abovedisplayskip 10\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus5\p@
   \abovedisplayshortskip \z@ \@plus3\p@
   \belowdisplayshortskip 6\p@ \@plus3\p@ \@minus3\p@
   \belowdisplayskip \abovedisplayskip
   \let\leftmargin\leftmargini
            \parsep\z@ \@plus\p@
            \topsep 8\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus4\p@
            \itemsep \parsep\@listi\@xpt{13} 
   \abovedisplayskip 10\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus5\p@
   \abovedisplayshortskip \z@ \@plus3\p@
   \belowdisplayshortskip 6\p@ \@plus3\p@ \@minus3\p@
   \belowdisplayskip \abovedisplayskip
   \let\leftmargin\leftmargini
            \parsep\z@ \@plus\p@
            \topsep 8\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus4\p@
            \itemsep \parsep\leftmargin\leftmargini
            \parsep\z@ \@plus\p@
            \topsep 8\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus4\p@
            \itemsep \parsep\@xpt{13} 
   \abovedisplayskip 10\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus5\p@
   \abovedisplayshortskip \z@ \@plus3\p@
   \belowdisplayshortskip 6\p@ \@plus3\p@ \@minus3\p@
   \belowdisplayskip \abovedisplayskip
   \let\leftmargin\leftmargini
            \parsep\z@ \@plus\p@
            \topsep 8\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus4\p@
            \itemsep \parsep\leftmargin\leftmargini
            \parsep\z@ \@plus\p@
            \topsep 8\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus4\p@
            \itemsep \parsep\@xpt{13} 
   \abovedisplayskip 10\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus5\p@
   \abovedisplayshortskip \z@ \@plus3\p@
   \belowdisplayshortskip 6\p@ \@plus3\p@ \@minus3\p@
   \belowdisplayskip \abovedisplayskip
   \let\leftmargin\leftmargini
            \parsep\z@ \@plus\p@
            \topsep 8\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus4\p@
            \itemsep \parsep\leftmargin\leftmargini
            \parsep\z@ \@plus\p@
            \topsep 8\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus4\p@
            \itemsep \parsep\@xpt{13} 
   \abovedisplayskip 10\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus5\p@
   \abovedisplayshortskip \z@ \@plus3\p@
   \belowdisplayshortskip 6\p@ \@plus3\p@ \@minus3\p@
   \belowdisplayskip \abovedisplayskip
   \let\leftmargin\leftmargini
            \parsep\z@ \@plus\p@
            \topsep 8\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus4\p@
            \itemsep \parsep\leftmargin\leftmargini
            \parsep\z@ \@plus\p@
            \topsep 8\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus4\p@
            \itemsep \parsep\@xpt{13} 
   \abovedisplayskip 10\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus5\p@
   \abovedisplayshortskip \z@ \@plus3\p@
   \belowdisplayshortskip 6\p@ \@plus3\p@ \@minus3\p@
   \belowdisplayskip \abovedisplayskip
   \let\leftmargin\leftmargini
            \parsep\z@ \@plus\p@
            \topsep 8\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus4\p@
            \itemsep \parsep\leftmargin\leftmargini
            \parsep\z@ \@plus\p@
            \topsep 8\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus4\p@
            \itemsep \parsep\bfseries}{Case~(\ref{def:fparam:existential_plain}).3.3:}
For all \(c, \ell \in \brac{\left\lvertI\right\rvert}\)
with \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \models \xi^{(c)}_\ell \land \neg\xi^{(c-1)}_{\ell+1}\),
we have \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \not\models \delta_i (\bar{a}_I)\) for all \(i\in I\),
and \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \models \psi^{(c)}_\ell (\bar{a}_I)\).
This is the only remaining subcase of
Case~(\ref*{def:fparam:existential_plain}).3.
Since we are still in Case~(\ref*{def:fparam:existential_plain}).3,
the assumptions imply \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \not\models\mu' (\bar{a}_I)\).
Hence, we have to show that
\(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \not\models \mu (\bar{a}_I)\).

Let us fix \(c, \ell \in \brac{\left\lvertI\right\rvert}\)
according to \cref{claim:proof:rp_gaifman:scattered_threshold}.
Thus, we have \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \models \xi^{(c)}_\ell \land \neg\xi^{(c-1)}_{\ell+1}\),
\(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \not\models \delta_i (\bar{a})\) for all \(i \in I\),
and
\(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \models \psi^{(c)}_\ell (\bar{a})\).
\emph{For contradiction}, assume that \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \models \mu (\bar{a}_I)\).
Then there exists a \(b_0 \in B\) such that
\(\distS{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}{\bar{a}_I; b_0} \,{>}\, r'\).
Since, for all \(i \in I\),
we have \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \not\models \delta_i (\bar{a}_I)\),
we obtain
\begin{equation}
  \label{eq:proof:rp_gaifman:distance_between_tuple_and_far_element}
  \distS{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}{\bar{a}_I; b_0} > \hat{r}.
\end{equation}
From \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \models \psi^{(c)}_\ell (\bar{a}_I)\),
we obtain that there exists a set \(J \subseteq I\)
with \(\left\lvertJ\right\rvert = \ell\) such that
\begin{equation}
  \label{eq:proof:rp_gaifman:distance_within_tuple}
  \distS{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}{a_j, a_{j'}}
  >
  4cr'
\end{equation}
holds for all distinct \(j, j' \in J\);
and for each \(j \in J\), there exists a \(b_j \in B\) with
\begin{equation}
  \label{eq:proof:rp_gaifman:distance_between_tuple_entry_and_partner}
  \distS{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}{a_j, b_j}
  \leqslant
  r'.
\end{equation}

We would like to use the elements \(b_0, (b_j)_{j \in J}\) as witnesses
certifying that \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \models \xi^{(c-1)}_{\ell+1}\).
All that remains to be done
is to show that they have pairwise distance \(> 2(2(c{-}1){+}1)r'\).
Note that \(2(2(c{-}1){+}1)r'= 4cr'-2r'\).

For distinct \(j, j' \in J\),
we apply \cref{claim:distance_by_double_triangle}
with \(s = 4cr' - 2r'\),
\(t = r'\),
and \((c_1, c_2, d_1, d_2) = (b_j, b_{j'}, a_j, a_{j'})\).
The assumptions of the claim are met due to
\cref{eq:proof:rp_gaifman:distance_within_tuple,eq:proof:rp_gaifman:distance_between_tuple_entry_and_partner}.
This yields
\(\distS{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}{c_1, c_2} \,{>}\, s\),
so
\(\distS{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}{b_j, b_{j'}} \,{>}\, 4cr' - 2r'\).

All that remains to be done is to show that
\(\distS{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}{b_j, b_0} > 4cr'-2r'\) for all \(j \in J\).
Consider an arbitrary \(j \in J\).
We have
\[
  \hat{r}
  ~ \stackrel{\eqref{eq:proof:rp_gaifman:distance_between_tuple_and_far_element}}{<} ~
  \operatorname{dist}^{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}(a_j,b_0)
  ~ \leqslant ~
  \operatorname{dist}^{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}(a_j,b_j) + \operatorname{dist}^{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}(b_j,b_0)
  ~ \stackrel{\eqref{eq:proof:rp_gaifman:distance_between_tuple_entry_and_partner}}{\leqslant} ~
  r' + \operatorname{dist}^{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}(b_j,b_0).
\]
Thus,
\(
\operatorname{dist}^{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}(b_j,b_0)
>
\hat{r}-r'
\).
From \eqref{eq:GNF-choice-of-r}, we know that
\(\hat{r} - r' = \tilde{r} - 2r' = \operatorname{r} ({q{+}1}, {k}, {d}) - 2r'\),
which, by \eqref{eq:def:radius_function}, is equal to \(4kr' - 2r'\),
and this is \(\geqslant 4cr' - 2r'\) since \(c \leqslant \left\lvertI\right\rvert \leqslant k\).

Finally, we have shown that \(b_0, (b_j)_{j \in J}\) serve as witnesses
certifying that \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \models \xi^{(c-1)}_{\ell+1}\).
However, this is a contradiction to the fact that
\(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \models \neg\xi^{(c-1)}_{\ell+1}\).
Therefore, we obtain that \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \not\models \mu (\bar{a}_I)\).
This, finally, completes Case~(\ref*{def:fparam:existential_plain}).

   \abovedisplayskip 10\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus5\p@
   \abovedisplayshortskip \z@ \@plus3\p@
   \belowdisplayshortskip 6\p@ \@plus3\p@ \@minus3\p@
   \belowdisplayskip \abovedisplayskip
   \let\leftmargin\leftmargini
            \parsep\z@ \@plus\p@
            \topsep 8\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus4\p@
            \itemsep \parsep\leftmargin\leftmargini
            \parsep\z@ \@plus\p@
            \topsep 8\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus4\p@
            \itemsep \parsep\@xpt{13} 
   \abovedisplayskip 10\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus5\p@
   \abovedisplayshortskip \z@ \@plus3\p@
   \belowdisplayshortskip 6\p@ \@plus3\p@ \@minus3\p@
   \belowdisplayskip \abovedisplayskip
   \let\leftmargin\leftmargini
            \parsep\z@ \@plus\p@
            \topsep 8\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus4\p@
            \itemsep \parsep\leftmargin\leftmargini
            \parsep\z@ \@plus\p@
            \topsep 8\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus4\p@
            \itemsep \parsep\@xpt{13} 
   \abovedisplayskip 10\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus5\p@
   \abovedisplayshortskip \z@ \@plus3\p@
   \belowdisplayshortskip 6\p@ \@plus3\p@ \@minus3\p@
   \belowdisplayskip \abovedisplayskip
   \let\leftmargin\leftmargini
            \parsep\z@ \@plus\p@
            \topsep 8\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus4\p@
            \itemsep \parsep\leftmargin\leftmargini
            \parsep\z@ \@plus\p@
            \topsep 8\p@ \@plus2\p@ \@minus4\p@
            \itemsep \parsep\bfseries}{Case~(\ref{def:fparam:aggregation}):}
\(\varphi\) is of the form
\(\bigl(s = \sum\ensuremath{\mathtt{w}}(\bar{y}).\varphi_1\bigr)\),
where
\(\ensuremath{\mathtt{w}} \in \ensuremath{\mathbf{W}}\),
\(S = \ensuremath{\textup{type}}(\ensuremath{\mathtt{w}})\) is finite,
\(s \in S\),
\(\ell = \operatorname*{ar}(\ensuremath{\mathtt{w}})\),
\(\bar{y} = (x_{k+1}, \dots, x_{k+\ell})\),
and \(\varphi_1 \in \ensuremath{\ensuremath{\@empty{F}}(q,k{+}\wmaxar,d)}\)
with \(\operatorname*{free}(\varphi_1)
\subseteq \operatorname*{free}(\varphi) \cup \ensuremath{\{x_{k+1}, \dots, x_{k+\ell}\}}
\subseteq \ensuremath{\{x_1, \dots,x_{k+\wmaxar}\}}\).

This case can be handled similarly as the analogous case in the
proof of \cite[Theorem~4.6]{vanBergeremSchweikardt_2021_FOWA},
but we rely on \cref{lemma:fv} instead of the Feferman--Vaught decompositions
of \cite{vanBergeremSchweikardt_2021_FOWA}.
The proof details are as follows.

Applying the induction hypothesis to \(\varphi_1\),
we obtain an equivalent formula \(\varphi_1'\)
with \(\operatorname*{free}(\varphi_1') = \operatorname*{free}(\varphi_1)\)
such that \(\varphi_1'\) is a Boolean combination of formulas in
\(\operatorname{S} ({q}, {k{+}\wmaxar}, {d}) \cup \operatorname{L} ({q}, {k{+}\wmaxar}, {d})\),
the formula \(\varphi_1'\) has outer quantifier rank
\(\leqslant \operatorname*{qr}(\varphi_1) = \operatorname*{qr}(\varphi)-1\),
and it has inner quantifier rank \(\leqslant \operatorname*{qr}(\varphi_1)-1 = \operatorname*{qr}(\varphi)-2\).

We first consider the case that \(\ell = 0\),
that is,
\(\operatorname*{ar}(\ensuremath{\mathtt{w}}) = 0\) and \(\operatorname*{free}(\varphi_1') = \operatorname*{free}(\varphi)\).
In this case, if \(s = 0_S\),
then \(\varphi\) is equivalent to
\(\bigl(\neg\varphi_1' \lor \bigl(0_S = \ensuremath{\mathtt{w}}()\bigr)\bigr)\);
and if \(s \neq 0_S\), then \(\varphi\) is equivalent to
\(\bigl(\varphi_1' \land \bigl(s = \ensuremath{\mathtt{w}}()\bigr)\bigr)\).
We are done, because both formulas are Boolean combinations of formulas in
\(\operatorname{L} ({q{+}1}, {k}, {d}) \cup \operatorname{S} ({q{+}1}, {k}, {d})\).

Henceforth, we consider the case that \(\ell \geqslant 1\).
We transfer \(\varphi_1'\) into disjunctive normal form in order to obtain an
equivalent formula of the form
\(\ensuremath{\bigvee}_{\nu = 1}^n \bigl(\chi_\nu \land \lambda_\nu\bigr)\),
where \(n \geqslant 1\) and,
for all \(\nu \in \brac{n}\),
\(\chi_\nu\) is a Boolean combination of sentences in
\(\operatorname{S} ({q}, {k{+}\wmaxar}, {d})\) of inner quantifier rank \(\leqslant \operatorname*{qr}(\varphi)-2\),
and \(\lambda_\nu \in \operatorname{L} ({q}, {k{+}\wmaxar}, {d})\) with
\(\operatorname*{free}(\lambda_\nu) \subseteq \operatorname*{free}(\varphi_1')\)
and \(\operatorname*{qr}(\lambda_\nu) \leqslant \operatorname*{qr}(\varphi)-1\).
For every \(J \subseteq [n]\), let
\[
  \chi_J \ \coloneqq \ \ensuremath{\bigwedge}_{j\in J}\chi_j \ \land
  \ensuremath{\bigwedge}_{j \in [n]\setminus J} \neg\chi_j
  \qquad\text{and}\qquad
  \lambda_J \ \coloneqq \ \ensuremath{\bigvee}_{j\in J} \lambda_j\,.
\]
Clearly, \(\ensuremath{\bigvee}_{\nu = 1}^n \bigl(\chi_\nu \land \lambda_\nu\bigr)\)
is equivalent to
\(\ensuremath{\bigvee}_{\emptyset \neq J \subseteq [n]} \bigl(\chi_J \land \lambda_J\bigr)\),
the \((\chi_J)_{J \subseteq [n]}\) are mutually exclusive
(i.e.\ \(\chi_J \land \chi_{J'}\) is unsatisfiable
for any distinct \(J, J' \subseteq [n]\)),
and they are Boolean combinations of sentences in
\(\operatorname{S} ({q}, {k{+}\wmaxar}, {d}) \subseteq \operatorname{S} ({q{+}1}, {k}, {d})\).
Furthermore,
for each \(J \subseteq [n]\) with \(J \neq \emptyset\),
we have
\(\lambda_J \in \operatorname{L} ({q}, {k{+}\wmaxar}, {d})\)
and
\(\operatorname*{free}(\lambda_J) \subseteq \operatorname*{free}(\varphi)
\cup \ensuremath{\{x_{k+1}, \dots, x_{k+\ell}\}}\).
Let
\[
  \tilde{\varphi}
  ~ \coloneqq ~
  \ensuremath{\bigvee}_{\emptyset \neq J \subseteq [n]} \paren[\Big]{
    \chi_J \land \paren[\big]{
      s = \sum \ensuremath{\mathtt{w}}(\bar{y}).\lambda_J
    }
  }.
\]
The following is straightforward to prove.

\begin{claim}
  \label{claim:GNF-tilde-phi}
  If \(s \neq 0_S\),
  then \(\varphi\) is equivalent to \(\tilde{\varphi}\).
  If \(s = 0_S\),
  then \(\varphi\) is equivalent to
  \(\bigl(\tilde{\varphi} \lor \chi_\emptyset\bigr)\).
\end{claim}
\begin{claimproof}
  Since \(\varphi_1 \equiv \varphi_1' \equiv \ensuremath{\bigvee}_{\emptyset \neq J \subseteq [n]}
  (\chi_J \land \lambda_J)\),
  the formula \(\varphi\) is equivalent to
  \[\hat{\varphi} \ \coloneqq \ \paren[\Big]{
    s = \sum \ensuremath{\mathtt{w}}(\bar{y}).\paren[\big]{
      \ensuremath{\bigvee}_{\emptyset \neq J \subseteq [n]}
      (\chi_J \land \lambda_J)
    }
  }.\]
  Now let \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}\) be a \(\paren{\sigma, \ensuremath{\mathbf{W}}}\)-structure,
  let \(\bar{a} \in A^k\),
  and let \(J^* \coloneqq \ensuremath{\ensuremath{\{j \in [n] : \ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \models \chi_j\}}}\).
  Then \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \models \chi_{J^*}\),
  and \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \not\models \chi_J\) for all \(J \subseteq [n]\) with \(J \neq J^*\).

  If \(J^* \neq \emptyset\),
  then we have that
  \((\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}, \bar{a}) \models \hat{\varphi}\)
  if and only if
  \((\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}, \bar{a}) \models \paren[\big]{
    s = \sum \ensuremath{\mathtt{w}}(\bar{y}).\lambda_{J^*}
  }\)
  if and only if
  \((\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}, \bar{a}) \models \ensuremath{\bigvee}_{\emptyset \neq J \subseteq [n]}
  \paren[\Big]{
    \chi_J \mathbin{\mathchoice{\ }{}{}{} \land \mathchoice{\ }{}{}{}}
    \paren[\big]{
      s = \sum \ensuremath{\mathtt{w}}(\bar{y}).\lambda_J
    }
  }\).
  Furthermore, it holds that \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \not\models \chi_\emptyset\).
  Combined, this shows that
  \((\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}, \bar{a}) \models \varphi\)
  if and only if
  \((\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}, \bar{a}) \models \tilde{\varphi}\)
  if and only if
  \((\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}, \bar{a}) \models \bigl(\tilde{\varphi} \lor \chi_\emptyset\bigr)\).

  If \(J^* = \emptyset\),
  then we have that
  \((\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}, \bar{a}) \models \hat{\varphi}\)
  if and only if
  \(s = 0_S\),
  and it holds that \((\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}, \bar{a}) \not\models \tilde{\varphi}\).
  Hence, if \(s = 0_S\),
  then we have
  \((\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}, \bar{a}) \models \varphi\)
  and
  \((\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}, \bar{a}) \models \bigl(\tilde{\varphi} \lor \chi_\emptyset\bigr)\).
  On the other hand,
  if \(s \neq 0_S\),
  then
  \((\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}, \bar{a}) \not\models \varphi\),
  and
  \((\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}, \bar{a}) \not\models \tilde{\varphi}\).

  All in all, this shows that
  \(\varphi \equiv \tilde{\varphi}\) if \(s \neq 0_S\),
  and
  \(\varphi \equiv \bigl(\tilde{\varphi} \lor \chi_\emptyset\bigr)\)
  if \(s = 0_S\).
\end{claimproof}

Based on \cref{claim:GNF-tilde-phi},
in order to complete the proof for Case~(\ref*{def:fparam:aggregation}),
it suffices to consider an arbitrary non-empty \(J \subseteq [n]\)
and the formula
\(\lambda \coloneqq \lambda_J\)
(with \(\lambda \in \operatorname{L} ({q}, {k{+}\wmaxar}, {d})\),
\(\operatorname*{free}(\lambda) \subseteq \operatorname*{free}(\varphi) \cup \ensuremath{\{x_{k+1}, \dots, x_{k+\ell}\}}\),
and \(\operatorname*{qr}(\lambda)\leqslant\operatorname*{qr}(\varphi)-1\))
and show how to transform the formula
\(
  \psi \coloneqq
  \bigl(
     s = \sum \ensuremath{\mathtt{w}}(\bar{y}).\lambda
  \bigr)
\)
into an equivalent formula \(\psi'\) that is a Boolean combination
of formulas in \(\operatorname{L} ({q{+}1}, {k}, {d}) \cup \operatorname{S} ({q{+}1}, {k}, {d})\)
with \(\operatorname*{free}(\psi') = \operatorname*{free}(\psi)\) and of outer (inner) quantifier rank
at most \(\operatorname*{qr}(\varphi)\) (resp.~\(\operatorname*{qr}(\varphi)-1\)).

If \(\operatorname*{free}(\psi) = \emptyset\),
then
\(\operatorname*{free}(\lambda) \subseteq \ensuremath{\{x_{k+1}, \dots, x_{k+\ell}\}}
= \ensuremath{\{y_1, \dots, y_\ell\}}\),
and \(\psi\) is a sentence in
\(\operatorname{S} ({q{+}1}, {k}, {d})\)
of inner quantifier rank \(\leqslant \operatorname*{qr}(\varphi)-1\);
hence, we are done by setting \(\psi' \coloneqq \psi\).

Henceforth, we consider the case that \(\operatorname*{free}(\psi) \neq \emptyset\);
in particular, this means that \(k \geqslant 1\).
Let \(k' \coloneqq k{+}\wmaxar\),
\(\bar{x} \coloneqq (x_1, \dots, x_{k'})\),
\(I \coloneqq \ensuremath{\ensuremath{\{i \in [k] : x_i \in \operatorname*{free}(\psi)\}}}\), and
\(K \coloneqq I \cup \ensuremath{\{x_{k+1}, \dots, x_{k+\ell}\}}\).
Then, \(\operatorname*{free}(\psi) = \ensuremath{\ensuremath{\{x_i : i \in I\}}}\),
and \(\bar{x}_{K \setminus I} = \bar{y}\).
Let \(T \coloneqq \ensuremath{\ensuremath{\{(t_1,t_2) \in S \times S : t_1 +_S t_2 = s\}}}\),
which is a finite set, because \(S\) is finite.
Clearly, \(\psi\) is equivalent to
\(\ensuremath{\bigvee}_{(t_1,t_2) \in T} \bigl(\psi'_{t_1} \land \psi''_{t_2}\bigr)\),
where
\begin{eqnarray*}
  \psi'_{t_1}
  & \coloneqq
  & \paren[\Big]{\,
    t_1 =
    \sum \ensuremath{\mathtt{w}}(\bar{y}).\paren[\big]{\,
      \lambda \mathbin{\mathchoice{\ }{}{}{} \land \mathchoice{\ }{}{}{}} \neg\;\operatorname{dist}(\bar{x}_I; \bar{y}) \,{>}\, r'
    \,}
  }
  \qquad\text{and}
  \\
  \psi''_{t_2}
  & \coloneqq
  & \paren[\Big]{\,
    t_2 =
    \sum \ensuremath{\mathtt{w}}(\bar{y}).\paren[\big]{\,
      \lambda \mathbin{\mathchoice{\ }{}{}{} \land \mathchoice{\ }{}{}{}} \operatorname{dist}(\bar{x}_I; \bar{y}) \,{>}\, r'
    \,}
  }.
\end{eqnarray*}
Throughout the remainder of this proof, consider an arbitrary tuple
\((t_1, t_2) \in T\).

\begin{claim}
  \label{claim:psi-prime-in-loc}
  \(\psi'_{t_1}\) is equivalent to a formula in \(\operatorname{L} ({q{+}1}, {k}, {d})\)
  with the same free variables and of the same quantifier rank.
\end{claim}
\begin{claimproof}
  Note that \(\neg\,\operatorname{dist}(\bar{x}_I; \bar{y}) \,{>}\, r'\)
  is equivalent to the formula
  \(\ensuremath{\bigvee}_{i\in I, j\in[\ell]} \operatorname{dist}(x_i,y_j) \,{\leqslant}\, r'\).
  Therefore, \(\psi'_{t_1}\) is equivalent to the formula
  \[
  \paren[\Big]{\,
    t_1 =
    \sum \ensuremath{\mathtt{w}}(\bar{y}).
    \paren[\big]{\,
      \lambda
      \mathbin{\mathchoice{\ }{}{}{} \land \mathchoice{\ }{}{}{}}\!\!\!
      \ensuremath{\bigvee}_{i\in I,j\in[\ell]}
      \operatorname{dist}(x_i,y_j) \,{\leqslant}\, r'
    \,}
  };
  \]
  and this formula belongs to \(\operatorname{L} ({q{+}1}, {k}, {d})\)
  and has the same quantifier rank as \(\psi'_{t_1}\).
\end{claimproof}

It remains to transform \(\psi''_{t_2}\)
into an equivalent Boolean combination of formulas in
\(\operatorname{L} ({q{+}1}, {k}, {d}) \cup \operatorname{S} ({q{+}1}, {k}, {d})\)
and of outer (inner) quantifier rank at most
\(\operatorname*{qr}(\varphi)\) (resp.~\(\operatorname*{qr}(\varphi)-1\)).
To achieve this, we apply \cref{lemma:fv} to \(\lambda(\bar{x}_K)\)
and obtain a finite and non-empty set \(\Delta\)
of pairs of formulas \(\paren{\alpha(\bar{x}_I), \beta(\bar{y})}\)
such that \(\alpha(\bar{x}_I) \in \operatorname{L} ({q}, {k{+}\wmaxar}, {d})\),
\(\beta(\bar{y}) \in \operatorname{L} ({q}, {k{+}\wmaxar}, {d})\),
the formulas have quantifier rank \(\leqslant \operatorname*{qr}(\lambda) \leqslant \operatorname*{qr}(\varphi)-1\),
the first entries in \(\Delta\) are \emph{mutually exclusive},
and
\begin{equation}
  \label{eq:GNF-aggregation-fv}
  \lambda(\bar{x}_K)
  \mathbin{\mathchoice{\ }{}{}{} \land \mathchoice{\ }{}{}{}}
  \operatorname{dist}(\bar{x}_I; \bar{y}) \,{>}\, r'
  ~~ \equiv ~
  \ensuremath{\bigvee}_{(\alpha,\beta) \in \Delta}
  \paren[\big]{
    \alpha \paren{{\myvec{x}}_{I}}
    \land
    \beta \paren{\bar{y}}
  }
  \mathbin{\mathchoice{\ }{}{}{} \land \mathchoice{\ }{}{}{}}
  \operatorname{dist}({\myvec{x}}_{I}; \bar{y}) \,{>}\, r'.
\end{equation}
Let
\[
  \tilde{\psi}_{t_2}
  ~ \coloneqq ~
  \ensuremath{\bigvee}_{(\alpha,\beta) \in \Delta}\paren[\Big]{
    \alpha(\bar{x}_I)
    \mathbin{\mathchoice{\ }{}{}{} \land \mathchoice{\ }{}{}{}}
    \paren[\Big]{
      t_2 = \sum \ensuremath{\mathtt{w}}(\bar{y}).\paren[\big]{
        \beta(\bar{y}) \mathbin{\mathchoice{\ }{}{}{} \land \mathchoice{\ }{}{}{}} \operatorname{dist}(\bar{x}_I; \bar{y}) \,{>}\, r'
      }
    }
  }.
\]
Let \(\myAlphas \coloneqq \ensuremath{\ensuremath{\{\alpha : \text{there is a \(\beta\) with } (\alpha,\beta) \in \Delta\}}}\)
and \(\myBetas \coloneqq \ensuremath{\ensuremath{\{\beta : \text{there is a \(\alpha\) with } (\alpha,\beta) \in \Delta\}}}\).
Using the fact that the first entries in \(\Delta\)
are mutually exclusive,
the following is straightforward to prove.

\begin{claim}
  \label{claim:GNF-psi-t-2}
  If \(t_2 \neq 0_S\),
  then \(\psi''_{t_2}\) is equivalent to
  \(\tilde{\psi}_{t_2}\).
  If \(t_2 = 0_S\),
  then \(\psi''_{t_2}\) is equivalent to
  \(\bigl(\tilde{\psi}_{t_2} \lor
  \ensuremath{\bigwedge}_{\alpha \in \myAlphas} \neg\alpha\bigr)\).
\end{claim}
\begin{claimproof}
  Let \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}\) be a \(\paren{\sigma, \ensuremath{\mathbf{W}}}\)-structure,
  and let \(\bar{a} \in A^k\).
  By \cref{eq:GNF-aggregation-fv},
  it holds that
  \((\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}, \bar{a}) \models \psi''_{t_2}\)
  if and only if
  \((\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}, \bar{a}) \models \paren[\Big]{
    t_2 = \sum \ensuremath{\mathtt{w}}(\bar{y}).\paren[\Big]{
      \ensuremath{\bigvee}_{(\alpha, \beta) \in \Delta}
      \bigl(\alpha({\myvec{x}}_{I}) \land \beta(\bar{y})\bigr)
      \mathbin{\mathchoice{\ }{}{}{} \land \mathchoice{\ }{}{}{}}
      \operatorname{dist}({\myvec{x}}_{I}; \bar{y}) \,{>}\, r'
    }
  }\).
  Since the first entries in \(\Delta\) are mutually exclusive,
  there is at most one \((\alpha, \beta) \in \Delta\)
  with \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \models \alpha(\bar{a}_I)\).

  If there is some \((\alpha, \beta) \in \Delta\)
  with \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \models \alpha(\bar{a}_I)\),
  then we have
  \((\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}, \bar{a}) \models \psi''_{t_2}\)
  if and only if
  \((\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}, \bar{a}) \models \Bigl(t_2 = \sum \ensuremath{\mathtt{w}}(\bar{y}).
  \bigl(\beta(\bar{y}) \mathbin{\mathchoice{\ }{}{}{} \land \mathchoice{\ }{}{}{}} \operatorname{dist}({\myvec{x}}_{I}; \bar{y}) \,{>}\, r'\bigr)\Bigr)\)
  if and only if
  \((\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}, \bar{a}) \models \tilde{\psi}_{t_2}\)
  if and only if
  \((\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}, \bar{a}) \models \bigl(\tilde{\psi}_{t_2} \lor
  \ensuremath{\bigwedge}_{\alpha \in \myAlphas} \neg\alpha\bigr)\).

  If there is no \((\alpha, \beta) \in \Delta\)
  with \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \models \alpha(\bar{a}_I)\),
  then \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \models \psi''_{t_2} (\bar{a})\)
  if and only if \(t_2 = 0_S\),
  and we have \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \not\models \tilde{\psi}_{t_2} (\bar{a})\)
  and \((\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}, \bar{a}) \models \ensuremath{\bigwedge}_{\alpha \in \myAlphas} \neg\alpha\).
  Thus, if \(t_2 = 0_S\),
  then \((\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}, \bar{a}) \models \psi''_{t_2}\)
  and \((\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}, \bar{a}) \models \bigl(\tilde{\psi}_{t_2} \lor
  \ensuremath{\bigwedge}_{\alpha \in \myAlphas} \neg\alpha\bigr)\).
  On the other hand, if \(t_2 \neq 0_S\),
  then \((\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}, \bar{a}) \not\models \psi''_{t_2}\)
  and \((\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}, \bar{a}) \not\models \tilde{\psi}_{t_2}\).

  All in all, this shows
  \(\psi''_{t_2} \equiv \tilde{\psi}_{t_2}\) if \(t_2 \neq 0_S\),
  and
  \(\psi''_{t_2} \equiv \bigl(\tilde{\psi}_{t_2} \lor
  \ensuremath{\bigwedge}_{\alpha \in \myAlphas} \neg\alpha\bigr)\)
  if \(t_2 = 0_S\).
\end{claimproof}

Based on \cref{claim:GNF-psi-t-2},
in order to complete the proof
for Case~(\ref*{def:fparam:aggregation}),
it suffices to consider an arbitrary \(\beta \in \myBetas\)
and transform the formula
\[
  \mu(\bar{x}_I)
  ~ \coloneqq ~
  \Big(
    t_2 =
    \sum\ensuremath{\mathtt{w}}(\bar{y}).\big(
      \beta(\bar{y}) \mathbin{\mathchoice{\ }{}{}{} \land \mathchoice{\ }{}{}{}} \operatorname{dist}(\bar{x}_I; \bar{y}) \,{>}\, r'
    \big)
  \Big)
\]
into an equivalent Boolean combination of formulas in
\(\operatorname{L} ({q{+}1}, {k}, {d}) \cup \operatorname{S} ({q{+}1}, {k}, {d})\)
and of outer (inner) quantifier rank at most
\(\operatorname*{qr}(\varphi)\) (resp.~\(\operatorname*{qr}(\varphi)-1\)).
To achieve this, we let
\(U \coloneqq \ensuremath{\ensuremath{\{(u_1,u_2) \in S \times S : u_1 -_S u_2 = t_2\}}}\).
This set is finite because \(S\) is finite.
Let
\(\hat{\mu}(\bar{x}_I) \coloneqq \ensuremath{\bigvee}_{(u_1,u_2) \in U}\big(
  \mu'_{u_1} \land \mu''_{u_2}(\bar{x}_I)
\big)\),
where
\begin{eqnarray*}
  \mu'_{u_1}
  & \coloneqq
  & \Big(u_1 = \sum \ensuremath{\mathtt{w}}(\bar{y}).\beta(\bar{y})\Big)
  \qquad\text{and}
  \\
  \mu''_{u_2}(\bar{x}_I)
  & \coloneqq
  & \Big(u_2 = \sum \ensuremath{\mathtt{w}}(\bar{y}).\big(
      \beta(\bar{y}) \mathbin{\mathchoice{\ }{}{}{} \land \mathchoice{\ }{}{}{}} \neg\;\operatorname{dist}(\bar{x}_I; \bar{y}) \,{>}\, r'
    \big)\Big).
\end{eqnarray*}
Here, \(\mu'_{u_1}\) checks that the sum of the weights of
all tuples satisfying \(\beta\) is equal to~\(u_1\),
and \(\mu''_{u_2}\) checks that the sum of the weights of
tuples satisfying \(\beta\) that \emph{are not} far from \(\bar{x}_I\)
is equal to~\(u_2\).
Hence, if \(\mu'_{u_1}\) and \(\mu''_{u_2}\) are satisfied,
then the sum of the weights of tuples satisfying \(\beta\)
that \emph{are} far from \(\bar{x}_I\) is equal to \(u_1 - u_2\).
Thus, using that $(S,+_S)$ is an \emph{abelian group},
and heavily relying on the existence of inverse elements in \(S\),
the following is straightforward to prove.
\begin{claim}
  \(\hat{\mu}(\bar{x}_I)\) is equivalent to \(\mu(\bar{x}_I)\).
\end{claim}
\begin{claimproof}
  Let \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}\) be a \(\paren{\sigma, \ensuremath{\mathbf{W}}}\)-structure,
  let \(\bar{a} \in A^k\),
  and let \(u', u'' \in S\)
  such that \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \models \mu'_{u'}\)
  and \((\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}, \bar{a}) \models \mu''_{u''}\)
  (and note that such \(u', u''\) always exist).
We let
  \begin{align*}
    B
    &\ \coloneqq \ \ensuremath{\bigl\{ \,\bar{b} \in A^\ell\, \ \mathrel{:} \  \,\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \models \beta(\bar{b})\, \bigr\}},\\
    B_>
    &\ \coloneqq \ \ensuremath{\bigl\{ \,\bar{b} \in B\, \ \mathrel{:} \  \,\distS{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}{\bar{a}_I; \bar{b}} \,{>}\,
      r'\, \bigr\}}, \ \text{ and}\\
    B_\leqslant
    &\ \coloneqq \ B \setminus B'.
  \end{align*}
  It is easy to see that \(u' = \sum_{\bar{b} \in B} \ensuremath{\mathtt{w}}^\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}(\bar{b})\),
  \(u'' = \sum_{\bar{b} \in B_\leqslant} \ensuremath{\mathtt{w}}^\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}(\bar{b})\),
  and \(u' = u'' +_S \sum_{\bar{b} \in B_>} \ensuremath{\mathtt{w}}^\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}(\bar{b})\).
  Since \(S\) is an abelian group, we obtain that
  \(u' -_S u'' = \sum_{\bar{b} \in B_>} \ensuremath{\mathtt{w}}^\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}(\bar{b})\).
  Furthermore, it can easily be verified that
  \((\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}, \bar{a}) \models \mu\)
  if and only if
  \(t_2 = \sum_{\bar{b} \in B_>} \ensuremath{\mathtt{w}}^\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}(\bar{b})\).

  Hence, combined, this shows that
  \begin{align*}
    (\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}, \bar{a}) \models \mu
    & ~\iff~
    t_2 = \sum_{\bar{b} \in B_>} \ensuremath{\mathtt{w}}^\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}(\bar{b})
    \\
    & ~\iff~
    t_2 = u' -_S u''
    \\
    & ~\iff~
    (u', u'') \in U
    \\
    & ~\iff~
    (\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}, \bar{a}) \models \hat{\mu}.
  \end{align*}
  Since \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}\) and \(\bar{a}\) were chosen arbitrarily,
  this shows that \(\mu \equiv \hat{\mu}\).
\end{claimproof}
Note that \(\mu'_{u_1}\) is a sentence in \(\operatorname{S} ({q{+}1}, {k}, {d})\)
of inner quantifier rank \(\leqslant \operatorname*{qr}(\varphi)-1\).
Furthermore, with the same reasoning as in the proof of
\cref{claim:psi-prime-in-loc}
(replacing \(\lambda\) with \(\beta\)),
we obtain that \(\mu''_{u_2}(\bar{x}_I)\)
is equivalent to a formula in \(\operatorname{L} ({q{+}1}, {k}, {d})\)
with free variables \(\ensuremath{\ensuremath{\{x_i : i \in I\}}}\)
and of the same quantifier rank.
Moreover, \(\operatorname*{qr}(\mu''_{u_2}) = \operatorname*{qr}(\beta)+1 \leqslant \operatorname*{qr}(\varphi)\).
This, finally, completes Case~(\ref*{def:fparam:aggregation})
and finishes the proof of \cref{thm:GNF_ngFOWplus_refined}.
<!-- end proof-7 -->
<!-- claim-12: body_md -->
\label{claim:GNF-tilde-phi}
  If \(s \neq 0_S\),
  then \(\varphi\) is equivalent to \(\tilde{\varphi}\).
  If \(s = 0_S\),
  then \(\varphi\) is equivalent to
  \(\bigl(\tilde{\varphi} \lor \chi_\emptyset\bigr)\).
<!-- end claim-12 -->
<!-- claim-13: body_md -->
\label{claim:psi-prime-in-loc}
  \(\psi'_{t_1}\) is equivalent to a formula in \(\operatorname{L} ({q{+}1}, {k}, {d})\)
  with the same free variables and of the same quantifier rank.
<!-- end claim-13 -->
<!-- claim-14: body_md -->
\label{claim:GNF-psi-t-2}
  If \(t_2 \neq 0_S\),
  then \(\psi''_{t_2}\) is equivalent to
  \(\tilde{\psi}_{t_2}\).
  If \(t_2 = 0_S\),
  then \(\psi''_{t_2}\) is equivalent to
  \(\bigl(\tilde{\psi}_{t_2} \lor
  \ensuremath{\bigwedge}_{\alpha \in \myAlphas} \neg\alpha\bigr)\).
<!-- end claim-14 -->
<!-- claim-15: body_md -->
\(\hat{\mu}(\bar{x}_I)\) is equivalent to \(\mu(\bar{x}_I)\).
<!-- end claim-15 -->
<!-- theorem-4: body_md -->
\label{thm:fo-mc}
  Let \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}\) be a nowhere dense class of \(\sigma\)-structures.
  For every \(\ensuremath{\@empty{FO}}[\sigma]\) sentence \(\varphi\) and every \(\varepsilon > 0\),
  there is an algorithm that, given \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}\),
  decides if \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \models \varphi\) in time
  \(\mathcal{O}_{\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}, \varphi, \varepsilon}(\left\lvert\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}\right\rvert^{1+\varepsilon})\).
<!-- end theorem-4 -->
<!-- lemma-6: body_md -->
\label{lem:splitter-strategy}
  Let \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}\) be a nowhere dense class of graphs.
  For every \(r \in \mathbb{N}\),
  there is a \(t = t(\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}, r) \in \ensuremath{\mathbb{N}_{\scriptscriptstyle \geqslant 1}}\) such that the following holds.
  Let \(G \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}\),
  and let
  \[\pi = (v^{(1)}, W^{(1)}, v^{(2)}, W^{(2)}, \dots, v^{(k)}, W^{(k)}),\]
  where \(k \in \mathbb{N}\) and, for all \(i \in [k]\),
  \(v^{(i)} \in V(G)\) and \(W^{(i)} \subseteq V(G)\).
  Let
  \begin{equation}\label{eq:splitter-strategy}
    G^{(0)} \coloneqq G \supseteq H^{(1)} \supseteq
    G^{(1)} \supseteq \cdots \supseteq H^{(k)} \supseteq G^{(k)}
  \end{equation}
  such that the following conditions are satisfied.
  \begin{romanenumerate}
    \item\label{item:splitter-strategy-i}
      For all \(i \in [k]\),
      it holds that \(v^{(i)} \in V(H^{(i)})\).
    \item\label{item:splitter-strategy-ii}
      \(W^{(1)} = \emptyset\)
      and for all \(i \in [2,k]\),
      it holds that \(W^{(i)} = \ensuremath{N_{r}^{H^{(i)}}(v^{(i)})} \cap \bigcup_{j=1}^{i-1} V(P^{(i)}_j)\),
      where \(P^{(i)}_j\) is a path of length at most \(r\)
      from \(v^{(j)}\) to \(v^{(i)}\) in the graph \(H^{(j)}\).
    \item\label{item:splitter-strategy-iii}
      For all \(i \in [k]\),
      it holds that
      \(G^{(i)} = H^{(i)}\big[\ensuremath{N_{r}^{H^{(i)}}(v^{(i)})} \setminus W^{(i)}\big]\).
  \end{romanenumerate}
  Then \(k \leqslant t\).
<!-- end lemma-6 -->
<!-- proof-8: body_md -->
We use the following characterisation,
  due to \cite{NesetrilOssonaDeMendez_2011_NowhereDense},
  of nowhere dense graph classes in terms of \emph{uniform quasi-wideness}
  (cf.~\cite{Dawar_2010_Quasiwide}).
  \emph{A class \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}\) of graphs is nowhere dense
  if and only if there are functions \(s \colon \mathbb{N} \to \mathbb{N}\)
  and \(N \colon \mathbb{N} \times \mathbb{N} \to \mathbb{N}\) such that,
  for all \(r, m \in \mathbb{N}\),
  all \(G \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}\),
  and all \(X \subseteq V(G)\) of size \(\left\lvertX\right\rvert > N(r,m)\),
  there is a set \(S \subseteq V(G)\) of size \(\left\lvertS\right\rvert \leqslant s(r)\)
  and a set \(Y \subseteq X\) of size \(\left\lvertY\right\rvert \geqslant m\)
  with \(\operatorname{dist}^{G \setminus S}(y,y') > r\) for all distinct \(y,y' \in Y\).}

  We choose the functions \(s, N\) for our nowhere dense class \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}\)
  according to this characterisation
  and let \(t \coloneqq N\bigl(r, 2s(r) + 2\bigr)\).
  Suppose for contradiction that \(k > t\).

  Let \(X \coloneqq \ensuremath{\{v^{(1)}, \dots, v^{(k)}\}}\).
  Choose \(S \subseteq V(G)\) of size \(\left\lvertS\right\rvert \geqslant s(r)\)
  and a set \(Y \subseteq X\) of size \(\left\lvertY\right\rvert = 2s(r) + 2\)
  with \(\operatorname{dist}^{G \setminus S}(y,y') > r\) for all distinct \(y, y' \in Y\).
  Say, \(Y = \ensuremath{\{v^{(i_1)},\dots,v^{(i_{2s(r)+2})}\}}\)
  with \(1 \leqslant i_1 < i_2 < \cdots < i_{2r(s)+2} \leqslant k\).
  For all \(j \in [s(r) + 1]\),
  let \(P_j \coloneqq P^{(i_{2j})}_{i_{2j-1}}\) be the path of length at most \(r\)
  from \(v^{(i_{2j-1})}\) to \(v^{(i_{2j})}\) in \(H^{(i_{2j-1})}\)
  from condition~\eqref{item:splitter-strategy-ii}.
  Then \(V(P_j) \cap V(G^{(i_{2j})}) = \emptyset\),
  and therefore \(V(P_j) \cap V(H^{(i_{2j+1})}) = \emptyset\)
  (if \(j < s(r) + 1\)).
  This means that the paths \(P_1, \dots, P_{2s(r)+1}\) are mutually vertex disjoint.
  Since, for every \(j\), the path \(P_j\) has length at most \(r\)
  and \(\operatorname{dist}^{G \setminus S}(v^{(i_{2j-1})}, v^{(i_{2j})}) > r\),
  it holds that \(V(P_j) \cap S \neq \emptyset\).
  Thus, \(\left\lvertS\right\rvert \geqslant s(r) + 1\), which is a contradiction.
  This completes the proof of Lemma~\ref{lem:splitter-strategy}.
<!-- end proof-8 -->
<!-- remark-1: body_md -->
\label{rem:compute-splitter-strategy}
  Following \cite[Remark~4.7]{GroheKreutzerSiebertz_2017_NowhereDense},
  we can compute this winning strategy as follows:
  for each \(i \in [k]\),
  we compute a breadth-first search tree \(T^{(i)}\) for the graph \(H^{(i)}\)
  rooted in \(v^{(i)}\) in time \(\mathcal{O}\bigl(\left\lVertH^{(i)}\right\rVert\bigr)\).
  Then, for each \(j \in [1, i{-}1]\),
  we use the tree \(T^{(j)}\) to compute the path \(P^{(i)}_j\)
  in time \(\mathcal{O}\bigl(\bigl\lvertP^{(i)}_j\bigr\rvert\bigr)\),
  which allows us to compute the set \(W^{(i)}\) in time \(\mathcal{O}(ri)\).
<!-- end remark-1 -->
<!-- lemma-7: body_md -->
\label{lem:nb-covers}
  Let \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}\) be a nowhere dense class of graphs.
  For every \(\varepsilon > 0\) and \(r \in \mathbb{N}\),
  there is an algorithm that,
  given a graph \(G \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}\) with \(n \coloneqq \left\lvertV(G)\right\rvert\) vertices,
  computes in time \(\mathcal{O}(n^{1+\varepsilon})\)
  an \(r\)-neighbourhood cover \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{X}}\) of \(G\)
  of radius at most \(2r\) and maximum degree \(\mathcal{O}(n^\varepsilon)\)
  and a centre function \(c \colon \ensuremath{\mathcal{X}} \to V(G)\)
  such that \(X \subseteq \ensuremath{N_{2r}^{G[X]}(c(X))}\) for every \(X \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{X}}\).
<!-- end lemma-7 -->
<!-- lemma-8: body_md -->
\label{lem:dist-is}
  Let \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}\) be a nowhere dense class of \(\sigma\)-structures.
  For all \(d, m \in \mathbb{N}\) and \(\varepsilon > 0\),
  there is an algorithm that,
  given a structure \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}\) and a set \(X \subseteq A\),
  decides in time \(\mathcal{O}(n^{1+\varepsilon})\)
  whether \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}\) has a \(d\)-independent set \(Y \subseteq X\) of order \(\left\lvertY\right\rvert \geqslant m\),
  where \(n \coloneqq \left\lvertA\right\rvert\).
<!-- end lemma-8 -->
<!-- lemma-9: body_md -->
\label{lem:fo-removal}
  Let \(q, k, d \in \mathbb{N}\) with \(k\geqslant 1\),
  let \(\sigma\) be a signature,
  and let \(r \coloneqq \operatorname{r_1} ({q}, {k}, {d})\).
  For every formula \(\varphi(\bar{x}) \in \ensuremath{\@empty{FO}}^+(q,k,d)\) of signature \(\sigma\)
  with \(\bar{x} = (x_1, \dots, x_k)\)
  and for every set \(I \subseteq [k]\),
  there is a formula \(\tilde{\varphi}_I(\bar{x}_{\setminus I}) \in \ensuremath{\@empty{FO}}^+(q,k,d)\)
  of signature \(\tilde{\sigma}^{}_{r}\)
  such that for every \(\sigma\)-structure \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}\) of order \(\left\lvertA\right\rvert \geqslant 2\),
  all \(c \in A\), and all \(\bar{a} = (a_1, \dots, a_k) \in A^k\)
  such that \(I = \ensuremath{\ensuremath{\{i \in [k] : a_i=c\}}}\),
  we have
  \begin{equation}
    \label{eq:removal}
    \ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \models \varphi(\bar{a})
    \quad\iff\quad
    \ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \lbag_r c \models \tilde{\varphi}_I(\bar{a}_{\setminus I}).
  \end{equation}
  Furthermore, there is an algorithm that computes \(\tilde{\varphi}_I\)
  from \(\varphi(\bar{x})\) and \(I\).
<!-- end lemma-9 -->
<!-- proof-9: body_md -->
Note that, for all distance atoms $\operatorname{dist}(y,z)\,{\leqslant}\,d'$ that occur
in a formula in $\ensuremath{\@empty{FO}}^+(q,k,d)$, it holds that $d'\leqslant\operatorname{r_1} ({q}, {k}, {d})$;
and \(\tilde{\sigma}^{}_{\operatorname{r_1} ({q}, {k}, {d})}\) contains unary relations $S_i$ for all $i$
with $1\leqslant i\leqslant \operatorname{r_1} ({q}, {k}, {d})$.

Let us fix a $d\in \mathbb{N}$.
We prove the lemma by induction on $q$, and for each fixed $q$, we show
that the assertion of the lemma holds for all $k\in \ensuremath{\mathbb{N}_{\scriptscriptstyle \geqslant 1}}$.
\medskip

For the induction base with $q=0$, consider an arbitrary $k\in\ensuremath{\mathbb{N}_{\scriptscriptstyle \geqslant 1}}$,
and let $r\coloneqq\operatorname{r_1} ({0}, {k}, {d})$.
We have to prove that the assertion of the lemma
holds for all formulas $\varphi(\bar
x)\in\ensuremath{\@empty{FO}}^+(0,k,d)$. First, consider the case that $\varphi(\bar
x)$ is an atomic formula.

  If \(\varphi(\bar{x})\) is of the form \(\operatorname{dist}(x_i, x_j) \leqslant d'\),
  then \(i, j \in [k]\) and \(d' \leqslant d \leqslant \operatorname{r_1} ({0}, {k}, {d}) = r\).
  If \(i, j \in I\),
  then we let
  \(\tilde{\varphi}_I(\bar{x}_{\setminus I}) \coloneqq \true\).
  If \(i \in I\) and \(j \not\in I\),
  we let
  \(\tilde{\varphi}_I(\bar{x}_{\setminus I}) \coloneqq S_{d'}(x_j)\).
  Analogously, if \(i \not\in I\) and \(j \in I\),
  we let
  \(\tilde{\varphi}_I(\bar{x}_{\setminus I}) \coloneqq S_{d'}(x_i)\).
  Finally, if \(i, j \not\in I\),
  we let
  \[\tilde{\varphi}_I(\bar{x}_{\setminus I}) \ \coloneqq \ \ \operatorname{dist}(x_i, x_j) \leqslant d'
  \ \lor \ensuremath{\bigvee}_{\substack{1 \leqslant d_1, d_2 \leqslant d'-1,\\ d_1 + d_2 = d'}}
  \bigl(S_{d_1}(x_i) \land S_{d_2}(x_j)\bigr).\]

  If \(\varphi(\bar{x})\) is of the form \(\true\) or \(\false\),
  then we also let \(\tilde{\varphi}_I(\bar{x}_{\setminus I}) \coloneqq \true\)
  or \(\tilde{\varphi}_I(\bar{x}_{\setminus I}) \coloneqq \false\), respectively.

  If \(\varphi(\bar{x})\) is of the form \(x_i {=} x_j\),
  then \(i,j \in [k]\).
  If \(i,j \in I\), then we let \(\tilde{\varphi}_I(\bar{x}_{\setminus I}) \coloneqq \true\).
  If \(i \in I\) and \(j \not\in I\) or if \(i \not\in I\) and \(j \in I\),
  then we let \(\tilde{\varphi}_I(\bar{x}_{\setminus I}) \coloneqq \false\).
  Finally, if \(i,j \not\in I\),
  then we let \(\tilde{\varphi}_I(\bar{x}_{\setminus I}) \coloneqq x_i {=} x_j\).

  If \(\varphi(\bar{x})\) is of the form \(R(x_{i_1}, \dots, x_{i_s})\)
  for some \(R \in \sigma\) with \(s \coloneqq \operatorname*{ar}(R)\) and \(i_1, \dots, i_s \in [k]\),
  then we let \(\tilde{\varphi}_I(\bar{x}_{\setminus I}) \coloneqq \tilde{R}_J(\bar{y})\),
  where \(J \coloneqq \ensuremath{\ensuremath{\{j \in [s] : i_j \in I\}}}\)
  and \(\bar{y}\) is the tuple obtained from \((x_{i_1}, \dots, x_{i_s})\)
  by dropping all entries \(x_{i_j}\) with \(j \in J\).

  This concludes the construction for atomic formulas.
  It can easily be verified that \(\tilde{\varphi}_I(\bar{x}_{\setminus I}) \in \ensuremath{\@empty{FO}}^+(0,k,d)\)
  and that \cref{eq:removal} holds for all \(\sigma\)-structures \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}\), \(c \in A\),
  and tuples \(\bar{a} \in A^k\) as specified.

  Boolean combinations of such formulas are handled in the obvious
  way:
  if \(\varphi = \neg\psi\), then \(\tilde{\varphi}_I \coloneqq \neg \tilde{\psi}_I\),
  and if \(\varphi = \psi \lor \chi\),
  then \(\tilde{\varphi}_I \coloneqq \tilde{\psi}_I \lor \tilde{\chi}_I\).
  This completes the induction base with $q=0$.
\medskip
  
For the induction step from $q$ to $q{+}1$, consider arbitrary $q,k\in
\mathbb{N}$ with $k\geqslant 1$.
We have to prove that the assertion of the lemma holds for all formulas in
$\ensuremath{\@empty{FO}}^+(q{+}1,k,d)$ and $r\coloneqq\operatorname{r_1} ({q{+}1}, {k}, {d})$.
From the induction hypothesis, we obtain that the assertion of the lemma
holds for all formulas in $\ensuremath{\@empty{FO}}^+(q,k{+}1,d)$ and $r'\coloneqq\operatorname{r_1} ({q}, {k{+}1}, {d})$.

By the definition of $\ensuremath{\@empty{FO}}^+(q{+}1,k,d)$, it consists of
formulas that are Boolean combinations of formulas of the types
\eqref{item:FOplusParam_One}--\eqref{item:FOplusParam_Four} (cf.,
\cref{sec:rpGNFforFOplus}).
Consider a $\varphi(\bar x)\in\ensuremath{\@empty{FO}}^+(q{+}1,k,d)$. 

If $\varphi(\bar x)$ is of type \eqref{item:FOplusParam_One}, it belongs to
$\ensuremath{\@empty{FO}}^+(q,k{+}1,d)$. We are done by using the induction
hypothesis and the fact that $r\geqslant r'$.

If $\varphi(\bar x)$ is of type \eqref{item:FOplusParam_Two}, it is a
distance atom and we can
handle it in the same way as we handled distance atoms in the base
case for $q=0$.

If $\varphi(\bar x)$ is of type \eqref{item:FOplusParam_Three}, it is of
the form
  \(\exists x_{k+1}\, \bigl(\operatorname{dist}(x_i, x_{k+1}) \leqslant \hat{d} \land \psi(\bar{x}')\bigr)\)
  for \(i \in [k]\), \(\psi(\bar{x}') \in \ensuremath{\@empty{FO}}^+(q,k{+}1,d)\),
  \(\bar{x}' = (x_1, \dots, x_{k+1})\),
  and \(\hat{d} \leqslant r-r' \leqslant r\).
  Let \(I' \coloneqq I \cup \ensuremath{\{k{+}1\}}\).
  If \(i \in I\), then we set
  \[\tilde{\varphi}_I(\bar{x}_{\setminus I}) \ \coloneqq \ \  \tilde{\psi}_{I'}(\bar{x}'_{\setminus I'})
  \ \lor\ \exists x_{k+1}\, \bigl(S_{\hat{d}}(x_{k+1}) \land \tilde{\psi}_I(\bar{x}'_{\setminus I})\bigr).\]
  If \(i \not\in I\), we set
  \begin{align*}
    \tilde{\varphi}_I(\bar{x}_{\setminus I}) \ \coloneqq \ \
    &\hphantom{\lor\ }\bigl(\,S_{\hat{d}}(x_i) \land \tilde{\psi}_{I'}(\bar{x}'_{\setminus I'})\,\bigr)\\
    &\lor \exists x_{k+1}\,\bigl(\operatorname{dist}(x_i, x_{k+1}) \leqslant \hat{d}
    \ \land \ \tilde{\psi}_I(\bar{x}'_{\setminus I})\bigr)\\
    &\lor \ensuremath{\bigvee}_{\substack{1 \leqslant d_1, d_2 \leqslant \hat{d}-1\\ d_1 + d_2 = \hat{d}}}
    \exists x_{k+1}\,\bigl(S_{d_1}(x_i) \land S_{d_2}(x_{k+1})
    \land \tilde{\psi}_I(\bar{x}'_{\setminus I})\bigr).
  \end{align*}

  If \(\varphi(\bar{x})\) is of type \eqref{item:FOplusParam_Four}, it is of the form \(\exists x_{k+1} \psi(\bar{x}')\)
  for \(\psi(\bar{x}') \in \ensuremath{\@empty{FO}}^+(q,k{+}1,d)\) and \(\bar{x}' = (x_1, \dots, x_{k+1})\).
  Let \(I' \coloneqq I \cup \ensuremath{\{k{+}1\}}\).
  We set
  \(\tilde{\varphi}_I(\bar{x}_{\setminus I}) \coloneqq \tilde{\psi}_{I'}(\bar{x}'_{\setminus I'})
  \lor \exists x_{k+1}\,\tilde{\psi}_I(\bar{x}'_{\setminus I})\).

   It can easily be verified that, in all four cases, the constructed
   formula $\tilde{\varphi}_I(\bar{x}_{\setminus I})$ belongs to $\ensuremath{\@empty{FO}}^+(q{+}1,k,d)$,
   and that \cref{eq:removal} is satisfied.

  Finally, Boolean combinations of formulas of the types
\eqref{item:FOplusParam_One}--\eqref{item:FOplusParam_Four} are
handled in the obvious way:
  if \(\varphi = \neg\psi\), then \(\tilde{\varphi}_I \coloneqq \neg \tilde{\psi}_I\),
  and if \(\varphi = \psi \lor \chi\),
  then \(\tilde{\varphi}_I \coloneqq \tilde{\psi}_I \lor \tilde{\chi}_I\).
 This completes the induction step, and it completes the proof of \cref{lem:fo-removal}.
<!-- end proof-9 -->
<!-- corollary-3: body_md -->
\label{cor:iterated-removal}
  Let \(q, d \in \mathbb{N}\), let \(k,s \in \ensuremath{\mathbb{N}_{\scriptscriptstyle \geqslant 1}}\),
  let \(\sigma\) be a signature,
  and let \(r \coloneqq \operatorname{r_1} ({q}, {k}, {d})\).
  For every formula \(\varphi(x) \in \ensuremath{\@empty{FO}}^+(q,k,d)\) of signature \(\sigma\),
  there are sentences \(\tilde{\varphi}_1, \dots, \tilde{\varphi}_s \in \ensuremath{\@empty{FO}}^+(q,k,d)\)
  and a formula
  \(\tilde{\varphi}_0(x) \in \ensuremath{\@empty{FO}}^+(q,k,d)\) of signature \(\tilde{\sigma}^{s}_{r}\)
  such that for every \(\sigma\)-structure \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}\) of order \(\left\lvertA\right\rvert \geqslant s+1\),
  all distinct \(c_1, \dots, c_s \in A\), and all \(a \in A\),
  we have
  \begin{equation}
    \label{eq:removal2}
    \ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \models \varphi(a) \iff
    \begin{cases}
      \ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \lbag_r c_1 \cdots c_s \models \tilde{\varphi}_0(a)
      &\text{if } a \in A \setminus \ensuremath{\{c_1, \dots, c_s\}},\\
      \ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \lbag_r c_1 \cdots c_s \models \tilde{\varphi}_1
      &\text{if } a = c_1,\\
      \mkern64mu \vdots
      &\qquad \vdots\\
      \ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \lbag_r c_1 \cdots c_s \models \tilde{\varphi}_s
      &\text{if } a = c_s.
    \end{cases}
  \end{equation}
  Furthermore, there is an algorithm that computes
  \(\tilde{\varphi}_0(x), \tilde{\varphi}_1, \dots, \tilde{\varphi}_s\)
  from \(\varphi(x)\) and \(s\).
<!-- end corollary-3 -->
<!-- lemma-10: body_md -->
\label{lem:fo-mc}
  Let \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}\) be a nowhere dense class of \(\sigma\)-structures.
  Furthermore, let \(\varphi(x)\) be an \(\ensuremath{\@empty{FO}}^+[\sigma]\) formula.
  For every \(\varepsilon > 0\), there is an algorithm that,
  given \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}\), computes the set \(\varphi(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}})\)
  in time \(\mathcal{O}_{\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}, \varphi, \varepsilon}(\left\lvertA\right\rvert^{1+\varepsilon})\).
<!-- end lemma-10 -->
<!-- proof-10: body_md -->
Let \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_G\) be the class of all Gaifman graphs of structures in \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}\)
  and all their subgraphs.
  Then \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_G\) is nowhere dense.

  We assume without loss of generality that \(\operatorname*{free}(\varphi) = \ensuremath{\{x\}}\);
  in case that \(\operatorname*{free}(\varphi) = \emptyset\),
  we replace \(\varphi\) with the formula \((\varphi \land x{=}x)\).
  Let \(q, k, d \in \mathbb{N}\) such that \(\varphi \in \ensuremath{\@empty{FO}}^+(q,k,d)\),
  and let \(r \coloneqq \operatorname{r_1} ({q}, {k}, {d})\), where $\operatorname{r_1}$ is
  the function defined in \cref{eq:specialRadiusFunction} in
  \cref{sec:rpGNFforFOandFOMOD}.
  We choose \(t \coloneqq t(\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_G,2r)\) according to \cref{lem:splitter-strategy}
  and let \(\ell \coloneqq (2r+1)t^2\) as in \cref{eq:ell-C-r}
  (for \(2r\) instead of \(r\)).
Let \(\varepsilon > 0\).
  Without loss of generality,
  we assume \(\varepsilon \leqslant \frac{1}{2}\)
  (which implies that \(\varepsilon^2 \leqslant \varepsilon/2\)).
  We let
  \begin{equation}
    \label{eq:eps}
    \varepsilon' \coloneqq \frac{\varepsilon}{2t}.
  \end{equation}
  Furthermore, we choose a \(c_1 \in \ensuremath{\mathbb{N}_{\scriptscriptstyle \geqslant 1}}\)
  such that for all graphs \(G \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_G\) of order \(n_G \coloneqq |G| > c_1\),
  the \(r\)-neighbourhood cover of \cref{lem:nb-covers},
  applied to \(G\) and \(\varepsilon'/2\),
  has maximum degree at most \(n_G^{\varepsilon'}\).
  Moreover, we let \(c_2 \coloneqq c_1 + \ell\).

  Let \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}\) be the input structure for our algorithm, and set \(n \coloneqq \left\lvertA\right\rvert\).
  If \(n \leqslant c_1\), then we compute the set \(\varphi(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}})\) by brute force.
  Hence, in the following, suppose that \(n > c_1\),
  and let \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{X}}\) be an \(r\)-neighbourhood cover of \(G_\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}\) of radius at most \(2r\)
  and with maximum degree at most \(n^{\varepsilon'}\)
  computed via \cref{lem:nb-covers}.

  By \cref{cor:GNF_FOplus},
  the \(\ensuremath{\@empty{FO}}^+(q,k,d)\) formula \(\varphi(x)\) is equivalent to a Boolean combination \(\varphi'(x)\)
  of \(r\)-local \(\ensuremath{\@empty{FO}}^+(q,k,d)\) formulas \(\psi(x)\) and basic local sentences of the form
  \begin{equation}
    \label{eq:fo-mc-basic-local}
    \xi \coloneqq \exists y_1\cdots\exists y_\ell\;
    \big(
      \ensuremath{\bigwedge}_{1\leqslant j < j'\leqslant \ell} \operatorname{dist}(y_j,y_{j'})\,{>}\,2r'
      \ \land\ \ensuremath{\bigwedge}_{j\in[\ell]} \lambda(y_j)
    \big),
  \end{equation}
  where \(1 \leqslant \ell \leqslant k+q\),
  \(y_1, \dots, y_\ell\) are \(\ell\) variables,
  \(r', \tilde{r} \in \mathbb{N}\) with \(\tilde{r} \leqslant r' \leqslant \frac{1}{2}r + \operatorname{r_1} ({q-1}, {k+1}, {d})\),
  \(\tilde{r} \leqslant \operatorname{r_1} ({q-1}, {k+1}, {d}) \leqslant r\),
  and \(\lambda(x_1) \in \ensuremath{\@empty{FO}}^+(q-1,k+1,d)\) is \(\tilde{r}\)-local.
  In particular, \(\lambda(x_1)\) is \(r\)-local.

  For every \(r\)-local formula \(\psi(x)\) that appears in \(\varphi'(x)\),
  either directly in the Boolean combination or as part of a basic local sentence,
  we shall compute the set \(\psi(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}})\).
  Then we can evaluate the basic local sentences \(\xi\)
  of the form~\eqref{eq:fo-mc-basic-local} as follows:
  we note that \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}} \models \xi\) if and only if \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}\) has a \(2r'\)-independent set
  \(Y \subseteq \psi(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}})\) of order \(\left\lvertY\right\rvert \geqslant \ell\).
  We can use \cref{lem:dist-is} to decide if this is the case in time \(\mathcal{O}(n^{1+\varepsilon'})\).
  Once we have computed the truth values of all basic local sentences \(\xi\) in \(\varphi'\)
  as well as the sets \(\psi(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}})\) for all local formulas \(\psi(x)\) in \(\varphi'\),
  we can easily evaluate the Boolean combination \(\varphi'\)
  and compute the set \(\varphi'(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}) = \varphi(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}})\).
  Thus, it remains to compute \(\psi(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}})\) for all formulas \(\psi(x)\)
  in a finite set \(\Psi\) of \(r\)-local \(\ensuremath{\@empty{FO}}^+(q,k,d)\) formulas.

  We shall recursively compute the set \(\psi(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}[X])\) for every \(X \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{X}}\)
  and every \(\psi(x) \in \Psi\).
  Observe that, if \(\ensuremath{\ensuremath{N_{r}^{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}}(a)}} \subseteq X\),
  then \(a \in \psi(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}) \iff a\in \psi(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}[X])\),
  because \(\psi(x)\) is \(r\)-local.
  Furthermore, \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{X}}\) is an \(r\)-neighbourhood cover,
  so this implies
  \begin{equation}
    \label{eq:evaluate-root}
    \psi(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}) = \ensuremath{\bigl\{ a \in A \ \mathrel{:} \  a \in \psi\bigl(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}[\ensuremath{\mathcal{X}}(a)]\bigr) \bigr\}}.
  \end{equation}

  Let us move on to the recursive step.
  It will be convenient to think of our recursive algorithm
  in terms of the recursion tree it builds.
  Each node of the recursion tree will be indexed by a tuple \(\bar{X} = (X_1, \dots, X_k)\),
  where \(k \in [0,t]\),
  and the \(X_i\) are subsets of \(A\).
  The root is indexed by the empty tuple \(()\).
  In the following, we will no longer distinguish between the nodes and their indices
  and refer to a tuple \(\bar{X}\) as a node of the tree.

  For each node \(\bar{X}\) that is not a leaf of the tree,
  we define a signature \(\sigma_{\bar{X}}\)
  that will always be of the form \(\tilde{\sigma}^{i}_{r}\)
  for some \(i \geqslant 0\),
  a \(\sigma_{\bar{X}}\)-structure \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}_{\bar{X}}\),
  its Gaifman graph \(G_{\bar{X}}\),
  and \(r\)-neighbourhood cover \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{X}}_{\bar{X}}\) of \(G_{\bar{X}}\)
  of radius at most \(2r\) and maximum degree at most \(\left\lvertG_{\bar{X}}\right\rvert^{\varepsilon'}\),
  a centre map \(c_{\bar{X}}\) for \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{X}}_{\bar{X}}\),
  and a finite set \(\Psi_{\bar{X}}\) of \(r\)-local \(\ensuremath{\@empty{FO}}^+(q,k,d)\) formulas
  with one free variable and of signature \(\sigma_{\bar{X}}\).
  For the root \(()\),
  we let \(\sigma_{()} \coloneqq \sigma = \tilde{\sigma}^{0}_{r}\),
  \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}_{()} \coloneqq \ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}\),
  \(G_{()} \coloneqq G_\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}\),
  \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{X}}_{()} \coloneqq \ensuremath{\mathcal{X}}\),
  and \(\Psi_{()} \coloneqq \Psi\).

  For nodes \(\bar{X} \neq ()\) that are not leaves,
  in addition to the structure \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}_{\bar{X}}\), the graph \(G_{\bar{X}}\),
  the neighbourhood cover \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{X}}_{\bar{X}}\),
  and the set \(\Psi_{\bar{X}}\) of formulas,
  we will define a graph \(H_{\bar{X}}\),
  a vertex \(v_{\bar{X}}\),
  and a set \(W_{\bar{X}}\).
  They will be defined in such a way that,
  for each node \(\bar{X} = (X_1, \dots, X_k)\),
  the vertices \(v^{(i)} \coloneqq v_{(X_1, \dots, X_i)}\),
  sets \(W^{(i)} \coloneqq W_{(X_1, \dots, X_i)}\),
  graphs \(G^{(0)} \coloneqq G_{()}\)
  and \(G^{(i)} \coloneqq G_{(X_1, \dots, X_i)}\),
  \(H^{(i)} \coloneqq H_{(X_1, \dots, X_i)}\) for \(i \in [k]\) satisfy
  the conditions \eqref{eq:splitter-strategy}
  and \eqref{item:splitter-strategy-i}--\eqref{item:splitter-strategy-iii}
  of \cref{lem:splitter-strategy} with \(2r\) instead of \(r\).
  Furthermore, we define a breadth-first search tree \(T_{\bar{X}}\)
  of the graph \(H_{\bar{X}}\) rooted in \(v_{\bar{X}}\).

  For a node \(\bar{X} = (X_1, \dots, X_k)\),
  if \(k \geqslant 1\) and \(\left\lvertX_k\right\rvert \leqslant c_2\), then \(\bar{X}\) is a leaf of the tree.
  If \(k = 0\) or \(\left\lvertX_k\right\rvert > c_2\),
  then the node \(\bar{X}\) has a child \(\bar{X} Y \coloneqq (X_1, \dots, X_k, Y)\)
  for every \(Y \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{X}}_{\bar{X}}\).

  Let \(\bar{X} = (X_1, \dots, X_k)\) and \(\bar{Y} = (X_1, \dots, X_k, Y)\)
  for some \(Y \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{X}}_{\bar{X}}\).
  Our goal at the node \(\bar{X}\) is to compute \(\psi(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}_{\bar{X}}[Y])\)
  for every \(\psi(x) \in \Psi_{\bar{X}}\).
  From these sets \(\psi(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}_{\bar{X}}[Y])\), we can compute
  \begin{equation}
    \label{eq:eval-from-children}
  \psi(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}_{\bar{X}}) = \ensuremath{\bigl\{ a \in A_{\bar{X}} \ \mathrel{:} \  a \in \psi\bigl(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}_{\bar{X}}[\ensuremath{\mathcal{X}}_{\bar{X}}(a)]\big) \bigr\}},
  \end{equation}
  exploiting that \(\psi(x)\) is \(r\)-local
  and that \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{X}}_{\bar{X}}\) is an \(r\)-neighbourhood cover
  of the Gaifman graph \(G_{\bar{X}}\) of \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}_{\bar{X}}\).
  For the root \(\bar{X} = ()\) of the recursion tree,
  this enables us to compute \(\varphi(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}})\), as argued above.

  Consider a node \(\bar{Y} = \bar{X} Y = (X_1, \dots, X_k, Y)\) for some \(k \in \mathbb{N}\).
  If \(\left\lvertY\right\rvert \leqslant c_2\), then \(\bar{Y}\) is a leaf,
  and we compute \(\psi(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}_{\bar{X}}[Y])\) for every \(\psi(x) \in \Psi_{\bar{X}}\) by brute force.
  Now suppose that \(\left\lvertY\right\rvert > c_2\).
  For \(i \in [0,k]\), let \(\bar{X}^{(i)} \coloneqq (X_1, \dots, X_i)\)
  and \(G^{(i)} \coloneqq G_{\bar{X}^{(i)}}\).
  Furthermore, if \(i \geqslant 1\),
  let \(v^{(i)} \coloneqq v_{\bar{X}^{(i)}}\),
  \(W^{(i)} \coloneqq W_{\bar{X}^{(i)}}\),
  and \(H^{(i)} \coloneqq H_{\bar{X}^{(i)}}\).
  Observe that \(Y\) is a cluster in the neighbourhood cover \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{X}}_{\bar{X}^{(k)}}\) of \(G^{(k)}\),
  and hence, \(Y \subseteq V(G^{(k)})\).
  We let
  \(v_{\bar{Y}} \coloneqq v^{(k+1)} \coloneqq c_{\bar{X}^{(k)}}(Y)\)
  and \(H_{\bar{Y}} \coloneqq H^{(k+1)} \coloneqq G^{(k)}[Y]\).
  We choose \(W_{\bar{Y}} \coloneqq W^{(k+1)} \subseteq Y\)
  according to \cref{lem:splitter-strategy}\eqref{item:splitter-strategy-ii}
  with \(2r\) instead of \(r\),
  and we let
  \(G_{\bar{Y}} \coloneqq G^{(k+1)} \coloneqq H^{(k+1)}
  \bigl[\ensuremath{N_{2r}^{H^{(k+1)}}(v^{(k+1)})} \setminus W^{(k+1)}\bigr]\)
  (according to \cref{lem:splitter-strategy}\eqref{item:splitter-strategy-iii}).
  Note that, actually, we have
  \(G^{(k+1)} = H^{(k+1)}[Y \setminus W^{(k+1)}]\),
  because the radius of the neighbourhood cover \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{X}}_{\bar{X}^{(k)}}\) is at most \(2r\)
  and \(v^{(k+1)} = c_{\bar{X}^{(k)}}(Y)\),
  so \(Y \subseteq \ensuremath{N_{2r}^{G^{(k)}[Y]}(v^{(k+1)})}\).

  If \(W^{(k+1)} = \emptyset\), then we proceed as follows.
  We let \(\sigma_{\bar{Y}} \coloneqq \sigma_{\bar{X}}\)
  and \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}_{\bar{Y}} \coloneqq \ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}_{\bar{X}}[Y]\).
  We let \(G_{\bar{Y}} \coloneqq G_{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}_{\bar{Y}}}\)
  and compute an \(r\)-neighbourhood cover \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{X}}_{\bar{Y}}\) of \(G_{\bar{Y}}\)
  of radius at most \(2r\) and maximum degree at most \(\left\lvertY\right\rvert^{\varepsilon'}\)
  together with a centre map \(c_{\bar{Y}}\) using \cref{lem:nb-covers}.
  Moreover, we let \(\Psi_{\bar{Y}} \coloneqq \Psi_{\bar{X}}\).
  The children of \(\bar{Y}\) are \(\bar{Y} Z\) for \(Z \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{X}}_{\bar{Y}}\),
  and, for all \(\psi(x) \in \Psi_{\bar{Y}} = \Psi_{\bar{X}}\),
  we can compute \(\psi(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}_{\bar{Y}}) = \psi(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}_{\bar{X}}[Y])\)
  from the recursively computed sets \(\psi(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}_{\bar{Y}}[Z])\)
  for \(Z \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{X}}_{\bar{Y}}\) as in \cref{eq:eval-from-children}.

  Now suppose that \(W^{(k+1)} = \ensuremath{\{w_1, \dots, w_s\}} \neq \emptyset\)
  for pairwise distinct \(w_1, \dots, w_s\).
  In order to define \(\sigma_{\bar{Y}}\), suppose that \(\sigma_{\bar{X}} = \tilde{\sigma}^{i}_{r}\).
  Then we let \(\sigma_{\bar{Y}} \coloneqq \tilde{\sigma}^{i+s}_{r}\)
  and \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}_{\bar{Y}} \coloneqq \ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}_{\bar{X}}[Y] \lbag_r w_1 \cdots w_s\).
  Set \(G_{\bar{Y}} \coloneqq G_{\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}_{\bar{Y}}}\),
  and note that \(V(G_{\bar{Y}}) = Y \setminus W^{(k+1)}\).
  Moreover, since \(\left\lvertY\right\rvert > c_2 = c_1 + \ell\) and \(s = \left\lvertW^{(k+1)}\right\rvert \leqslant \ell\),
  we have \(\left\lvertG_{\bar{Y}}\right\rvert > c_1\).
  We compute an \(r\)-neighbourhood cover \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{X}}_{\bar{Y}}\) of \(G_{\bar{Y}}\)
  of radius at most \(2r\) and maximum degree at most
  \(\left\lvertY \setminus W^{(k+1)}\right\rvert^{\varepsilon'}\)
  together with a centre map \(c_{\bar{Y}}\) using \cref{lem:nb-covers}.
  The children of \(\bar{Y}\) are \(\bar{Y} Z\) for \(Z \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{X}}_{\bar{Y}}\).
For each \(\psi(x) \in \Psi_{\bar{X}} \subseteq \ensuremath{\@empty{FO}}^+(q,k,d)\),
  we compute sentences \(\tilde{\psi}_1, \dots, \tilde{\psi}_s \in \ensuremath{\@empty{FO}}^+(q,k,d)\)
  and a formula \(\tilde{\psi}_0(x) \in \ensuremath{\@empty{FO}}^+(q,k,d)\) by \cref{cor:iterated-removal}.
  The corollary shows that we can compute \(\psi(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}_{\bar{X}}[Y])\)
  from the truth values of the sentences \(\tilde{\psi}_i\) in \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}_{\bar{Y}}\)
  and the set \(\psi_0(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}_{\bar{Y}})\).
  We apply \cref{cor:GNF_FOplus} to each of the \(\tilde{\psi}_i\)
  and obtain a formula \(\tilde{\psi}'_i\) in Gaifman normal form.
  As explained above for \(\varphi(x)\),
  from this formula in Gaifman normal form,
  we obtain a set \(\Psi_i\) of \(r\)-local formulas with one free variable in \(\ensuremath{\@empty{FO}}^+(q,k,d)\)
  such that we can reduce the evaluation of \(\tilde{\psi}_i\) in \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}_{\bar{Y}}\)
  to the evaluation of all formulas in \(\Psi_i\) in \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}_{\bar{Y}}\).
  We let \(\Psi_\psi \coloneqq \bigcup_{i=0}^s \Psi_i\).
  Then we can reduce the evaluation of \(\psi\) in \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}_{\bar{X}}[Y]\)
  to the evaluation of all formulas in \(\Psi_\psi\) in \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}_{\bar{Y}}\).
  Finally, we let \(\Psi_{\bar{Y}} \coloneqq \bigcup_{\psi \in \Psi_{\bar{X}}} \Psi_\psi\).
  Then we can compute \(\psi(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}_{\bar{X}}[Y])\) for all \(\psi \in \Psi_{\bar{X}}\)
  from the recursively computed \(\chi(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}_{\bar{Y}}[Z])\) for \(Z \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{X}}_{\bar{Y}}\)
  and \(\chi(x) \in \Psi_{\bar{Y}}\).

  This completes the description of the recursive algorithm.
  It remains to analyse the algorithm.
  We first observe that the depth of the recursion is at most \(t\);
  this follows from \cref{lem:splitter-strategy}.
  Moreover, for each node \(\bar{X} = (X_1, \dots, X_k)\),
  the sizes of the sets \(W_{\bar{X}_i}\) for \(\bar{X}_i \coloneqq (X_1, \dots, X_i)\)
  add up to at most \(\ell\) by \cref{eq:splitter2}
  (for \(2r\) instead of \(r\)) and our choice of \(\ell\).
  Whenever we remove the elements of a non-empty set \(W_{\bar{X}_i}\) of size \(s\),
  we extend the signature from \(\sigma_{\bar{X}_{i-1}} = \tilde{\sigma}^{j}_{r}\)
  to \(\sigma_{\bar{X}_{i}} = \tilde{\sigma}^{j+s}_{r}\).
  Thus, \(\sigma_{\bar{X}} = \tilde{\sigma}^{j'}_{r}\) for \(j' = \sum_{i=1}^k \left\lvertW_{\bar{X}_i}\right\rvert\).
  As \(j' \leqslant \ell\),
  it follows that \(\sigma_{\bar{X}} \subseteq \tilde{\sigma}^{\ell}_{r} \eqqcolon \sigma^*\).
  Note that the size of \(\sigma^*\) only depends on the input formula \(\varphi(x)\)
  (via \(\sigma\) and \(r\))
  and the class \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}\)
  (via \(t = t(\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}_G, 2r)\)),
  but not on the size of the structure $\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}$.

  Thus, all formulas \(\psi(x) \in \Psi_{\bar{X}}\) are of a signature
  that is contained in \(\sigma^*\).
  Furthermore, by the construction of \(\Psi_{\bar{X}}\),
  all formulas in \(\Psi_{\bar{X}}\) are contained in a finite set of \(\ensuremath{\@empty{FO}}^+(q,k,d)\) formulas
  that only depends on \(\varphi(x)\) and \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}\),
  but not on the size of the structure $\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}$.
  Thus, the number and size of the formulas we need to evaluate at each node
  only contributes a constant factor to the running time.

  All structures \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}_{\bar{X}}\) have signature contained in \(\sigma^*\),
  which means that we can bound their size \(\left\lVert\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}_{\bar{X}}\right\rVert\)
  and the size of their Gaifman graph \(G_{\bar{X}}\)
  by \(\mathcal{O}\bigl(\left\lvert\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}_{\bar{X}}\right\rvert^{1+\varepsilon'}\bigr)\).
  Also note that for \(\bar{X} = (X_1, \dots, X_k)\),
  we have \(\left\lvert\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}_{\bar{X}}\right\rvert \leqslant \left\lvertX_k\right\rvert \eqqcolon n_{\bar{X}}\).
  At each non-leaf node,
  we have to compute the neighbourhood cover \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{X}}_{\bar{X}}\) and centre map \(c_{\bar{X}}\),
  which can be done in time \(\mathcal{O}\bigl(n_{\bar{X}}^{1+\varepsilon'}\bigr)\) by \cref{lem:nb-covers}.
  Furthermore, we compute the breadth-first search tree \(T_{\bar{X}}\) of \(H_{\bar{X}}\),
  which can also be done in time
  \(\mathcal{O}(\left\lVertH_{\bar{X}}\right\rVert) \subseteq \mathcal{O}\bigl(n_{\bar{X}}^{1+\varepsilon'}\bigr)\).
  By using the trees \(T_{\bar{X}_i}\) of the ancestors \(\bar{X}_i\) of \(\bar{X}\) in the tree,
  we can compute \(W_{\bar{X}}\) in constant time (cf.~\cref{rem:compute-splitter-strategy}).

  Based on this,
  we can compute \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}_{\bar{X}}\) in time
  \(\mathcal{O}(\left\lVert\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}_{\bar{X}}\right\rVert) \subseteq \mathcal{O}\bigl(n_{\bar{X}}^{1+\varepsilon'}\bigr)\).
  Finally, assuming \(\bar{X} = \bar{X}' X_k\),
  we have to compute \(\psi(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}_{\bar{X}'}[X_k])\) for each \(\psi(x) \in \Psi_{\bar{X}'}\)
  from the recursively computed \(\chi(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}_{\bar{X}}[Y])\) for \(Y \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{X}}_{\bar{X}}\)
  and \(\chi(x) \in \Psi_{\bar{X}}\).
  Combining the sets \(\chi(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}_{\bar{X}}[Y])\) to \(\chi(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}_{\bar{X}})\) requires time
  \(\mathcal{O}\bigl(\sum_{Y \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{X}}_{\bar{X}}} \left\lvertY\right\rvert\bigr)
  \subseteq \mathcal{O}\bigl(n_{\bar{X}}^{1+\varepsilon'}\bigr)\) by \cref{eq:Delta},
  since the maximum degree of \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{X}}_{\bar{X}}\) is at most \(n_{\bar{X}}^{\varepsilon'}\).
  Computing \(\psi(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}_{\bar{X}'}[X_k])\) for all \(\psi(x) \in \Psi_{\bar{X}'}\)
  from the sets \(\chi(\ensuremath{\mathcal{A}}_{\bar{X}})\) for \(\chi(x) \in \Psi_{\bar{X}}\)
  involves a large constant factor to handle all formulas
  and may involve computing \(2r'\)-independent sets
  using \cref{lem:dist-is} to evaluate basic local sentences,
  but it is possible in time \(\mathcal{O}\bigl(n_{\bar{X}}^{1+\varepsilon'}\bigr)\).

  Overall, the running time at each non-leaf node \(\bar{X}\) is in
  \(\mathcal{O}\bigl(n_{\bar{X}}^{1+\varepsilon'}\bigr)\)
  (not including the time for the recursive calls).
  The running time at a leaf node is constant.

  We obtain the following recurrence for the running time \(T(j,m)\)
  the algorithm spends in the subtree rooted at a node
  \(\bar{X} = (X_1, \dots, X_{t-j})\) with \(n_{\bar{X}} = m\):
  \begin{align*}
    T(0,m)
    &\in \mathcal{O}(1)
    &&\text{for all } m,\\
    T(j,m)
    &\in \mathcal{O}(1)
    &&\text{for all } j \in [t] \text{ and all } m \leqslant c_2,\\
    T(j,m)
    &\leqslant \sum_Y T(j-1, m_Y) + \mathcal{O}(m^{1+\varepsilon'})
    &&\text{for all } j \in [t] \text{ and all } m > c_2.
  \end{align*}
  The sum ranges over all clusters \(Y\) in the neighbourhood cover \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{X}}_{\bar{X}}\),
  and \(m_Y \coloneqq n_{\bar{X} Y}\).
  Recall that \(\sum_Y m_Y \leqslant m^{1+\varepsilon'}\) by \cref{eq:Delta},
  using that the maximum degree of \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{X}}_{\bar{X}}\) is at most \(m^{\varepsilon'}\).
  Note that the clause \(T(0,m) \in \mathcal{O}(1)\) is justified by the fact
  that the height of the tree is at most \(t\).
  Therefore, all nodes of the form \((X_1, \dots, X_t)\) must be leaves.

  The same recurrence was obtained in \cite{GroheKreutzerSiebertz_2017_NowhereDense}.
  The straightforward analysis,
  carried out in \cite{GroheKreutzerSiebertz_2017_NowhereDense}
  (and using, among other things, the inequality
  \(\sum_i y_i^p \leqslant (\sum_i y_i)^p\)
  for \(y_i \geqslant 0\) and \(p \geqslant 1\),
  based on the fact that the \(\ell_p\) norm of a vector \((y_i)_i\)
  is bounded from above by its \(\ell_1\) norm),
  yields \(T(j,n) \in \mathcal{O}(n^{1 + 2j \varepsilon'}) \subseteq \mathcal{O}(n^{1+\varepsilon})\)
  for all \(j \in [0,t]\).
  In particular, this bounds the running time at the root
  to \(\mathcal{O}(n^{1+\varepsilon})\)
  and completes the proof of \cref{lem:fo-mc}.
<!-- end proof-10 -->
